**Read this before anything else.** Until 2026-09-04 this file said "144 rows, 142 ✅, only
`WEBGPU-1` and `WEBGPU-107` open — every WebGPU task that could be completed and verified in this
environment is done". That sentence was true about *this file's own task list* and false about the
renderer. The list had simply never asked about whole feature families that the reference renderer
(`EasyGL`) implements. The **EasyGL parity audit of 2026-09-04** (next section) re-derived the live
state from the current source and found **44 concrete gaps**, now `WEBGPU-153`–`WEBGPU-196` in
Phase 70.

The old rows are **not** rewritten. Each of them delivered and verified what it said it would; the
audit did not disprove a single row's own acceptance criteria. (Five of the audit's *own* new rows
were later rewritten by the second pass — `161`, `163`, `165`, `166`, `180` — each keeping its ID and
carrying the reason in its Notes.) What it disproved is the *inference*
that a green row table means renderer parity. So the completion condition changed:

> **WebGPU may be called EasyGL-equivalent for this phase only when every in-scope EasyGL
> capability/behaviour has either (a) an equivalent WebGPU implementation plus meaningful
> verification, or (b) a documented fundamental WebGPU/platform limitation with truthful public
> capability reporting and no silent behavioural substitution.**
>
> "Every old `WEBGPU-*` row is green" is **not** a completion condition.

## EasyGL parity audit — 2026-09-04

### What "parity with EasyGL" means here

`EasyGL` (`modules/renderers/easygl/`, 11 780 lines of renderer + 245 example/test programs) is the
reference renderer for this workstream. The target is:

> For the **normal CNA/XNA graphics surface** that EasyGL supports, WebGPU should be a genuinely
> equivalent renderer — same observable behaviour, same truthful capability answers, comparable
> verification — not merely a renderer whose own plan happens to be fully checked off.

Where EasyGL itself looks wrong against XNA/FNA, this plan **records the divergence** instead of
cloning it; two such cases are listed under *EasyGL defects found while auditing* below.

### Out of scope: the modern CNAEXT graphics workstream

This phase does **not** add WebGPU support for anything whose *public* API belongs to
`plans/plan_modern.md` / `misc/CNAEXT.md`. Deliberately excluded, with the reason:

| Excluded | Why |
|---|---|
| Compute shaders, storage buffers, image load/store, memory barriers, GPU timers, indirect draw | `plan_modern.md` authority (`MOD-1500`, `MOD-2090`); WebGPU reports `false` through `SupportsComputeShadersEXT()`/`SupportsIndirectDrawEXT()`/`SupportsGpuTimerEXT()` at the `GraphicsDevice` seam, which is truthful |
| ~~Float / half-float render targets~~ | **Withdrawn 2026-09-04 (second pass) — this exclusion was wrong.** `Single`, `Vector2`, `Vector4`, `HalfSingle`, `HalfVector2`, `HalfVector4` and `HdrBlendable` carry no `EXT` suffix in `SurfaceFormat.hpp`: they are ordinary XNA 4.0 enumerators reached from the ordinary `RenderTarget2D` constructor. That `plan_modern.md`'s HDR pipeline is one *consumer* of them does not make the format contract CNAEXT — by that reasoning `SurfaceFormat::Color` would be CNAEXT too. `Rgba64` was not even listed. EasyGL implements all eight behind a runtime probe. Now in scope: `WEBGPU-197`–`WEBGPU-202`. What stays excluded is the HDR *pipeline* — tonemap, bloom, exposure, the `CNA::Graphics` passes — not the format |
| The `CNA::Graphics` HDR pipeline itself (tonemap, bloom, exposure, auto-exposure, the pass graph) | `plan_modern.md` `MOD-100`/`MOD-101`/`MOD-104` own the passes. `WEBGPU-197`–`202` give them a float target to render into; they do not implement any pass |
| Shadow sampling, image-based lighting, cascades, punctual shadows | `plan_modern.md`; `GpuDrawParams` carries them and a renderer with no variant is documented as accepting-and-ignoring |
| `CNA::Graphics::*` passes (bloom, SSAO, tonemap, decals, particles, …) and their GLSL ES shaders — 33 of EasyGL's 245 example programs | `plan_modern.md` / `NEXT_modern.md` |
| `ShaderEffect`'s **cube and volume** sampler bindings (`IEffectRenderer::BindTextureCube`/`BindTexture3D`, unimplemented on WebGPU, implemented on EasyGL) | `ShaderEffect` is marked `CNAEXT` in its own header. WebGPU's existing `ShaderEffect` support (`WEBGPU-76`/`142`) must not regress, but *extending* it is CNAEXT work. **Recorded here so it is not lost**, not scheduled |
| A new shader IR / "shader Rosetta stone", glTF modernization | Out of this file's remit entirely |

The boundary was drawn by **tracing the public call**, never by an `EXT` suffix. Two examples of
things that ARE in scope despite `EXT`-suffixed internals: `ClassifyRenderTargetFormatEXT` /
`CreateRenderTarget2DEXT` (reached from the plain `RenderTarget2D` constructor) and
`IsCompressedCubeTransferFormatEXT` (reached from `TextureCube::SetData`).

### How this audit was done, and what it could not verify

Done: full read of `IGraphicsRenderer.hpp` (3 161 lines) with a mechanical override diff of all 200
virtuals across both renderers; `WebGPURenderer.{hpp,cpp}` (14 097 lines) vs `EasyGLRenderer.{hpp,cpp}`
(13 599 lines); `GraphicsDevice`/`Texture2D`/`TextureCube`/`Texture3D`/`RenderTarget2D`/`Effect`/
`SpriteBatch` as the public seam; the shared `VertexDeclarationFidelity.hpp` contract; the capability
enum and the `GraphicsDevice::SupportsCapability` seam; a family-by-family classification of all 245
EasyGL example programs against the 53 WebGPU ones; `plans/plan_fx.md` §10.2/§10.3, `plans/plan_graphics.md`,
`plans/plan_modern.md` (boundary only), `docs/webgpu-renderer.md`, `docs/webgpu-vs-vulkan-deviations.md`,
`docs/sampler-state-support.md`.

**Not done, and therefore not claimed:** no build and no test run. This worktree has no
`cmake-build-webgpu/` and configuring one would be a full from-scratch build, which the run was not
authorized to spend and which `CLAUDE.md`'s SSD rules discourage for a planning pass. The machine
*does* have a usable adapter (`/dev/dri/renderD128`, AMD RADV, Vulkan 1.4) so the suite is runnable —
it simply was not run here. **Every finding below is source-derived.** No row is marked ✅ on the
strength of this audit, and no existing ✅ was flipped on source inspection alone.

### Old conclusions this audit disproves

1. **"`FillMode::WireFrame` is the single entry this renderer genuinely lacks … a wireframe request
   cannot reach any native pipeline state"** (`WebGPURenderer.cpp` `SupportsCapability`,
   `docs/webgpu-vs-vulkan-deviations.md`). EasyGL renders a correct wireframe **without any
   polygon-mode API**, by re-expanding triangle indices into a `GL_LINES` draw
   (`EasyGLRenderer::DrawWireframe`, REMED-GFX-219, shared oracle: interior 0/1089, all three edges
   present). WebGPU can express exactly that (`WGPUPrimitiveTopology_LineList` + a 32-bit index
   buffer). The refusal contract `WEBGPU-115` delivered is intact and honest; its **premise is
   wrong**. → `WEBGPU-153`/`154`.
2. **"WebGPU's stock 3D dispatch is at parity with EasyGL."** It is not: WebGPU selects its stock
   pipeline *from the vertex stride alone* and refuses any declaration that does not match that
   stride's canonical packed layout (`RequireFaithfulVertexDeclaration`, `UnlistedStrideLayout::
   RendererRefusesIt`). **EasyGL is the only renderer in the repo that binds stock-program inputs by
   declaration *semantic*** (`RequireDeclarationMatchesStockProgram` +
   `ConfigureDeclarationForStockProgramEXT`), and it carries three stock program shapes WebGPU has no
   shader for at all. Concretely, these render on EasyGL and throw on WebGPU:
   `BasicEffect` + lighting on a `VertexPositionNormal` mesh (stride 24, SAMPLE-002); a stride-36
   Position+Normal+Colour+UV mesh, which is what the **stock XNA `ModelProcessor` emits** for a mesh
   with a colour channel (FX-125); a stride-32 Position+Colour vertex with no normal
   (REMED-GFX-234); and `DualTextureEffect` with an independent `TEXCOORD1` set (SAMPLE-073).
   → `WEBGPU-155`–`159`.
3. **"`SamplerState` is fully mapped."** `ApplySamplerMipState` and `ApplySamplerAddressW` are
   **not overridden** by WebGPU, so `SamplerState.AddressW`, `MaxMipLevel` and
   `MipMapLevelOfDetailBias` are silently discarded. EasyGL implements the first two on every
   non-ES2 profile and documents the third's limits. `WGPUSamplerDescriptor` has `addressModeW` and
   `lodMinClamp`; only LOD bias is genuinely absent from WebGPU. → `WEBGPU-160`/`161`.
4. **"Presentation/virtual resolution is done."** WebGPU implements `ComputeLogicalViewport()`
   (Letterbox/Overscan/Stretch/FixedHeightDynamicWidth) but does **not** override
   `GetDefaultViewportRect()`, so `GraphicsDevice::UpdateViewportFromWindow()` pushes the **logical**
   rectangle to `SetViewport()` as if it were physical. EasyGL had to override exactly this
   (`EasyGLSurfaceState::GetDefaultViewportRect`) for a real bug report — a resized window rendered
   the game into a corner. → `WEBGPU-162`.
5. **"`WEBGPU-53` render targets ✅."** The row is honest (it says mip regeneration was deferred),
   but `docs/webgpu-renderer.md`'s *Important limitations* list has it **backwards**: it lists
   `RenderTargetCube` `mipMap=true` as throwing and per-face cube MSAA as ignored — both were
   implemented by `WEBGPU-114` — while omitting the one that really does throw,
   `CreateRenderTarget2D(mipMap=true)`. → `WEBGPU-164`, and `WEBGPU-194` for the drift.
6. **"Every `SupportsCapability` member reports truthfully"** (`WEBGPU-135`). True at the time and
   still true *as reporting*, but two `false`s are parity gaps rather than platform limits
   (`WireFrame`, `MultiStreamVertexInput`) and one is a **resource-kind confusion**:
   `ClassifySurfaceFormatEXT` answers `Supported` for BC formats without knowing which resource is
   asking, so `TextureCube(device, size, mipMap, SurfaceFormat::Dxt1)` **constructs successfully** on
   WebGPU and then throws from every `SetData` (`IsCompressedCubeTransferFormatEXT` is not
   overridden; `WebGPUTextureCubeRenderer` is RGBA8-only). EasyGL implements the compressed cube
   path. → `WEBGPU-163`.
7. **"Compiled XNA Effects are out of scope for WebGPU"** (`plan_fx.md` §10.3: *"`WEBGPU` consumes
   WGSL, which the pinned MojoShader does not emit; it is reconsidered only if a SPIR-V route into
   wgpu-native is proven"*). **The pinned wgpu-native v29.0.1.1 exposes that route**:
   `WGPUShaderSourceSPIRV` / `WGPUSType_ShaderSourceSPIRV` /
   `WGPUInstanceFeatureName_ShaderSourceSPIRV` / `wgpuDeviceCreateShaderModuleSpirV`
   (`include/webgpu/webgpu.h` 2799-2819, `include/webgpu/wgpu.h` 1263-1490), and MojoShader's pin
   enables the `SPIRV` profile unconditionally. The condition `plan_fx.md` set has therefore been
   met on the *native* side; the browser side has not (WebGPU in a browser accepts WGSL only). This
   is a real, large XNA compatibility gap, not a permanent exclusion. → `WEBGPU-166`–`171`.
8. **"The test corpus is adequate."** 245 EasyGL example programs vs 53 WebGPU ones. After removing
   the 71 that are CNAEXT/PBR/glTF/model or `ShaderEffect` work, **~174 in-scope EasyGL behaviour
   programs face ~45 WebGPU ones**, and the shortfall is not evenly spread — see the matrix.

### Corrections to the parity audit — 2026-09-04, second planning pass

The audit above is kept verbatim; nothing in it was deleted. This section records where a **later
read of the same sources contradicts it**, and it wins wherever the two disagree. Every correction
has the same shape: the first pass accepted "the native API has no such field / the current renderer
has no such machinery" as proof of a *platform* limitation, which is exactly the reasoning it had
just disproved for wireframe. Source-derived, same as the audit; still no build and no test run.

**C1. The float and `Rgba64` render-target formats are in scope, and were excluded on a bad
argument.** The *Out of scope* table excluded `SurfaceFormat::Single`/`Vector2`/`Vector4`/
`HalfSingle`/`HalfVector2`/`HalfVector4`/`HdrBlendable` as render targets "because the feature exists
to carry `plan_modern.md`'s HDR pipeline". Scope is decided from the public API, and
`SurfaceFormat.hpp` settles it: none of the seven carries an `EXT` suffix, all seven are XNA 4.0
enumerators, and they are reached from the plain `RenderTarget2D(device, w, h, mipMap, format, …)`
constructor. Who *consumes* a format cannot decide whether the format contract is XNA. `Rgba64` was
not mentioned by the audit at all, in the exclusion table or in the matrix.

What EasyGL actually does (`EasyGLRenderer.cpp`, `MapRenderTargetColorFormat` /
`ClassifyRenderTargetFormatEXT` / `ProbeFloatRenderTargetSupportEXT`): it maps **eight** XNA formats
to real GL colour storage — `Color`→RGBA8, `Rgba64`→`GL_RGBA16` (desktop core only), `Single`→R32F,
`Vector2`→RG32F, `Vector4`→RGBA32F, `HalfSingle`→R16F, `HalfVector2`→RG16F, and
`HalfVector4`/`HdrBlendable`→RGBA16F — then **probes the live context** rather than encoding a
support matrix: it creates a 1×1 attachment of the real format and asks whether the framebuffer is
complete, caching the answer. Formats it cannot map return `Defer`; a mapped float format the
context cannot render returns `Unsupported`, and `CreateRenderTarget2DEXT` throws by name rather
than substituting `Color` (`MOD-115`: refuse, never substitute).

What WebGPU does: `WebGPURenderer` **does not override `ClassifyRenderTargetFormatEXT` at all**, so
every format defers to `Texture::ValidateFormat`, which accepts `Color` alone. A
`RenderTarget2D(device, 64, 64, false, SurfaceFormat::HdrBlendable, …)` therefore throws
`std::runtime_error` from the shared layer, and `GraphicsDevice::SupportsCapability(FloatRenderTargets
/ HalfFloatRenderTargets)` reports false through `SupportsSurfaceFormatAsRenderTargetEXT`. Truthful —
and a parity gap, not a platform limit, because the pinned wgpu-native header carries every format
the mapping needs: `WGPUTextureFormat_R32Float`/`RG32Float`/`RGBA32Float` (webgpu.h 1039/1058/1066),
`R16Float`/`RG16Float`/`RGBA16Float` (1034/1046/1065), and `RGBA16Unorm` (1061). The half-float trio
is renderable, filterable and blendable in core WebGPU; the 32-bit trio is renderable in core, with
`WGPUFeatureName_Float32Filterable` (0x0E) and `WGPUFeatureName_Float32Blendable` (0x0F) governing
sampling and blending; `RGBA16Unorm` needs `WGPUNativeFeature_TextureFormat16bitNorm` (wgpu.h
0x0003000B, native-only, listed for Vulkan/DX12/Metal) or, in a browser, the newer
`WGPUFeatureName_TextureFormatsTier2` (0x14). None of that is a matrix to hard-code: EasyGL's own
answer is *probe the device*, and `WebGPURenderer::Supports4xMsaa()` already shows this renderer
probing a scratch texture inside a `WGPUErrorFilter_Validation` scope. → `WEBGPU-197`–`WEBGPU-202`.

The one genuinely hard part is not the format: it is that **a WebGPU render pipeline bakes its colour
target format and sample count**, and all twelve of this renderer's pipeline families bake the
swap-chain format and one renderer-global `sampleCount_`. That single root is shared with C3 below,
which is why `WEBGPU-197` exists and both families depend on it.

**C2. A negative native SPIR-V spike does not prove compiled Effects impossible on WebGPU.**
`WEBGPU-166` as written let one failed experiment close `167`–`171` as "a documented fundamental
limitation". It cannot: the experiment tests exactly one route — XNA/D3D9 bytecode → MojoShader's
`SPIRV` profile → `wgpuDeviceCreateShaderModuleSpirV`. Its failure would disprove *that route*, not
the proposition "WebGPU can run compiled XNA Effects". A shader translation problem is engineering
work until someone names the thing that cannot be translated.

The browser case makes this concrete rather than theoretical. Browser WebGPU accepts **WGSL only**
(`WGPUShaderSourceWGSL`); the SPIR-V entry points the audit found — `WGPUShaderSourceSPIRV` /
`WGPUSType_ShaderSourceSPIRV` / `WGPUInstanceFeatureName_ShaderSourceSPIRV` /
`wgpuDeviceCreateShaderModuleSpirV` — are wgpu-native's, all confirmed present in the pin. So even a
**successful** native spike leaves the browser needing a translation step, and the plan must own that
either way. The alternate architecture to evaluate is a pipeline, not a library:

> XNA/D3D9 shader bytecode → MojoShader intermediate output → SPIR-V (or another suitable
> intermediate) → WGSL → WebGPU

with a MojoShader profile that emits WGSL directly as the other candidate shape (MojoShader's
profiles are pluggable; `profiles/mojoshader_profile_spirv.h` is the precedent for adding one).
**No library is prescribed here, deliberately** — the candidates are named as things to measure when
`WEBGPU-203` runs, against what this repository can actually build. What the tree already provides:
MojoShader is pinned with FNA3D (`cmake/ThirdPartyFNA3D.cmake`, `cna_configure_mojoshader()`) with
the `SPIRV` profile enabled, five committed patches against it, and `cna_mojoshader_vulkan_probe`
(`plan_fx.md` `FX-064`) — a working precedent for a CNA-authored nine-function
`MOJOSHADER_effectShaderContext` backend written straight onto the SPIR-V profile, which is what
`CNA_VULKAN_COMPILED_EFFECTS` ships. `SupportsCompiledEffects()` may stay permanently false only when
a row names the blocker; "no implementation yet" is not one. → `WEBGPU-166` rewritten,
`WEBGPU-203`/`204`.

**C3. Per-target `multiSampleCount` is publicly observable, so "no pixel difference" cannot close
it.** `WEBGPU-165` allowed a differential fixture to end in "documented equivalence". It cannot,
because the divergence is readable without rendering anything: `RenderTarget2D`'s constructor
overwrites `multiSampleCount_` with `rtRenderer_->GetMultiSampleCount()` (`RenderTarget2D.cpp` 114),
WebGPU's render targets set `appliedMultiSampleCount_ = owner_->sampleCount_` unconditionally
(`WebGPURenderer.cpp` 1823/2307), and `RenderTarget2D::getMultiSampleCountProperty()` returns it. On a
device where `PickSampleCount` chose 4, `RenderTarget2D(device, w, h, false, Color, None, 0)` — an
explicit request for **no** multisampling — reports `MultiSampleCount == 4`. EasyGL honours the
per-instance request, clamping to `GL_MAX_SAMPLES` and reporting the clamped value
(`EasyGLRenderTargetRenderer` 3284-3289; `easygl_rendertarget2d_msaa_test`,
`easygl_rendertarget2d_properties_test`). WebGPU can express per-target sample counts through
target-compatible pipeline variants — that is what `WEBGPU-197`'s pipeline key is for — so this is an
implementation gap. Measurement still decides the *clamping rule*, which counts the adapter offers
(`Supports4xMsaa()` probes only 4), and the resolve behaviour; it does not decide whether the
substitution is acceptable. → `WEBGPU-165` rewritten, depends on `WEBGPU-197`.

**C4. `MipMapLevelOfDetailBias` is emulable in the shader, so "no `lodBias` field" is not the
answer.** `WGPUSamplerDescriptor` indeed has no `lodBias` (it carries `addressModeU/V/W`,
`magFilter`, `minFilter`, `mipmapFilter`, `lodMinClamp`, `lodMaxClamp`, `compare`, `maxAnisotropy`
— confirmed field-by-field in the pin). That is the same shape of argument the audit correctly
rejected for `FillMode::WireFrame`: absent *sampler state* is not absent *capability*. WGSL's
`textureSampleBias(t, s, coords, bias)` applies exactly XNA's semantic — a bias added to the computed
level of detail — and is available in the fragment stage, which is where every one of this renderer's
sampling calls already sits (`webgpu_shaders.hpp`: `textureSample(...)` at lines 34, 110, 154, 218,
335, 382, 434, 484-485, 531-532, 601, 617, 718, 854-917). The work is a per-slot bias uniform and a
call swap, not a new subsystem. EasyGL itself only implements the state on desktop core
(`GL_TEXTURE_LOD_BIAS` does not exist in ES at all), so a WebGPU emulation covering every stock
effect and `SpriteBatch` would be *broader* than the reference. Compiled effects inherit it when that
path exists; a custom CNAEXT `ShaderEffect` writes its own WGSL and is out of scope, which is a route
boundary to state rather than a silent drop. → `WEBGPU-161` narrowed to `MaxMipLevel`,
`WEBGPU-205` added for the bias.

**C5. BC-compressed cube textures are representable on WebGPU, so honest refusal is an interim
state, not parity.** `WEBGPU-163` offered "implement, **or** refuse honestly" as equal outcomes. They
are not equal: refusal is the right *immediate* fix for a false promise (today
`TextureCube(device, size, mipMap, SurfaceFormat::Dxt1)` constructs and every `SetData` throws), but
parity is the implementation. `WebGPURenderer::CreateTextureCube(size, mipMap, /*surfaceFormat*/)`
discards the format outright and `WebGPUTextureCubeRenderer`'s constructor takes only `(owner, size,
mipMap)`, while the 2D path already has everything needed: `ClassifyWebGPUTextureFormat` maps
DXT1/3/5, DXT5-sRGB and BC7/BC7-sRGB to `WGPUTextureFormat_BC*` with their block sizes, and
`bcSupported_` gates it on `WGPUFeatureName_TextureCompressionBC` being enabled on the device. In
WebGPU a cube is a 2D texture with six array layers plus a `WGPUTextureViewDimension_Cube` view;
nothing in the BC feature restricts it to non-array 2D. So `WEBGPU-206` must implement it and `163`
keeps only the stop-lying half. → `WEBGPU-163` rewritten, `WEBGPU-206` added.

**C6. The wireframe refusal message is factually wrong about wgpu-native, which strengthens
`WEBGPU-153` rather than changing it.** `RequireSupportedFillModeEXT` tells the caller "wgpu-native
exposes no polygon mode, so a wireframe request cannot reach any native pipeline state". The pin has
one: `WGPUPrimitiveStateExtras::polygonMode` with `WGPUPolygonMode_Line`, behind
`WGPUNativeFeature_PolygonModeLine` (wgpu.h 0x00030013, listed for DX12/Vulkan/Metal). It is
native-only — a browser build cannot use it — so **triangle-edge re-expansion stays the
implementation** `WEBGPU-153` schedules, because it is the one route that works in both targets. The
correction is to the *claim*: the sentence must not survive into the implemented renderer, and it is
one more instance of the pattern this section exists to catch.

**C7. Two "derived capabilities report false, which is truthful" entries were filed under
*not a gap*.** The audit's closing bullet lists `FloatRenderTargets` and `HalfFloatRenderTargets`
among six capabilities whose `false` is "truthful at the `GraphicsDevice` seam". Truthful is not the
question the parity rule asks. Both are now gaps with rows (`WEBGPU-199`/`200`); the other four
(`CompiledEffects` — see C2 — `HalfFloatTextureLinearFiltering`, `ComputeShaders`, `IndirectDraw`)
keep their classification, the last two by the `plan_modern.md` boundary.

**C8. The shared behavioural harness must come first, not last.** `WEBGPU-193` scheduled the
renderer-neutral comparison mechanism as the final local task, which would have every behavioural row
between here and there write a WebGPU-only test that a later corpus then duplicates. The mechanism
already exists and needs extending, not inventing: `modules/graphics/examples/
cross_renderer_diagnostic_scene.cpp` and `cross_renderer_2d_corpus.cpp` are renderer-agnostic sources
built once per renderer (EasyGL, WebGPU, Vulkan, SOFTWARE, Direct2D all register their own binary),
dumping a 64×64 RGBA8 frame in one format that `cross_renderer_diagnostic_compare.cpp` judges, with
`scripts/run-webgpu-parity-test.sh` driving the Emscripten side. `WEBGPU-207` stands that up as
reusable fixture infrastructure before Wave 1; `WEBGPU-193` stays as the corpus-complete milestone.

**C9. `WEBGPU-180` was a placeholder audit task.** "Inventory what must survive a device loss" is
work the audit had already done. The inventory is written out below as a table in this file, so
`WEBGPU-180` now owns only the three questions the inventory genuinely cannot answer from source.

### EasyGL defects found while auditing (record, do not clone)

- `EasyGLRenderer::DrawPrimitivesEx`/`DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx` gate the wireframe re-expansion on
  `!multiStream`, so a **multi-stream draw under `FillMode::WireFrame` silently renders solid** while
  `SupportsCapability(WireFrame)` reports true. That is the exact defect class `WEBGPU-115` was
  written to prevent. WebGPU's own implementation (`WEBGPU-154`) must handle it or refuse it by name.
  Belongs to EasyGL's backlog, not this one.
- `easygl_unknown_stride_rejection_test` and the stride-32 program selection are still partly
  stride-driven (`REMED-GFX-234` closed one case by asking the declaration; the selection itself is
  not fully semantic). Noted so `WEBGPU-155` copies the *contract*, not the implementation shape.

### Parity matrix by feature family

Legend: **=** equivalent; **~** implemented but under-verified on WebGPU; **≠** WebGPU behaves
differently or refuses; **∅** absent on WebGPU; **out** out of scope (see the boundary table).
Counts are in-scope EasyGL example programs vs WebGPU ones for that family.

| Family | EGL | WGPU | State | Evidence / new rows |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Clear / present / backbuffer readback | — | 2 | **=** | `WebGPU_Clear_Readback`, `ReadBackbuffer` |
| Presentation modes, virtual resolution, window→logical transforms | 8 | 1 | **≠** | `GetDefaultViewportRect` not overridden; no resize test → `WEBGPU-162`, `WEBGPU-192` |
| BlendState (13 factors, 5 functions, separate colour/alpha, BlendFactor, MultiSampleMask, ColorWriteChannels) | 9 | ~2 | **~** | Native maps complete and correct; per-value pixel coverage is EasyGL-only → `WEBGPU-190` |
| DepthStencilState (8 compare funcs, 8 stencil ops, masks, two-sided, write enable) | 9 | 3 | **~** | Maps complete; `WebGPU_Stencil*` covers stencil, not the depth compare sweep → `WEBGPU-190` |
| RasterizerState — CullMode, depth bias, slope-scale bias, scissor | 4 | 1 | **~** | Implemented (`depthBias` is baked into the pipeline key); no cull/bias pixel test → `WEBGPU-190` |
| RasterizerState — `FillMode::WireFrame` | 1 | 1 | **≠** | EasyGL emulates; WebGPU refuses → `WEBGPU-153`/`154` |
| Viewport (sub-region, MinDepth/MaxDepth, reset on target switch) | 2 | 1 | **~** | Per-draw capture is real (`REMED-GFX-116`); the letterbox default is wrong → `WEBGPU-162`, `WEBGPU-190` |
| SamplerState — filter, AddressU/V, anisotropy | 8 | ~1 | **~** | Implemented for all 16 slots; only slots 0/1 reach a stock draw; little pixel coverage → `WEBGPU-186` |
| SamplerState — AddressW, MaxMipLevel | 1 | 0 | **∅** | `ApplySamplerAddressW`/`ApplySamplerMipState` not overridden at all; `WGPUSamplerDescriptor` has `addressModeW` and `lodMinClamp` → `WEBGPU-160`/`161` |
| SamplerState — MipMapLevelOfDetailBias | 1 | 0 | **∅** | No sampler field in WebGPU, but WGSL `textureSampleBias` expresses the same semantic in the fragment stage where all 20+ of this renderer's sampling calls already live (C4) → `WEBGPU-205` |
| Texture2D create/SetData/GetData/mips/NPOT | 8 | 4 | **~** | Real; partial-rect, NPOT, mip-filter coverage missing → `WEBGPU-185`/`187` |
| TextureCube / Texture3D | 7 | 2 | **~** | Real (`WEBGPU-56`/`57`/`112`/`113`); partial-rect/box and face coverage missing → `WEBGPU-185` |
| Block-compressed textures — Texture2D | 2 | 2 | **=** (better) | WebGPU is the only renderer with native BC upload (`WEBGPU-144`) |
| Block-compressed textures — TextureCube | 1 | 0 | **≠** | Claimed by `ClassifySurfaceFormatEXT`, absent in the cube renderer (which discards `surfaceFormat` entirely). A BC cube is a six-layer 2D texture plus a cube view — representable wherever `WGPUFeatureName_TextureCompressionBC` is enabled (C5) → `WEBGPU-163` stops the false promise, `WEBGPU-206` reaches parity |
| SurfaceFormat — packed 16-bit (`Bgr565`/`Bgra5551`/`Bgra4444`) and `NormalizedByte2`/`4` | 2 | 0 | **∅** | EasyGL stores them natively (REMED-GFX-244); WebGPU maps every non-BC format to `RGBA8Unorm` and the shared `Texture::ValidateFormat` refuses them by name (honest, not silent) → `WEBGPU-183`/`184` |
| RenderTarget2D — half-float formats (`HalfSingle`/`HalfVector2`/`HalfVector4`/`HdrBlendable`) | 1 | 0 | **∅** | Added 2026-09-04 second pass (C1); the audit had excluded this whole family. EasyGL maps and probes all four; WebGPU never overrides `ClassifyRenderTargetFormatEXT`, so `Texture::ValidateFormat` refuses everything but `Color`. `R16Float`/`RG16Float`/`RGBA16Float` are renderable, filterable and blendable in core WebGPU → `WEBGPU-197`→`198`→`199` |
| RenderTarget2D — full-float formats (`Single`/`Vector2`/`Vector4`) | 1 | 0 | **∅** | Same origin (C1). `R32Float`/`RG32Float`/`RGBA32Float` are renderable in core WebGPU; sampling the result back needs `Float32Filterable` and blending into it needs `Float32Blendable`, both probeable adapter features → `WEBGPU-197`→`198`→`200` |
| RenderTarget2D — `Rgba64` | 1 | 0 | **∅** | Same origin (C1), and not scheduled by the audit at all. EasyGL allocates `GL_RGBA16` on desktop core and reports `Unsupported` elsewhere; WebGPU has `RGBA16Unorm` behind `WGPUNativeFeature_TextureFormat16bitNorm` (native) or `TextureFormatsTier2` (browser) → `WEBGPU-201` |
| Render-target colour format → pipeline compatibility | — | — | **∅** | Not a public API of its own, but the shared root of the three rows above and of per-target MSAA: all twelve WebGPU pipeline families bake the swap-chain format and one global sample count → `WEBGPU-197` |
| RenderTarget2D — bind, draw, sample back, GetData, depth formats, MSAA | 6 | 2 | **~** | Real and tested; `PreserveContents`/`DiscardContents`, MSAA change and RT properties untested → `WEBGPU-189` |
| RenderTarget2D — `mipMap=true` | 1 | 0 | **∅** | `CreateRenderTarget2D` throws → `WEBGPU-164` |
| RenderTarget2D / RenderTargetCube — per-instance `multiSampleCount` | 1 | 0 | **≠** | Ignored; mirrors the renderer-global count. Publicly observable through `RenderTarget2D.MultiSampleCount` without rendering anything (C3) → `WEBGPU-197` (pipeline key) then `WEBGPU-165` |
| RenderTargetCube | 3 | 1 | **~** | Real incl. per-face MSAA and mips (`WEBGPU-114`) |
| MRT | 1 | 1 | **=** | `WEBGPU-85`/`86`/`87` |
| Occlusion query | 2 | 1 | **=** | `WEBGPU-84`; exact sample counts |
| VertexBuffer / IndexBuffer — SetData, SetDataOptions, GetData, 16/32-bit, dynamic | 5 | 3 | **~** | Buffer `GetData` is a shared CPU shadow, so renderer-neutral; 32-bit index draw untested → `WEBGPU-188` |
| Draw routes — `DrawUserPrimitives`, range validation, primitive-type validation, `VertexBufferBinding.VertexOffset` | 7 | 2 | **~** | The single-stream binding offset is folded into `vertexStart`/`baseVertex` by `GraphicsDevice`, so it **is** honoured; the rest is untested here → `WEBGPU-188` |
| Vertex declarations — semantic-driven stock layout, `LitUntextured`, stride 36, stride-32-without-normal, DualTexture `TEXCOORD1` | ~6 | 0 | **≠** | The largest single behavioural gap → `WEBGPU-155`–`159` |
| Multi-stream vertex input (`MultiStreamVertexInput`) | 1 | 0 | **∅** | `WebGPURenderer.cpp` never reads `params.vertexStreams`; capability false, `GraphicsDevice` refuses the draw → `WEBGPU-172` |
| Instancing (one per-instance stream, offsets, frequency) | 1 | 3 | **=** | `WEBGPU-27`/`38`/`68` + two cardinality tests |
| BasicEffect | 20 | ~7 | **~/≠** | Fog at parity; lit-untextured and stride-36 missing; combination coverage thin → `WEBGPU-156`/`157`/`173` |
| AlphaTestEffect | 6 | 2 | **✅** | All 8 `CompareFunction`s proven by an 8×3 signature matrix, null-texture and vertex-colour+diffuse covered → `WEBGPU-174` |
| DualTextureEffect | 10 | 2 | **✅** | `TEXCOORD1` consumed independently (`WEBGPU-159`); doubling, overlay, `DiffuseColor`, `Alpha` and both null-layer fallbacks proven byte-identical to EasyGL → `WEBGPU-175` |
| EnvironmentMapEffect | 12 | 3 | **✅** | Specular, multilight, world transform, `EyePosition` and the Fresnel gradient all proven; the per-fragment Fresnel defect fixed → `WEBGPU-176` |
| SkinnedEffect | 14 | 4 | **✅** | Identity/translation/50-50-blended bones, the palette's last slot, specular, light count, vertex colour and `Vector4` blend indices all proven → `WEBGPU-177` |
| Compiled XNA Effect Framework (`Effect(device, byte[])`) | 1 file, 1 025 lines of tests | 0 | **∅** | `SupportsCompiledEffects()` false; `Effect` throws `NotSupportedException`. Native SPIR-V is one candidate route, not the only one, and the browser needs a WGSL route whatever the native answer is (C2) → `WEBGPU-166`, `203`, `204`, then `167`–`171` |
| Custom `ShaderEffect` (CNAEXT) — 2D source pair, SpriteBatch effect, MRT fan-out | 9 | 3 | **out** (partial) | WebGPU has WGSL `ShaderEffect` + SpriteBatch effects; cube/volume sampler binding absent — recorded, not scheduled |
| SpriteBatch — source rect, rotation, origin, scale, flip, tint/alpha, layer depth, transform, sort modes, render targets, state leak | 9 | 3 | **✅** | Both families proven by parity fixtures against a four-quadrant chart; a stale `GraphicsDevice.Viewport` that scaled every sprite was found and fixed → `WEBGPU-178` |
| SpriteFont | 6 | 0 | **✅** | A three-colour atlas, a newline and a default-character substitution proven byte-identical to EasyGL → `WEBGPU-179` |
| Resource lifetime — disposal, double disposal, destruction order, bound-resource dispose, leaks, move | 8 | 1 | **~** | `REMED-GFX-167` keep-alives exist; nothing stresses them → `WEBGPU-191` |
| Device/context loss and recovery | 2 | 0 | **∅** | `DebugSimulateContextLoss`/`DebugRestoreContext`/`SetContextRecoveryEnabled`/`CanBeginDrawEXT`/`ShareCpuPixels` all inherited → `WEBGPU-107`, `WEBGPU-180`–`182` |
| Cross-renderer pixel parity | — | 1 | **~** | One diagnostic triangle (`WEBGPU-123`) on an existing renderer-neutral harness that every behavioural row should be extending from the start (C8) → `WEBGPU-207` first, `WEBGPU-193` as the completion milestone |
| CNAEXT engine-layer passes, PBR/glTF/model, compute, HDR | 62 | 2 | **out** | `plan_modern.md` / `plan_cnj.md` / `plan_gltf.md` |

### Leads that were checked and did NOT confirm as gaps

Recorded so nobody re-opens them:

- **`Texture3D`'s ignored `surfaceFormat`.** `WebGPURenderer::CreateTexture3D` does drop the
  parameter, but `Texture3D`'s constructor calls `Texture::ValidateFormat(format)` unconditionally,
  which accepts only `SurfaceFormat::Color` on **every** renderer including EasyGL. No public
  behaviour differs. (The dead parameter is a comment-tidy item at most.)
- **`VertexBufferBinding.VertexOffset` on the ordinary draw routes.** `GraphicsDevice::
  FoldedVertexStreamOffset()` folds the common per-vertex offset into `vertexStart`/`baseVertex`
  before the renderer sees it, and WebGPU honours both. `OrdinaryDrawBindingOffsetTests` already
  names WebGPU in its renderer set. Only *differing* per-stream offsets need `WEBGPU-172`.
- **SpriteBatch and depth.** Neither EasyGL nor WebGPU puts `layerDepth` into a Z coordinate or
  depth-tests sprites; both hardcode a disabled depth state. Equal, so not a parity gap (whether
  *XNA* does is a separate question for `plan_graphics.md`).
- **`SetImmediateMode`.** Overridden by Vulkan/SDL_GPU/Canvas/PixiJS/Blend2D; **not** by EasyGL and
  not by WebGPU. Equal.
- **Blend factor / blend function / compare function / stencil operation maps.** All complete and
  correct on WebGPU (13/5/8/8 values). Only the pixel-level verification is thin.
- **Depth bias and slope-scale depth bias.** Implemented, baked into the pipeline cache key with
  FNA's own 24-bit scale.
- **`RenderTargetUsage` (`preserveContents`) on both target kinds.** Threaded and honoured
  (`REMED-GFX-136`); only the test is missing.
- **`HasRealDepthBuffer`/`HasRealStencilBuffer`.** The inherited defaults are correct for WebGPU's
  exact per-value `DepthFormat` mapping (`WEBGPU-39`).
- **The six "derived" capabilities** (`CompiledEffects`, `FloatRenderTargets`,
  `HalfFloatRenderTargets`, `HalfFloatTextureLinearFiltering`, `ComputeShaders`, `IndirectDraw`) all
  report `false` at the `GraphicsDevice` seam, which is truthful.
  **Partly withdrawn 2026-09-04, second pass (C7).** Truthful reporting is not the question the
  parity rule asks. `FloatRenderTargets` and `HalfFloatRenderTargets` are gaps
  (`WEBGPU-199`/`WEBGPU-200`), and `CompiledEffects` is a gap until a named blocker exists
  (`WEBGPU-203`/`204`). `HalfFloatTextureLinearFiltering`, `ComputeShaders` and `IndirectDraw` keep
  the original classification — the last two by the `plan_modern.md` boundary.

> follows the dependency order); the next new ID is **208**. **No ID is ever reused** — a rewritten

> have never existed here — see the archival banner's own note.

> stated acceptance criteria; 🟨 code or documentation exists but has not met them; ⬜ not
> implemented. A source comment is not verification. A passing build is not pixel correctness. A
> screenshot that "looks right" is not sufficient where a deterministic pixel assertion is possible.

Run it after every status change and paste its output into the Status summary; never hand-write a count. ### Phase 70.0 — P0: false or under-delivered promises, and behaviour that renders wrong pixels

| # | Task | Status | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| WEBGPU-207 | Stand up the shared EasyGL↔WebGPU behavioural fixture mechanism **before** Wave 1. | ✅ | **Why first (C8):** the audit scheduled the renderer-neutral comparison as `WEBGPU-193`, the last local task. Every behavioural row between here and there would then write a WebGPU-only test that the corpus later duplicates, and two independently written tests of the same behaviour are two oracles that can disagree. **The mechanism already exists — extend it, do not invent one.** `modules/graphics/examples/cross_renderer_diagnostic_scene.cpp` and `cross_renderer_2d_corpus.cpp` are renderer-agnostic sources (no `#ifdef`, no renderer include) built once per renderer — EasyGL, WebGPU, Vulkan, SOFTWARE and Direct2D each register their own binary — dumping a 64×64 RGBA8 frame in the single format `cross_renderer_diagnostic_compare.cpp` reads; `scripts/run-webgpu-parity-test.sh` drives the Emscripten side. **Deliverable:** a fixture convention plus the CMake wiring that lets ONE renderer-neutral source be added and automatically built for both EasyGL and WebGPU with a comparator invocation, so a behavioural row's cost is "write the fixture" rather than "write two tests and a runner". Include the tolerance convention `WEBGPU-193` already states (interior pixels, or a justified tolerance where fill rules legitimately differ) so each row does not re-invent it. **Execution rule this row establishes:** every behavioural row in Phase 70 adds or reuses a shared fixture here; a WebGPU-only test is correct only for native lifecycle/validation detail that has no EasyGL counterpart (device creation, wgpu validation scopes, deferred-replay internals). At minimum the harness must be able to carry the fixtures for `WEBGPU-155`–`159` (semantic declarations, DualTexture `TEXCOORD1`), `153` (wireframe), `164` (render-target mipmaps), `172` (multi-stream), `197`–`202` (surface formats) and `173`–`177` (stock effects). **Acceptance:** one new shared fixture is added through the convention and produces comparable dumps under both renderers; the convention is documented where a later row will find it. **Delivered 2026-09-04.** `modules/graphics/examples/parity/` holds the convention: `ParityFixture.hpp` (a `PixelTestGame` subclass that owns a fixed unscaled `NativeBackBuffer` device, a `ParityGrid` cell mapper, and the assertions a parity case needs -- `ExpectAverage`, `ExpectDistinct`, `ExpectBrighter`, `ExpectFlat` -- plus a raw RGBA8 frame dump in exactly the format `cross_renderer_diagnostic_compare.cpp` already reads), and `ParityFixtures.cmake` holds the ONE fixture list plus `cna_register_parity_fixtures()`, which each renderer calls once (`modules/renderers/{easygl,webgpu}/examples/CMakeLists.txt`) to get one executable and one CTest per fixture through its own build macro. Adding a case is "write `parity_<name>.cpp`, append `<name>` to one list" -- no per-renderer edit and no second oracle. `cna_diag_compare` gained an optional `WxH` argument so a fixture may pick its own resolution and still be judged by the existing comparator; `scripts/run-parity-fixture.sh` runs both legs and diffs them. **Verified:** five fixtures (`vertex_semantics`, `lit_untextured`, `lit_vertex_color`, `unlit_position_color`, `dual_texture_uv1`) execute under BOTH renderers with 53/53 assertions passing on each, and `scripts/run-parity-fixture.sh <name>` reports **max diff = 0** on all five -- byte-identical frames, not "within tolerance". Documented in [`docs/cross-renderer-parity-fixtures.md`](../docs/cross-renderer-parity-fixtures.md). **Commands:** `ctest -R EasyGL_Parity` / `ctest -R WebGPU_Parity`; `CNA_PARITY_DISPLAY=:131 scripts/run-parity-fixture.sh vertex_semantics`. |
| WEBGPU-153 | Implement `FillMode::WireFrame` by triangle-edge re-expansion, as EasyGL does, and report the capability true. | ✅ | **Missing behaviour:** a `RasterizerState` with `FillMode::WireFrame` throws `System::NotSupportedException` on every polygon draw route (`WebGPURenderer::RequireSupportedFillModeEXT`). **EasyGL evidence:** `EasyGLRenderer::DrawWireframe()` expands a `TriangleList`/`TriangleStrip` index sequence into a 32-bit `GL_LINES` index buffer and draws it — no polygon-mode API is involved, which is why REMED-GFX-219 made `SupportsCapability(WireFrame)` true on every GL profile that has 32-bit indices. **Expected:** the same expansion, drawn through a `WGPUPrimitiveTopology_LineList` variant of whichever stock/custom pipeline the draw already selected, with a per-draw scratch index buffer taken from the `WEBGPU-12`/`59` transient pool. Line and point primitives keep today's accept-unchanged behaviour. **Source:** `WebGPURenderer.cpp` `RequireSupportedFillModeEXT`, `SupportsCapability`, `Make3DPipelineKey` (already keys `wireframe`), `Build3DPipelineEXT`. **Depends on:** nothing. **Tests:** flip `modules/graphics/tests/.../WebGpuWireFrameContractTests.cpp` from "refuses" to the shared oracle in `WireFrameTriangleOracle.hpp`; a new `WebGPU_WireFrame` example asserting the same numbers EasyGL asserts (interior 0/N filled, all three edges present, and a Solid/WireFrame A/B that is NOT byte-identical). **Acceptance:** shared oracle passes; `GraphicsDevice::SupportsCapability(WireFrame)` true; no pipeline-cache explosion (one extra key per topology). Also update `docs/webgpu-vs-vulkan-deviations.md`, whose "Wireframe: refused, not emulated" section states the now-disproved rationale. **Correction 2026-09-04, second pass (C6):** the refusal message `RequireSupportedFillModeEXT` currently prints — "wgpu-native exposes no polygon mode, so a wireframe request cannot reach any native pipeline state" — is factually wrong. The pin has `WGPUPrimitiveStateExtras::polygonMode` with `WGPUPolygonMode_Line`, behind `WGPUNativeFeature_PolygonModeLine` (wgpu.h 0x00030013, listed for DX12/Vulkan/Metal). It is native-only, so **edge re-expansion stays the implementation this row schedules** — it is the one route that works natively and in the browser — but that sentence must not survive into the implemented renderer, and `WEBGPU-194` must not carry it forward into the documentation. **Delivered 2026-09-05.** `ExpandTriangleEdgesForWireframeEXT()` rewrites a queued triangle draw into a 32-bit indexed `WGPUPrimitiveTopology_LineList` draw over the same vertices -- the reference renderer's own mechanism. It runs at QUEUE time, so no `Issue*Draw` path has a wireframe branch at all: what the replay receives is an ordinary indexed line-list command, and the topology was already part of every pipeline cache key. Wired into **eleven** queue families (colored, textured, colored-textured, lit-textured, alpha-test, dual-texture, env-map, skinned, PBR, skinned-PBR, instanced) plus the custom-WGSL `ShaderEffect` route, which gained the `wireframe` field it was missing. `SupportsCapability(WireFrame)` is now true. **One thing the row did not anticipate:** WebGPU forbids a non-zero depth bias on a line topology, so `Build3DPipelineEXT` and the custom-effect pipeline now drop `RasterizerState.DepthBias` for a non-triangle topology. That is not a substitution -- a depth bias is defined as an offset along a polygon's depth slope, which a line has none of -- and without it a wireframe draw under a depth-biased `RasterizerState` would fail pipeline creation rather than render. `WebGpuWireFrameContract.WireFrameAndRecoveryAreNativelyClean` is the arm that would have caught it. **C6 discharged:** the refusal message's claim -- "wgpu-native exposes no polygon mode, so a wireframe request cannot reach any native pipeline state" -- is gone from the renderer, from `docs/webgpu-vs-vulkan-deviations.md` and from `docs/webgpu-renderer.md`, each of which now records why `WGPUPrimitiveStateExtras::polygonMode` is deliberately unused (native-only; edge expansion is the one route that also works in the browser). **Verified.** The SHARED oracle: `WireFrameTriangleOracle.hpp`'s `RendersEdges()` now includes WebGPU and `RejectsWireFrame()` is empty again (a reading, not an omission), so `GraphicsDeviceCapabilityTest`'s four wireframe arms judge WebGPU by the same measurements as every other renderer -- solid `total=18176 interior=1089/1089`, wireframe `total=607 interior=0/1089 AB=25 BC=31 CA=26`. `WebGpuWireFrameContractTests.cpp` was rewritten from the refusal contract to the wireframe one, keeping its native cardinality counters: a wireframe draw costs exactly 1 command, 1 native draw, 1 extra pipeline, 0 uncaptured errors, and `EveryPublicDrawRouteWireframesAndAcceptsSolid` asserts it per route across all 12 routes. New shared fixture `parity_fill_mode_wireframe` (`WEBGPU-207`) passes 10/10 on both renderers. 13/13 wireframe tests, 19/19 WebGPU 3D + parity CTests. **Tolerance note:** this fixture is judged by its assertions, NOT by the whole-frame dump. Measured EasyGL vs WebGPU: 367 of 32768 pixels differ (1.12%), 120 on the solid triangle's edges and 247 on the wireframe's lines, none elsewhere. A picture made entirely of one-pixel lines is all boundary, and boundary is where two conforming rasterizers may differ; widening the comparator's tolerance to swallow that would make it meaningless, so the fixture says so in its header instead. |
| WEBGPU-154 | Decide and enforce the wireframe boundary for the routes the re-expansion cannot cover. | ✅ | **Why separate:** EasyGL gates its own expansion on `!multiStream` and skips it for indirect draws, so a **multi-stream wireframe draw silently renders solid there** while its capability says true — the exact defect `WEBGPU-115` exists to prevent, and it must not be cloned. **Expected:** on WebGPU each uncovered route either performs the expansion or refuses by name with a message naming the route; never a silent solid fill. **Depends on:** `WEBGPU-153`, and interacts with `WEBGPU-172`. **Tests:** one contract test per uncovered route asserting the named refusal (or the correct wireframe once covered). **Acceptance:** no route accepts a wireframe request and draws a filled polygon. Record the EasyGL defect in `plans/plan_graphics.md` rather than fixing it here. **Half delivered 2026-09-05; the other half decided and implemented 2026-09-06.** **DONE — the 3D routes.** `WEBGPU-153` did not leave an uncovered 3D route to gate: the expansion is wired into all eleven deferred draw families plus the custom-WGSL `ShaderEffect` route, and `WebGpuWireFrameContract.EveryPublicDrawRouteWireframesAndAcceptsSolid` asserts it PER ROUTE across all twelve — ordinary non-indexed and indexed (16- and 32-bit, with nonzero `vertexStart` / `startIndex` / `baseVertex`), both `DrawUserPrimitives` families, the stock-effect family and the instanced route. Each leg asserts interior 0, a non-empty frame and `wire.LitTotal() * 4 < solid.LitTotal()` separately, because "some routes wireframe and the rest quietly fill" is the defect this row exists to prevent and a whole-suite total would hide it. `OnlyPolygonTopologiesAreExpanded` tests the boundary from both sides. **Multi-stream: DONE 2026-09-05.** This clause used to read "not a silent fill because the capability is false", which `WEBGPU-172` made obsolete by turning it true. It is now covered rather than vacuous: `ExpandTriangleEdgesForWireframeEXT` rewrites INDICES only and never touches the vertex data, so it is independent of how many streams a draw binds -- the same expansion runs for a two-stream draw as for a one-stream one, and `EveryPublicDrawRouteWireframesAndAcceptsSolid` still passes on every route including the instanced one, which `WEBGPU-172` also converted. The EasyGL defect this row names (its expansion is gated on `!multiStream`) is therefore one WebGPU does **not** share. **OPEN — SpriteBatch.** `SpriteBatch` draws triangle pairs, and neither renderer applies the fill mode to them. MEASURED on EasyGL, four ways (plain `Begin()`, a Solid `RasterizerState` passed to `Begin`, a WireFrame one passed to `Begin`, and a WireFrame one set on the device only): **576/576 pixels filled in every case** — a silent solid fill on the reference renderer, which real XNA would wireframe. The WebGPU side could NOT be measured cleanly: in a `ParityFixture` configuration (`PresentationMode::NativeBackBuffer`, sprites to the backbuffer, `GetBackBufferData` inside `Draw()`) its sprite output was not observed at all, while all nine existing `WebGPU_SpriteBatch*` CTests pass — so the probe configuration is the outlier and no renderer defect is claimed. **(1) SETTLED, and the earlier probe was the outlier it was suspected of being.** A `ParityFixture` does observe sprite output: three shared fixtures draw sprites and read them back in exactly that configuration — `sprite_state`, `sprite_font` and, added 2026-09-06, `sprite_sampler_state`, whose whole subject is sprite pixels and which reads four sprite cells per run on both renderers with byte-identical frames. Nothing about the harness prevents it. **(2) DECIDED — implemented, not refused.** `SpriteBatch` now wireframes. The decision turned on this project's own tie-break rather than on taste: XNA's `FillMode` is a `RasterizerState` field that applies to every primitive the device rasterizes, `SpriteBatch.Begin` ASSIGNS the device's `RasterizerState`, and where FNA (here, the reference renderer) and XNA disagree, XNA wins. Filling would also have violated this row's own acceptance — no route may accept a wireframe request and draw a filled polygon — and "the reference does it too" is exactly the defence `WEBGPU-115` exists to reject. **How:** the sketch above, unchanged. `SpriteCommand` captures `fillModeWireframe_` at enqueue like every other per-sprite state; `EnsureSpriteWireIndexBufferEXT()` creates ONE 12-entry uint16 line list, shared by every sprite ever drawn because each sprite's six vertices are reached through `baseVertex = spriteIndex * 6`, so a wireframe batch allocates nothing per sprite; and the fill mode joins the sprite pipeline cache key, since a line-list pipeline is a different pipeline object rather than a different draw call. The custom-WGSL `ShaderEffect` sprite route takes the same two changes, so neither sprite route fills. The six edges are the quad's outline plus its shared diagonal drawn twice — the same thing `ExpandTriangleEdgesForWireframeEXT` does with a shared interior edge, so a wireframe sprite and a wireframe quad agree rather than merely resemble each other. **Verified:** `EveryPublicDrawRouteWireframesAndAcceptsSolid` grew a thirteenth route. Solid fills 36864 pixels, WireFrame draws 957. The per-route interior probe is new and load-bearing: the oracle's probe is the shared TRIANGLE's centroid, and asserting it about a quad measures the quad's diagonal rather than its fill, so `Route` now states its own box — and the SOLID leg asserts that box is FULL, or "empty under WireFrame" could be satisfied by a probe that lands outside the geometry entirely. **Negative control:** forcing `command.wireframe = false` at enqueue turned exactly the SpriteBatch leg red (interior 1089/1089) and left the other nine tests green. 73/73 sprite-related CTests pass. **The divergence is recorded, not hidden:** `plans/plan_graphics.md` row 1115 now carries the decision and the fact that until EasyGL adopts the same recipe the two renderers differ here by design. **EasyGL defect recorded** in `plans/plan_graphics.md` as the row directs, not fixed here. |
| WEBGPU-155 | Build the stock 3D vertex layout from the declaration's **semantics**, not from the buffer stride. | ✅ | **Missing behaviour:** `WebGPURenderer::DrawPrimitivesEx`/`DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx` select a stock family by `webgpuVb.Stride()` and every `GetOrCreatePipeline*3D()` hardcodes that stride's attribute offsets; `RequireFaithfulDeclarationEXT` then refuses any declaration whose elements do not sit exactly where the canonical table for that stride puts them (`UnlistedStrideLayout::RendererRefusesIt`). **EasyGL evidence:** `EasyGLRenderer::ConfigureDeclarationForStockProgramEXT()` binds each stock program input (`kPos`/`kColor`/`kUv`/`kUv1`/`kNormal`/`kTangent`/`kWeights`/`kIndices`) by `(usage, usageIndex)` at the declaration's own offset and format, and `RequireDeclarationMatchesStockProgram()` checks only the *format* of the semantics the program consumes — offsets and element ORDER are deliberately unrestricted, because XNB model data routinely orders `TextureCoordinate` before `Normal`. **Expected:** WebGPU builds its `WGPUVertexAttribute` array from the declaration for the stock families, keeping the stride only as the pipeline's `arrayStride`; unnamed inputs stay unbound with the same neutral defaults the stock WGSL already assumes. **Source:** `WebGPURenderer.cpp` `Build3DPipelineEXT` + the twelve `GetOrCreatePipeline*3D()` families; `modules/graphics/include/CNA/Internal/Graphics/VertexDeclarationFidelity.hpp`. **Depends on:** nothing; **blocks** `WEBGPU-156`–`159`. **Tests:** a WebGPU example that draws the same mesh through three declarations that differ only in element order/offset and asserts identical pixels; keep `RequireFaithfulDeclarationEXT` for genuinely unrepresentable declarations and keep its refusal test. **Acceptance:** declaration order and offsets no longer change the rendered result; the pipeline cache key includes the declaration shape. **Delivered 2026-09-04.** New renderer-neutral resolver `modules/graphics/include/CNA/Internal/Graphics/StockVertexSemantics.hpp`: the canonical `StockProgramInput` constants, `FindDeclaredSemanticEXT`/`DeclarationNamesUsageEXT`, `ResolveStockVertexLayoutEXT` (binds each input by `(usage, usageIndex)` at the declared element's own offset and format) and `HashResolvedStockVertexLayoutEXT` for the cache key. In the renderer: `SelectStockVertexShapeEXT()` picks the family from the declaration's semantics plus the effect state, `ResolveStockVertexLayoutForDrawEXT()` validates the format (`RequireDeclarationMatchesStockProgram`) and resolves the bindings, `BuildStockVertexStateEXT()` turns them into `WGPUVertexBufferLayout`s, and ONE `DispatchStockDrawEXT()` replaces the stride ladder in both `DrawPrimitivesEx` and `DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx`. The eight converted `GetOrCreatePipeline*3D()` families take the resolved layout and fold its digest into their cache key; every hardcoded `offsetof()` attribute array is gone; the stride survives only as `arrayStride`. **The neutral record:** a semantic a program declares but the declaration does not supply reads a shared 16-byte `(0,0,0,1)` buffer bound at slot 1 with per-instance step -- D3D9's rule for missing vertex-register components, and equally OpenGL's disabled generic attribute, which is what the reference renderer leaves such an input at. **NOT converted, deliberately:** the skinned, PBR and instanced routes keep the stride table and `RequireFaithfulDeclarationEXT`'s refusal (now raised inside the `StrideDerived` arm rather than at the entry point); `WEBGPU-172`/`WEBGPU-177` own their conversion. *(Update 2026-09-05: `WEBGPU-172` converted the instanced route. Only skinned and PBR remain stride-derived.)* **Verified:** the shared fixture `parity_vertex_semantics` draws one mesh through four legal declarations of the same semantic content (canonical stride 24; the same stride with the semantics MOVED; the same placement PADDED to stride 32 -- `VertexPositionNormalTexture`'s; the same placement with the declaration list REORDERED) and gets 16 identical exact colours per leg -- 33/33 on both renderers, and `run-parity-fixture.sh vertex_semantics` reports max diff 0. The renderer-neutral `VertexDeclarationLayoutTests` moved WebGPU from the refusing arm to the translating one (`TranslatesDeclarations()`), and its 6 layout cases plus `DeclarationGuardTest.TheTranslatingRendererStillRendersEveryCollidingDeclaration` pass: 87 passed, 5 skipped (the refusal arms, correctly). 14/14 WebGPU 3D-family CTests still pass. **Commands:** `CnaTests --gtest_filter='VertexDeclaration*:*DeclarationGuard*'` in `cmake-build-webgpu`; `ctest -R "WebGPU_(Colored3D|DrawPrimitivesEx|Textured3D|ColoredTextured3D|LitTextured3D|ShaderValidation|AlphaTest3D|DualTexture3D|EnvMap3D|Instanced3D|Skinned3D|Pbr3D)"`. |
| WEBGPU-156 | `BasicEffect` lighting on an untextured `VertexPositionNormal` mesh (stride 24, two elements). | ✅ | **Missing behaviour:** WebGPU has no lit-untextured shader. A stride-24 buffer is assumed to be `VertexPositionColorTexture`, so a Position+Normal declaration is refused by `RequireFaithfulDeclarationEXT` ("…carries Normal, which this renderer's native layout for a 24-byte record does not bind at all"). **EasyGL evidence:** `StockProgramShape::LitUntextured` / `LitVertexLitUntextured`, selected by the exact SAMPLE-002 declaration test in `EasyGLRenderer::SelectProgram()`; `easygl_basiceffect_position_normal_test`. **Expected:** a `lit_untextured3d` WGSL pair (per-pixel and per-vertex variants, to match `PreferPerPixelLighting`) reusing the existing `LitLightParams` UBO, driven from the declaration rather than the stride. **Depends on:** `WEBGPU-155`. **Tests:** port `easygl_basiceffect_position_normal_test`'s oracle; assert the lit gradient, not just non-black. **Acceptance:** the same mesh renders on both renderers within the parity corpus tolerance (`WEBGPU-193`). **Delivered 2026-09-04 -- and it needed NO new shader.** The audit expected a `lit_untextured3d` WGSL pair. Once `WEBGPU-155`'s neutral record exists, a `Position+Normal` declaration binds the existing lit family with TEXCOORD0 (and COLOR0) defaulted, and the `textureEnabled` uniform the shader already reads is 0 for an untextured `BasicEffect`, so the sample is white and the lighting is the real one. Two shapes therefore collapsed into one (`StockVertexShapeEXT::Lit` / `LitVertexLit` cover stride 24, 32 and 36), which is less code than the row asked for rather than more. **Verified:** shared fixture `parity_lit_untextured` ports `easygl_basiceffect_position_normal_test`'s oracle -- one directional light down -Z, red diffuse, no ambient/emissive/specular -- with the light's diffuse deliberately 0.6 rather than 1.0, so a lit surface (153,0,0) cannot be confused with an unlit one carrying the same red `DiffuseColor` (255,0,0). Three legs: lit at stride 24, normal reversed (black), and the same semantics padded to stride 32. 5/5 on both renderers, max diff 0. **EasyGL defect found and NOT cloned:** a fourth leg -- the same two elements declared in the other ORDER -- renders correctly on WebGPU (153,0,0) and *incorrectly* on the reference (255,0,0, its unlit `DiffuseColor`). `EasyGLRenderer::SelectStockProgramShape` detects a lit stride-24 vertex by an exact pattern match (`declaredElements[0] == Position@0 && declaredElements[1] == Normal@12`), so any other order falls through to the unlit colour program and the normal is dropped. Measured, not inferred. The leg is left OUT of the shared fixture -- with a comment saying why, so it is not "helpfully" re-added -- because a shared fixture asserts what both renderers must satisfy; fixing EasyGL's selection belongs to REMED-GFX-217, not here. |
| WEBGPU-157 | Stride-36 `Position+Normal+Color+TextureCoordinate` lit vertices — the stock `ModelProcessor`'s output. | ✅ | **Missing behaviour:** stride 36 is not in the canonical table at all, so `InferredLayoutForStride` abstains, every stride branch in `DrawPrimitivesEx` misses, and the draw falls through to `DrawColoredPrimitives()`, which throws for any stride but 16. **EasyGL evidence:** `plan_fx.md` FX-125 — XNA's stock `ModelProcessor` emits exactly this vertex for a mesh with a colour channel and sets `BasicEffect.VertexColorEnabled`; EasyGL's `kLitColor` inputs and its `stride == 36 && params.lightingEnabled` branch render it lit (SAMPLE-047's `Sphere01` was a flat green disc before that fix). **Expected:** the lit WGSL families gain a colour attribute, bound by semantic. **Depends on:** `WEBGPU-155`, and shares the shader change with `WEBGPU-156`. **Tests:** a lit sphere/quad with a colour channel asserting the shaded gradient *and* the tint; a discriminating A/B with `VertexColorEnabled` off. **Acceptance:** a real XNB model with vertex colours renders lit on WebGPU. **Delivered 2026-09-04.** Both lit WGSL families gained `@location(3) color: vec4f` and a `tint` varying, and multiply the vertex colour into the diffuse result BEFORE the specular term is added -- XNA's `VSBasicVertexLightingVc` ordering, matching the reference renderer's own `FragColor = texture * vec4(litRGB, alpha) * vc; FragColor.rgb += specularRGB * FragColor.a`. The colour is gated by the `vertexColorEnabled` uniform the block already carried, and a declaration that names no COLOR0 leaves it on the neutral record, so every pre-existing lit draw is byte-identical. Stride 36 needs no branch of its own: it is simply a declaration that names all four semantics. **Verified:** shared fixture `parity_lit_vertex_color` draws the stock `ModelProcessor` vertex (Position@0, Normal@12, Color@24, TexCoord@28, stride 36) three ways -- `VertexColorEnabled` true (153,77,38), false (153,153,153), and the normal reversed (0,0,0) -- with the false column as the discriminating A/B a renderer that always multiplied would fail. 6/6 on both renderers, max diff 0. |
| WEBGPU-158 | A stride-32 vertex that declares **no** Normal must render unlit and keep its declared colour. | ✅ | **Missing behaviour:** WebGPU maps stride 32 unconditionally to `lit_textured3d` (Position/Normal/TexCoord). A Position+Colour vertex padded to 32 bytes therefore fails `RequireFaithfulDeclarationEXT`. **EasyGL evidence:** REMED-GFX-234 — EasyGL asks the declaration (`DeclarationNamesUsage(..., Normal)`) and falls back to `StockProgramShape::Colored`; before that fix the declared colour was silently dropped. **Expected:** the same question, asked from the declaration. **Depends on:** `WEBGPU-155`. **Tests:** the REMED-GFX-234 shape — a padded Position+Colour stride-32 buffer must render its colour, and a genuine `VertexPositionNormalTexture` must still light. **Acceptance:** both, in one test file. **Delivered 2026-09-04, by `WEBGPU-155` alone -- no further code.** `SelectStockVertexShapeEXT` asks `DeclarationNamesUsageEXT(..., Normal)`, so a Position+Colour record padded to 32 bytes selects the unlit colour family and a real `VertexPositionNormalTexture` at the same stride still selects the lit one. **Verified:** shared fixture `parity_unlit_position_color` puts both in one frame with lighting ENABLED for both columns -- the point being that the declaration, not the effect state and not the stride, decides whether a vertex can be lit -- and with the light facing away, so the lit column is black (0,0,0) and the unlit one keeps its declared (40,200,120). 4/4 on both renderers, max diff 0. |
| WEBGPU-159 | `DualTextureEffect` must consume `TEXCOORD0` and `TEXCOORD1` independently. | ✅ | **Missing behaviour:** `kDualTexture`/`kDualTextureColored` in `webgpu_shaders.hpp` declare a single `@location(1) uv` and sample **both** textures with it; there is no `TEXCOORD1` input, and the canonical `PositionNormalDualTexture` vertex (stride 40) is an unlisted stride, so such a draw is refused outright. **EasyGL evidence:** `kDualTextured = {kPos, kUv, kUv1}` / `kDualTexturedColored = {kPos, kColor, kUv, kUv1}`; `easygl_dualtextureeffect_independent_uv_test` (SAMPLE-073) draws a stride-40 `PositionNormalDualTexture` and asserts the two layers use different UV sets. XNA's `DualTextureEffect` is defined this way (lightmapping). **Expected:** a second UV attribute in both dual-texture WGSL families, bound by semantic; when the declaration names no `TEXCOORD1`, match whatever EasyGL's unbound-attribute behaviour actually is — **measure it first** and record the answer in the row rather than assuming. **Depends on:** `WEBGPU-155`. **Tests:** port SAMPLE-073's oracle; add the one-UV case with the measured expectation. **Acceptance:** the two layers can be scrolled independently. **Delivered 2026-09-04.** Both dual-texture WGSL families gained a `uv1` input at their own `kUv1` location and sample `tex1` with it. The stride-40 canonical `PositionNormalDualTexture` that this route used to refuse outright now simply resolves. **The unbound-TEXCOORD1 answer, MEASURED as the row required:** `(0,0)` -- the shared fixture `parity_dual_texture_uv1` asserts it under both renderers and both agree exactly. That is the neutral record's `(0,0,0,1)` on WebGPU and the disabled generic vertex attribute on EasyGL, i.e. the same value from two independent mechanisms, and it is D3D9's rule for a missing vertex register. **Verified:** three columns sharing one TEXCOORD0 -- overlay texel 1 (blue), overlay texel 0 (green), and a declaration with no TEXCOORD1 whose TEXCOORD0 points at the OTHER texel so a renderer reusing TEXCOORD0 would fail rather than pass by luck. 5/5 on both renderers, max diff 0. **One existing test's premise was invalidated and repaired, not suppressed:** `stock_effect_sampler_contract_test.cpp`'s M3 asserted that slot 1's sampler is observable through a mesh that declares no TEXCOORD1. It cannot be -- Texture2 is then sampled at one constant coordinate -- and the REFERENCE renderer was already failing it (`EasyGL_StockEffectSamplerContract` 64/65 before this task, measured). M3 now draws a real stride-28 dual-UV quad, with a named skip for a renderer whose native layout cannot express one. Both renderers: 65/65. |
| WEBGPU-160 | `SamplerState.AddressW` must reach the sampler. | 🟨 | **Missing behaviour:** `IGraphicsRenderer::ApplySamplerAddressW` is not overridden, so the value is discarded silently — including for `Texture3D` sampling, where it is the only axis that matters. **EasyGL evidence:** `EasyGLRenderer::ApplySamplerAddressW()` writes `GL_TEXTURE_WRAP_R` on every non-ES2 profile (`plan_fx.md` FX-092). **Expected:** store per slot alongside `SlotSamplerState` and set `WGPUSamplerDescriptor::addressModeW`, using the same `TextureAddressMode` table `addressU`/`addressV` already use. **Source:** `WebGPURenderer.cpp` `ApplySamplerState`, `slotSamplers_`, the sampler-descriptor builders at ~834 and ~5038/6235. **Tests:** a `Texture3D` sampled past [0,1] in W under Wrap/Clamp/Mirror, with distinct expected texels per mode. **Acceptance:** three modes, three different readbacks. **Implemented and structurally verified 2026-09-05; 🟨 because the row's own acceptance cannot be met on this renderer yet, and that is a fact about the renderer rather than about the fix.** **Done:** `SlotSamplerState` carries `addressW`, `ApplySamplerAddressW()` is overridden, `FillWGPUSamplerDescriptor()` sets `WGPUSamplerDescriptor::addressModeW` through the SAME `ToAddressMode` ordinal table `addressU`/`addressV` already use (it was hardcoded to `ClampToEdge`), all ten draw-command families capture it beside `addressU`/`addressV`, and every one of the thirteen `GetOrCreateSlotSampler` call sites passes it — including the two SpriteBatch ones and the second slot of the dual-texture and env-map families. **`ApplySamplerState` deliberately does NOT reset W.** The interface splits the two calls so a renderer can adopt the W axis independently, and `GraphicsDevice::applySamplerStatesToRenderer()` drives them separately; clearing W from the other setter would make the surviving value depend on the order the two arrive in. There is a test for exactly that. **Two bugs the new test caught while it was being written, both mine, both real:** the 32-bit sampler cache key was EXACTLY full (filter, U, V, anisotropy, one byte each), so the key had to widen to 64-bit or a fifth field would have aliased two different samplers onto one entry — and widening the key computation without widening `slotSamplerCache_`'s own `std::uint32_t` key type silently TRUNCATED W off the top, so three distinct keys hit one entry and the first sampler was handed to all three. The renderer's `CNA_WEBGPU_SAMPLER_TRACE` output is what showed it: three different keys, three `HIT`s, one pointer. **WHY 🟨 — the named blocker.** `AddressW` is observable in PIXELS only where a renderer samples a VOLUME texture, and this renderer has no public route that does: `IEffectRenderer::BindTexture3D` is unimplemented on WebGPU, and extending `ShaderEffect`'s volume binding is explicitly outside this phase (see the out-of-scope table). So "three modes, three different readbacks" is not reachable here, and calling the row ✅ on a structural test alone would misreport it. **What remains:** the readback acceptance, once a volume-sampling route exists. Nothing else — the state is correct and will be correct the moment one does. **Verified:** `WebGpuSamplerAddressWTests.cpp` — three draws differing ONLY in `AddressW` create three distinct native samplers (`0 -> 3`), repeating all three creates none (`3 -> 3`, which is what would catch a key that aliases), and returning to an earlier state after changing only the filter reuses its sampler (which is what would catch `ApplySamplerState` resetting W). 14/14 sampler/Texture3D/readback/SpriteBatch CTests, 114/114 related gtests. |
| WEBGPU-161 | `SamplerState.MaxMipLevel` must reach the sampler. | ✅ | **Missing behaviour:** `IGraphicsRenderer::ApplySamplerMipState` is not overridden, so `MaxMipLevel` and `MipMapLevelOfDetailBias` are both discarded in silence. **EasyGL evidence:** `EasyGLRenderer::ApplySamplerMipState()` maps `MaxMipLevel`→`GL_TEXTURE_MIN_LOD`, the same mapping FNA3D's SDL_GPU driver makes with `min_lod`. **Expected:** override `ApplySamplerMipState` and map `MaxMipLevel` → `WGPUSamplerDescriptor::lodMinClamp` on all 16 slots, through this renderer's existing sampler-cache path so a changed value produces a new sampler rather than mutating a live one. **Scope change 2026-09-04, second pass (C4):** the LOD-bias half has LEFT this row. It is `WEBGPU-205`, an implementation task. This row must not write "LOD bias is unsupported" into `docs/sampler-state-support.md`, `GetAdditionalLimitationsTextEXT()` or anywhere else — that sentence would be false. **Tests:** a mipped texture sampled with `MaxMipLevel` 0 vs 2 reads back different texels, authored as a shared fixture under `WEBGPU-207` so EasyGL judges the same source. **Acceptance:** `MaxMipLevel` changes WebGPU pixels, and the shared fixture agrees with EasyGL. **Delivered 2026-09-05.** `ApplySamplerMipState` is overridden and maps `MaxMipLevel` to `WGPUSamplerDescriptor::lodMinClamp` — XNA's `MaxMipLevel` is the highest-RESOLUTION level sampling may use, so it is a LOWER clamp on the level of detail, which is the same mapping the reference renderer makes to `GL_TEXTURE_MIN_LOD` and FNA3D's SDL_GPU driver to `min_lod`. It rides the same per-slot path `WEBGPU-160` built: stored in `SlotSamplerState`, captured by all ten draw-command families, passed at every `GetOrCreateSlotSampler` call site, and folded into the 64-bit cache key — so a changed value produces a NEW sampler rather than mutating a live one, which is what the row asked for. **The LOD-bias half is untouched and nothing here claims it is unsupported** (C4): `ApplySamplerMipState` takes `lodBias`, ignores it, and says in a comment that `WEBGPU-205` owns it as an IMPLEMENTATION task. No documentation, capability or limitation text was written. **Verified by a shared fixture, and the acceptance is met exactly.** `parity_sampler_max_mip_level` builds an 8x8 mip chain whose FOUR levels are each a flat, distinct colour — deliberately not what a real chain looks like, because a correct chain is nearly self-similar and a test built on one cannot tell level 1 from level 0 at all. The quad is drawn at 1:1 so the natural LOD is 0 and every difference is the clamp's; the filter is Point so a level is SELECTED rather than blended. `MaxMipLevel` 0 reads level 0 `(220,40,40)` and `MaxMipLevel` 2 reads level 2 `(50,80,230)` — on BOTH renderers, exactly, and `run-parity-fixture.sh` reports **max diff 0**. **One fixture bug found and fixed while writing it:** the quad was fed to a triangle STRIP in ring order (TL, BL, BR, TR), which draws `(TL,BL,BR)` and `(BL,BR,TR)` and leaves a quarter of the quad uncovered — both renderers reported a mean 75% of the way to the expected colour, which is what a quarter of clear background does. The same wrong order was corrected in `WebGpuSamplerAddressWTests.cpp` at the same time. **Suite:** 110/112 `WebGPU_` CTests (the two failures are the pre-existing `DescriptorCapacityContract`/`PointSamplingContract` pair proved unrelated under `WEBGPU-162`), 7/7 EasyGL parity, 48/48 sampler gtests. |
| WEBGPU-205 | `SamplerState.MipMapLevelOfDetailBias` by shader emulation, with a stated route boundary. | ✅ | **Reclassified 2026-09-04, second pass (C4):** the audit accepted the absence of a `lodBias` member in `WGPUSamplerDescriptor` as proof the state must stay unsupported. That is the same argument it had just disproved for `FillMode::WireFrame` — an absent *state field* is not an absent *capability*. Confirmed field-by-field in the pin: the descriptor carries `addressModeU/V/W`, `magFilter`, `minFilter`, `mipmapFilter`, `lodMinClamp`, `lodMaxClamp`, `compare`, `maxAnisotropy` and nothing else. **What can express it:** WGSL's `textureSampleBias(t, s, coords, bias)` applies exactly XNA's semantic — a bias added to the computed level of detail — in the fragment stage, which is where every one of this renderer's sampling calls already sits (`webgpu_shaders.hpp` lines 34, 110, 154, 218, 335, 382, 434, 484-485, 531-532, 601, 617, 718, 854-917). **Expected:** carry the per-slot bias in the existing per-draw uniform block, swap `textureSample` for `textureSampleBias` on the routes that take it, and cover: every stock effect (Basic, AlphaTest, DualTexture — both samplers — EnvironmentMap, Skinned), `SpriteBatch`, and compiled effects once `WEBGPU-204` selects a route (the translation layer inherits the same uniform). **Route boundary to state truthfully, not to hide behind:** a custom CNAEXT `ShaderEffect` supplies its own WGSL and is out of this phase's scope; a route that cannot apply the bias must say so by name through `GetAdditionalLimitationsTextEXT()` — no route may silently ignore the state. Note WGSL clamps bias to roughly [-16, +16); state the clamp rather than pretending it is unbounded. Note also that EasyGL implements this only on desktop core (`GL_TEXTURE_LOD_BIAS` does not exist in ES), so a complete WebGPU emulation is *broader* than the reference — compare against the desktop-core EasyGL profile. **Depends on:** `WEBGPU-161` for the sampler plumbing. **Tests:** a mipped checker sampled at a fixed scale with bias 0 / +1 / -1 must read back three different results, as a shared fixture (`WEBGPU-207`), plus one per non-stock route. **Acceptance:** the bias changes pixels on every in-scope route, or the route is named as unable to. A permanent limitation needs a reason no shader can express the semantic — not the absence of a sampler field. **Implemented on every stock 3D route 2026-09-05; the SpriteBatch half closed 2026-09-06 by measuring it, and the answer was not the one this row predicted.** **C4 vindicated, and measured rather than argued.** The audit read the absence of a `lodBias` member in `WGPUSamplerDescriptor` as proof the state must stay unsupported; that is the same reasoning already disproved for `FillMode::WireFrame`. WGSL `textureSampleBias` applies XNA's semantic exactly, and the shared fixture now shows three biases reading three different mip levels on WebGPU, **identical to the reference renderer**. **How:** the per-slot bias is stored beside `MaxMipLevel` in `SlotSamplerState`, travels in the primary per-draw uniform block — widened 160→176 bytes for a 16-byte bias tail, exactly as `WEBGPU-149` widened it for fog, because the 40-float block was full — and every stock sampling call became `textureSampleBias(..., u.samplerBias.x)`, with `.y` for the SECOND sampler of the dual-texture and environment-map families. Covered: `BasicEffect` (textured, coloured, lit per-pixel and vertex-lit), `AlphaTestEffect` (both variants), `DualTextureEffect` (both samplers, both variants), `EnvironmentMapEffect` (base texture and cube) and `SkinnedEffect` (all four variants) — 14 sampling calls across 14 shader families. **The clamp is stated, not hidden:** WGSL clamps a sample bias to roughly [-16, +16), so the value is clamped on the way in and the range is written in `docs/sampler-state-support.md` and in `GetAdditionalLimitationsTextEXT()`. **The route boundary is named, as the row demands — no route ignores the state silently.** `GetAdditionalLimitationsTextEXT()` now names the three that do not apply it: `SpriteBatch`, a custom CNAEXT `ShaderEffect` (its own WGSL, its own sampling calls), and `PbrEffect`/`SkinnedPbrEffect` (the glTF route, not an XNA stock effect). **The SpriteBatch half: the blocker this row named was one layer too low, and the real one is not in this renderer.** The row recorded the obstacle as "the stock sprite pipeline binds no uniform buffer, so the bias has no channel to travel in", and weighed a transient UBO per sprite against a per-vertex float. Both would have been wasted: **the value never reaches the renderer at all.** `SpriteBatch::Begin` applies the blend, depth-stencil and rasterizer states through the `GraphicsDevice` properties, exactly as FNA does, but routes the sampler AROUND the device into `ISpriteBatchRenderer::SetSamplerFilter(int)` and `SetSamplerAddressMode(int, int)` — which is the entire sampler channel that interface has. `MipMapLevelOfDetailBias`, `MaxMipLevel`, `MaxAnisotropy` and `AddressW` stop in `modules/graphics/src/Xna/SpriteBatch.cpp`. XNA does not narrow it that way: `SpriteBatch.PrepRenderState` assigns `GraphicsDevice.SamplerStates[0] = samplerState` (FNA `SpriteBatch.cs:1426`) and all six fields apply. **Measured on both renderers rather than read off the interface,** because an interface can always be circumvented and the question is what pixels do: the new shared fixture `parity_sprite_sampler_state` draws a 256x256 mip chain of flat distinct colours into a 64x64 cell — minified 4x, so the natural level is 2 and a bias has room to move in both directions — under batch biases 0, +1 and -2.
