All four sprite columns read level 2 on WEBGPU **and on OPENGL33**, while the fixture's own 3D control row (same texture, same scale, same three biases through the device) reads levels 2, 3 and 0. 10/10 on both renderers, frames byte-identical (`run-parity-fixture.sh`, max diff 0). **A leak that would have made the two renderers disagree was probed and ruled out:** EasyGL applies the bias to a GL sampler OBJECT that outlives a draw, so a preceding 3D draw's bias could have survived into a following batch there and not here; it does not, because `EasyGLRenderer::ApplySamplerState` writes `GL_TEXTURE_LOD_BIAS = 0` unconditionally on desktop core. Checked by temporarily giving the fixture's last control column a bias of +1 and re-running both. **So the route boundary this row demands is stated with its true cause, and the defect is recorded upstream rather than worked around here** — `plans/plan_graphics.md` row **1119**. Reading `slotSamplers_[0].lodBias` at sprite enqueue was considered and rejected: that is the DEVICE's sampler state, not the batch's, and applying it would invent a value the batch never asked for while diverging from every other renderer. `GetAdditionalLimitationsTextEXT()` names `SpriteBatch` with the framework reason, not a WebGPU one. **Verified:** shared fixture `parity_sampler_lod_bias` — a 64x64 mip chain whose levels are flat distinct colours, drawn so the natural LOD is ~0 (which is load-bearing: a bias is ADDED to the computed LOD, so a magnified quad keeps it negative and the test would measure nothing — that mistake was made and caught here). bias 0 → level 0, +1 → level 1, +2 → level 2, and −1 clamps to level 0 rather than wrapping. 7/7 on BOTH renderers, `run-parity-fixture.sh` max diff 0. **One bug the work surfaced:** `EnvMapParams` grew a sixteenth `vec4f` and its bind-group layout still declared `minBindingSize = 240`, which is a pipeline-creation error — caught by `WebGPU_ShaderValidation`/`WebGPU_EnvMap3D` rather than by a wrong picture. **Suite:** 111/113 `WebGPU_` CTests on a FULL build, the two failures being the pre-existing `DescriptorCapacityContract`/`PointSamplingContract` pair. |
| WEBGPU-162 | Push the **physical** presentation rectangle, not the logical one, after a resize or mode change. | 🟨 | **Missing behaviour:** `GetDefaultViewportRect()` is inherited, so it returns `(0, 0, GetViewportSize())` — and WebGPU's `GetViewportSize()` returns `ComputeLogicalViewport().logicalWidth/Height`. `GraphicsDevice::UpdateViewportFromWindow()` then calls `SetViewport(0, 0, logicalW, logicalH)`, and `ApplyDrawViewport()` uses that as the native rectangle. Under the default `FixedHeightDynamicWidth` (or Letterbox/Overscan) after a window resize, 3D draws are placed in a logical-sized rectangle at the **window origin** instead of the computed, centred, scaled rectangle; sprites additionally take the `customViewport` branch because that rectangle *is* a contained sub-region. **EasyGL evidence:** `EasyGLSurfaceState::GetDefaultViewportRect()` exists solely because the inherited default "was actively wrong here" — a real report (galaxy-eggbert, 2026-08-21: resizing or going fullscreen did not enlarge the game). `OpenGL2Renderer` and `SdlGpuRenderer` do the same. **Expected:** override `GetDefaultViewportRect()` to return `ComputeLogicalViewport()`'s `x/y/width/height` (the physical rect), leaving `GetViewportSize()` logical. **Source:** `WebGPURenderer.cpp` `ComputeLogicalViewport`, `GetViewportSize`, `SetViewport`, `ApplyDrawViewport`, and the `viewportIsContainedSubRegion` guard added by `WEBGPU-141`(A), which should be re-examined once the default rectangle is physical. **Tests:** a `WebGPU_BackbufferResize` example mirroring `easygl_backbuffer_resize_test`: clear + one 3D draw + one sprite at 800×600, resize to 1200×800, assert the drawn content fills the letterboxed rectangle and the bars stay clear-coloured. **Acceptance:** the resized frame is not a corner-rendered miniature; `WEBGPU-141`'s Wrap/Mirror readback checks still pass. **ATTEMPTED AND WITHDRAWN 2026-09-05. The four-line override this row describes is not sufficient, and shipping it regressed real behaviour.** **What was tried:** `WebGPURenderer::GetDefaultViewportRect()` overridden to return `ComputeLogicalViewport()`'s `x/y/width/height`, exactly as the row specifies. The rectangle MATHS is right and was proved renderer-neutrally — see the test below — but the renderer as a whole does not agree with it. **Why it was withdrawn, MEASURED not assumed.** WebGPU's sprite and 3D paths treat `GraphicsDevice.Viewport` as a LOGICAL rectangle and project into it directly, so making the default rectangle physical moves every draw. Traced on an 800x480 drawable with a 96x72 virtual resolution under `FixedHeightDynamicWidth`: logical `120x72`, physical rect `(0,0,800x480)`; a sprite the caller placed at logical `(24,15)` landed at physical `(160,100)`, outside the region the caller reads back. `WebGPU_SpriteBatch_CustomViewport` (Check A0) and `WebGPU_BackbufferFirstRead` both saw an empty frame. Confirmed by A/B: neutralising the override and rebuilding turned both green again, and restoring it turned both red. **The real scope, which this row must now carry:** the override cannot land until the consumers stop treating the viewport as logical — the sprite path's `customViewport` branch, the 3D `ApplyDrawViewport`, and the coordinate space `GetBackBufferData` reads in. That is precisely what `WEBGPU-141`(A)'s `viewportIsContainedSubRegion` guard was compensating for, so the guard and this row have to be settled together rather than one after the other. **Kept from the attempt:** the renderer-neutral `modules/graphics/tests/CNA/Internal/Renderers/Common/PresentationRectangleTests.cpp` states the contract for every renderer that has a virtual resolution and measures the physical size from the renderer itself rather than assuming it. It passes 5/5 on `OPENGL33`, which implements the contract correctly (Letterbox `(0,80,800x320)`, Overscan `(-200,0,1200x480)` on an 800x480 drawable). WebGPU is excluded from its renderer set with that reason written at the exclusion, so the test is a live specification of what closing this row means rather than a record of a failed attempt. **A process finding that matters more than this row:** the regression was invisible for two tasks because `cmake --build --target <specific>` leaves every OTHER test binary linked against the previous renderer, so `ctest` re-ran STALE executables and reported them green. A full build before a suite run is not optional after a renderer change — `WEBGPU-153`'s own stale arm in `webgpu_graphicsstate_test.cpp` was hidden the same way. |
| WEBGPU-163 | Stop promising a BC format for a `TextureCube` the cube path cannot store — interim, superseded by `WEBGPU-206`. | ✅ | **Missing behaviour:** `WebGPURenderer::ClassifySurfaceFormatEXT()` answers `Supported` for `Dxt1/3/5/Dxt5Srgb/Bc7/Bc7Srgb` whenever the device enabled the BC feature, but that one classifier serves **both** `Texture2D` and `TextureCube`. `WebGPURenderer::CreateTextureCube(size, mipMap, /*surfaceFormat*/)` discards the format, `WebGPUTextureCubeRenderer`'s constructor takes only `(owner, size, mipMap)` and is RGBA8-only, and `IsCompressedCubeTransferFormatEXT()` is not overridden (false). So `TextureCube(device, size, mipMap, SurfaceFormat::Dxt1)` **constructs**, and every `SetData` on it throws. **Expected (this row — the interim safe state, explicitly NOT the parity answer):** classify per resource kind so a cube asked for a BC format is refused at construction, by name, with the same verdict `GraphicsDevice` would report; and fix the content route, where `KeepCompressedOnLoadEXT` consults the **2D** `IsCompressedTransferFormatEXT` and can hand a cube loader blocks it cannot store. **Scope change 2026-09-04, second pass (C5):** the audit offered "implement compressed cube support **or** refuse honestly" as equivalent closures. They are not equivalent under this phase's parity rule — a BC cube is a six-layer 2D texture plus a `WGPUTextureViewDimension_Cube` view, and nothing in `WGPUFeatureName_TextureCompressionBC` restricts it — so refusal is a stopgap and `WEBGPU-206` is the parity state. This row may never be cited as a platform limitation. **Tests:** constructing a `Dxt1` `TextureCube` throws at construction and `ClassifySurfaceFormatEXT`'s cube verdict matches. **Acceptance:** construction and use agree; nothing is promised at construction and refused at use. Closing this row does **not** close the family — `WEBGPU-206` does. **Done 2026-09-05.** `IGraphicsRenderer` gained `ClassifyTextureCubeFormatEXT(int)`, defaulting to `ClassifySurfaceFormatEXT(surfaceFormat)` so no renderer's behaviour moved by adding it, and `TextureCube::ValidateTextureCubeFormatEXT` now asks it instead of the 2D classifier -- the split by resource kind the row asked for, made at the seam rather than inside one renderer. `WebGPURenderer` overrides it to `Unsupported` for anything `ClassifyWebGPUTextureFormat` reports as block-compressed, so all six BC formats are refused at construction, by name (`"SurfaceFormat 4 is not supported for a cube texture by the active renderer, even where the same format is supported for a Texture2D"`), and the message says the 2D route is unaffected so a caller can fall back deliberately. **The content-route half of this row did not exist.** Re-measured rather than assumed: `TextureCubeContentTypeReader.cpp:51-57` already gates `keepCompressed` on `IsCompressedCubeTransferFormatEXT`, not the 2D query, and `TextureCube::DDSFromStreamEXT` decodes to `SurfaceFormat::Color` unconditionally; `KeepCompressedOnLoadEXT` has exactly one caller (`Texture2D.cpp:2238`) and never reaches a cube. Nothing changed there. **Test:** `modules/graphics/tests/.../TextureCubeCompressedFormatAgreementTests.cpp`, a renderer-neutral invariant rather than one renderer's answer -- *if a `TextureCube` constructs with a block-compressed format, a compressed `SetData` on it must not then be refused for lack of a route*. Both outcomes are legal, so the same test passes on both renderers while measuring opposite arms: WEBGPU refuses all six at construction; OPENGL33 constructs `Dxt1`/`Dxt3`/`Dxt5` and **accepts** the upload (the success arm is genuinely exercised, not two renderers both doing nothing) and refuses `Dxt5SrgbEXT`/`Bc7EXT`/`Bc7SrgbEXT`, which it does not classify. **Negative control:** with the override reverted to the pre-fix single classifier and rebuilt, the test fails on WEBGPU for all six -- "constructed ... and then refused its compressed upload" -- so it measures the defect and not the fix. Note this hardware has `WGPUFeatureName_TextureCompressionBC`, which is what made the false promise reachable here at all. **Left alone deliberately:** the two refusal sites disagree on exception type (`System::NotSupportedException` from the verdict gate, `std::runtime_error` from `Texture::ValidateFormat` for a deferred format). Recorded in the test; out of this row's scope. Still interim -- `WEBGPU-206` is the parity state and this row is not citable as a platform limitation. |

### Phase 70.1 — P1: major XNA compatibility families

| # | Task | Status | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| WEBGPU-164 | `RenderTarget2D(mipMap: true)` — regenerate the mip chain instead of throwing. | ✅ | **Missing behaviour:** `WebGPURenderer::CreateRenderTarget2D()` throws `std::runtime_error` for `mipMap == true`. The throw is deliberate and honest (the XNA layer has already called `CalculateMipLevels`), but it is a hard refusal of a normal XNA constructor. **EasyGL evidence:** `EasyGLRenderTargetRenderer` pre-allocates every level and regenerates the chain from level 0 on unbind, mirroring FNA3D's `OPENGL_ResolveTarget`; `easygl_rendertarget2d_mip_test`. **Expected:** the same on-unbind timing, reusing the `GenerateMipsForLayer()` render-pass downsample cascade `WEBGPU-52`/`114` already built (the cube target already does exactly this per face). Compose with MSAA the way the cube target does: resolve writes level 0, then the cascade runs. **Source:** `WebGPURenderer.cpp` `CreateRenderTarget2D`, `WebGPURenderTargetRenderer`, `RenderPendingDrawsToRenderTarget`, `GenerateMipsForLayer`. **Depends on:** nothing. **Tests:** render into a mipped target, sample it at level 0 and level 2 through `SpriteBatch`/`BasicEffect`, and `GetData` both levels. **Acceptance:** `mipMap=true` no longer throws and `Texture2D::GetData(level>0)` on the target returns filtered content. Fix `docs/webgpu-renderer.md`'s limitations list in the same commit (see `WEBGPU-194`). **Done 2026-09-05.** `WebGPURenderTargetRenderer` allocates the chain `CalculateMipLevels` already declared and `RenderPendingDrawsToRenderTarget` regenerates levels 1.. from level 0 after its submit -- the unbind timing the row asked for, reusing `GenerateMipsForLayer` exactly as the cube target does, so no new machinery. MSAA composes for free: the resolve writes level 0 and the cascade then reads it. **Two structural details:** the colour texture now needs TWO views -- `colorView_` spanning the chain for SAMPLING and a new `colorLevel0View_` naming one level for the render-pass attachment and the MSAA resolve target, because a colour attachment may name only one mip level -- and `GetData(level)` reads that level's own dimensions, `bytesPerRow` and copy extent rather than the target's. A level outside the chain still throws, by name, which is the property the old blanket refusal was protecting. **A latent bug fell out of it, in shared code:** `EnsureMipBlitPipeline` caches BY FORMAT but built its fragment state with `InitStockColorTargetsEXT`, i.e. the attachment count of whatever MRT set was bound at first creation. A two-target set therefore baked a two-target blit pipeline that every later single-attachment blit then reused -- "the RenderPass uses formats [Bgra8Unorm] but the RenderPipeline uses [Bgra8Unorm, Bgra8Unorm]", an abort. Unreachable until a mipped target could join an MRT set, which is what this row made possible. Now always exactly one target, which is what a blit pass has by construction. **A second latent bug, in render-target LIFETIME, surfaced the same way.** `~WebGPURenderTargetRenderer` cleared `currentRenderTarget_` when the dying target was the bound one but left `mrtExtraTargets_` holding the SURVIVING slots of a set whose slot 0 no longer existed: the queued draws were dropped (the survivor read back transparent black) and the next flush would have built an N-attachment pass around a null slot 0. The destructor now flushes the set FIRST -- while every attachment the pass names is still alive -- and then drops the whole binding rather than half of it. Reachable only through a stack-allocated target's destructor, which bypasses `RenderTarget2D::Dispose()`'s bound-target refusal, and only in a leg that needs a MIPPED survivor -- which this renderer refused to create at all. Found by `bound_target_lifetime_test` leg L1, which had been taking its declared-boundary exit since the leg was written. **And with it:** mip regeneration now runs for EVERY slot of an MRT set, not only slot 0. A mipped target in slot 1 is as written an attachment as slot 0 is, and leaving its chain stale would make what a later sample reads depend on which slot the game bound it in. **Six stale declarations deleted rather than left describing a boundary that no longer exists:** `kMipMappedTargetSupported` in `rendertarget_msaa_mip_readback_test.cpp`, `rendertarget_msaa_depth_contract_test.cpp`, `rendertarget_invalid_mip_level_test.cpp` and `rendertarget_msaa_first_readback_test.cpp`, and `kMipmappedRenderTargetSupported` in `rendertarget_first_use_test.cpp` and `rendertarget_effect_source_test.cpp`, each of which had a `#if defined(CNA_RENDERER_WEBGPU)` false arm. Two of them mattered beyond tidiness: their helpers SKIP the render step when the flag is false, so a target that now constructs was returned unpainted and every later assertion measured an empty frame. A stale declaration is not inert. Their legs now measure content on this renderer instead of measuring the shape of a refusal. `WebGPU_RenderTarget2D` Check G was rewritten the same way -- it asserted the throw, and now paints a 32x32 target in two halves and asserts level 4 keeps both. **Tests:** the row's own list, as a shared fixture rather than a WebGPU-only one -- new `render_target_mip` parity fixture (`WEBGPU-207`): a 64x64 target painted in four far-apart quadrants whose mean appears nowhere in level 0, `GetData` asserted at BOTH ends of the chain (level 0 returns the drawn quadrant; the 1x1 level returns the regenerated mean), then the target sampled back through `BasicEffect` twice, unrestricted and pinned to its coarsest level via `MaxMipLevel`. **EasyGL and WebGPU frames are byte-identical, max diff 0** -- `glGenerateMipmap`'s box filter and this renderer's render-pass downsample cascade agree exactly on this content. All 21 `WebGPU_RenderTarget*`/`Msaa*`/`Mip*`/`MRT`/`BoundTargetLifetime` CTests pass. |
| WEBGPU-197 | Make the render pipeline key carry the **bound colour target's format and sample count**, not the renderer-global pair. | ✅ | **Why this is the root:** a WebGPU render pipeline bakes `WGPUColorTargetState.format` and `WGPUMultisampleState.count`. All twelve of this renderer's pipeline families read the swap-chain `surfaceFormat_` and one global `sampleCount_`, which is why `CreateRenderTarget2D` cannot honour a per-target sample count (`WEBGPU-165`) and why no non-`Color` target can ever be drawn into (`WEBGPU-198`–`202`). Both families are the same missing dimension. **Expected:** extend `Make3DPipelineKey` (and the SpriteBatch equivalent) with the currently-bound target's colour format and sample count; resolve those from the bound `WebGPURenderTargetRenderer`/`WebGPURenderTargetCubeRenderer`/MRT set, falling back to the backbuffer's pair when none is bound. Keep `ClearAllPipelineCaches()`'s existing invalidation contract — `WEBGPU-58` recorded that reusing a shader module/bind-group layout/pipeline layout across a sample-count change silently renders wrong on this pin, so the key change must not quietly reintroduce that reuse. Vulkan's `RecordCommandBuffer` is the in-repo precedent for selecting a variant per bound target. **Watch:** cache growth — key by the small set of formats actually reachable, and record the resulting cache cardinality. **Blocks:** `WEBGPU-165`, `WEBGPU-198`–`202`. **Tests:** bind targets of two different formats and two different sample counts in one frame and confirm each draw lands correctly; assert the pipeline cache holds distinct entries. **Acceptance:** a draw into a non-backbuffer-format or non-global-sample-count target renders correctly, and the `WEBGPU-58` MSAA behaviour is unregressed. **Plumbed 2026-09-05; NOT yet usable by `WEBGPU-165`, and the reason is the hazard this row's own text warned about.** **Done:** `ReplayDrawsInOrder` now records the pass's colour format and sample count (`replayColorFormat_`, `replaySampleCount_`, and `replayMrtColorFormats_` for the extra slots) exactly as it already recorded `replayDepthFormat_`; `Build3DPipelineEXT` reads them instead of `surfaceFormat_`/`sampleCount_`, `InitStockColorTargetsEXT` gives each MRT slot its own format, and both are folded into `Make3DPipelineKey` at all twelve call sites. `PassDestination` already carried both values per target, so nothing new had to be threaded through the pass builders. The SpriteBatch path needed no change at all -- `WebGPUSpriteBlendSnapshot` has keyed on the bound target's format and sample count since it was written, and this is the 3D side catching up to it. **Verified non-regressive:** the whole `WebGPU` ctest label, 115 tests, passes with only the two long-standing XNA-pixel-centre failures. **Why this is 🟨 and not ✅: there is no observable case yet, and one hazard is now live.** (1) Every target this renderer can currently create has `colorFormat_ == surfaceFormat_` and mirrors the global sample count, so `replayColorFormat_`/`replaySampleCount_` are today always equal to the values they replaced. The change is real but unprovable until `WEBGPU-198`/`199` make a non-`Color` target creatable or `165` makes a per-target sample count possible -- marking it done on a passing regression run alone would be exactly the "green plan" this phase exists to avoid. (2) **The `WEBGPU-58` reuse hazard is now reachable, and `165` must not be attempted until it is re-measured.** That task recorded, empirically on this pin, that a `WGPUShaderModule`/`WGPUBindGroupLayout`/`WGPUPipelineLayout` already used for one pipeline and then reused UNCHANGED for a pipeline with a different `WGPUMultisampleState.count` silently renders wrong -- no validation error, correct-looking draw, wrong pixels -- which is why `ClearAllPipelineCaches()` tears down every module and layout when `sampleCount_` changes. Building per-pass sample-count variants from the same long-lived modules is precisely that reuse. It is harmless TODAY only because every pass in a frame shares one count. **Update 2026-09-05, same day:** (a) is DONE and the answer is negative -- `WebGPU_MsaaModuleReuseProbe` measures a max channel difference of **0** between a 4x frame from a reused module set and one from a fresh set, stable over repeated runs, so (b) is not needed. `165` landed on that basis and is this row's first observable case: two live targets at two sample counts in one frame, both property and edge pixels asserted. **What still keeps this row open is only its colour-format half:** every target this renderer can build still carries `surfaceFormat_`, so `replayColorFormat_` has yet to differ from the value it replaced. `WEBGPU-198`/`199` supply that, and this row closes with them plus the cache-cardinality measurement it asks for. **CLOSED 2026-09-05, once both halves had something to prove them.** **Sample count:** `WEBGPU-165` landed the same day -- two live `RenderTarget2D`s at two different counts in one frame, with the backbuffer at 4x, asserting the property AND the edge pixels in both directions (`webgpu_msaa_test` Check D, 7/7). The `WEBGPU-58` module-reuse hazard that gated it was re-measured and does not reproduce (`WebGPU_MsaaModuleReuseProbe`, max channel difference 0), so no module/layout keying was needed. **Colour format:** `WEBGPU-198` landed the same day and makes a non-`Color` target creatable -- `RenderTargetFormatAgreement` binds, clears, takes a stock 3D draw into, and samples back all six float formats this adapter reports. The 3D-draw leg is the one that measures THIS row: a pipeline built against the swap chain's format and used in a float target's pass is a hard native error on this pin, `"Render pipeline targets are incompatible with render pass"` -- demonstrated the same day by the mip-blit bug `WEBGPU-164` found -- so those eight draws landing is the key change working, not an absence of evidence. **Cache cardinality, as the row asked:** the whole `WebGPU` label (116 tests, including the instanced/viewport/scissor pipeline-variant CARDINALITY tests, which count variants rather than key values) is green, so the two new dimensions split no pipeline that was previously shared. Every draw in one pass sees the same pair, which is why they cannot. |
| WEBGPU-198 | Override `ClassifyRenderTargetFormatEXT` on WebGPU, answered by a device probe. | ✅ | **Missing behaviour:** `WebGPURenderer` does not override `ClassifyRenderTargetFormatEXT` at all, so every format falls through to `Texture::ValidateFormat`, which accepts `Color` alone; `RenderTarget2D(device, w, h, false, SurfaceFormat::HdrBlendable, …)` throws `std::runtime_error` from the shared layer and `GraphicsDevice::SupportsCapability(FloatRenderTargets/HalfFloatRenderTargets)` reports false. **Scope correction 2026-09-04, second pass (C1):** this whole family was excluded because `plan_modern.md`'s HDR pipeline consumes it. Consumers do not decide scope; `SurfaceFormat.hpp` does, and none of these enumerators carries an `EXT` suffix. **EasyGL evidence:** `ClassifyRenderTargetFormatEXT` + `MapRenderTargetColorFormat` + `ProbeFloatRenderTargetSupportEXT` — eight mapped formats, `Defer` for anything unmapped, `Unsupported` for a mapped format the live context cannot render, and `CreateRenderTarget2DEXT` throwing by name rather than substituting `Color` (`MOD-115`). **Expected:** the same three-part shape here — a format→`WGPUTextureFormat` map, a cached **probe** (create a 1×1 `WGPUTextureUsage_RenderAttachment` texture of the real format inside a `WGPUErrorFilter_Validation` scope, exactly as `Supports4xMsaa()` already does), and `CreateRenderTarget2DEXT`/`CreateRenderTargetCubeEXT` refusing an `Unsupported` format by name instead of dropping the argument as they do today. Probe; do not hard-code a spec/adapter matrix. **Depends on:** `WEBGPU-197`. **Blocks:** `199`, `200`, `201`. **Tests:** `SupportsSurfaceFormatAsRenderTargetEXT` and the `RenderTarget2D` constructor agree for every `SurfaceFormat` value, on this adapter, in one table-driven test. **Acceptance:** the verdict is the renderer's own and probed, and no format is accepted at construction that fails at allocation. **Done 2026-09-05.** The row's three-part shape, built exactly as it asks. **(1) The map:** `MapRenderTargetColorFormatEXT` carries the reference renderer's own eight-format set -- `Single`->R32Float, `Vector2`->RG32Float, `Vector4`->RGBA32Float, `HalfSingle`->R16Float, `HalfVector2`->RG16Float, `HalfVector4`/`HdrBlendable`->RGBA16Float. `Color` is deliberately NOT in the table: it maps to the live `surfaceFormat_`, and keeping it out is what leaves every existing `Color` target byte-identical to its pre-198 form. `Rgba64` is absent for a different reason -- **WebGPU has no 16-bit UNORM colour format at all**, so there is nothing to map it to; it falls to `Defer` and the framework refuses it, which settles `WEBGPU-201` in advance as a genuine platform absence rather than an unimplemented path. **(2) The probe, not a table:** `ProbeRenderTargetFormatEXT` creates a 1x1 render-attachment texture of the real native format inside a `WGPUErrorFilter_Validation` scope -- the same technique `Supports4xMsaa()` uses -- and caches the answer per format. **Measured on this adapter: all six float formats are renderable**, so WEBGPU reports 8 of 27 renderable against OPENGL33's 9 (which adds `Rgba64`). **(3) The refusal:** a new `CreateRenderTarget2DEXT` override refuses an `Unsupported` format by name and, crucially, ALLOCATES IN THE FORMAT ASKED FOR. `IGraphicsRenderer`'s default forwards to the format-less overload and drops the argument, which is precisely what would have let a target be classified in one format and allocated in another. **`WEBGPU-197`'s missing half is supplied by this row and now proven** -- see that row. **The typed readback came with it, because leaving it out WAS a promise/refuse split.** The first cut of this row refused `RenderTarget2D.GetData` on a float target by name and deferred the transfer to `WEBGPU-202`. The full suite disagreed: nine `HdrRenderTargetRoundTripTest` cases had been SKIPPING on `SupportsSurfaceFormatAsRenderTargetEXT`, started running the moment this row made float targets creatable, and five failed on that refusal -- a game could query the format, create the target, render HDR into it, and then not read it back. That is the shape `WEBGPU-163` was closed to prevent, so the readback was widened instead of the capability narrowed: `BytesPerTexelEXT` gives the width from the format (2/4/8/16) rather than a hardcoded 4, and the BGRA swizzle now applies to BGRA8 alone, since reordering a float texel's BYTES would corrupt it. All nine round-trip cases pass, including values above 1.0 surviving, a multisampled float target resolving without clamping, and a float target generating a mip chain -- which is `WEBGPU-164` and this row composing. **The cube path needed the same fix, for the same reason.** `CreateRenderTargetCubeEXT` was not overridden either, so a `RenderTargetCube(HdrBlendable)` reported the float format it was asked for while holding 8-bit texels -- MOD-107's silent substitution, in the one path image-based lighting depends on, and passing its test only because that test does not read values back. Both cube creation and its colour allocation now carry the requested format. **Tests:** `RenderTargetFormatAgreementTests.cpp`, renderer-neutral and four legs deep, each one closing a gap the previous leaves open. (a) the query and the constructor agree for **all 27** `SurfaceFormat` enumerators, both directions, and a target that constructs must report back the format it was asked for (so a silent `Color` substitution cannot pass); plus a non-degeneracy check, since answering the same way 27 times would satisfy agreement vacuously. (b) every format the query calls renderable also BINDS and clears -- allocation succeeding is not the same as a pass being buildable. (c) every one takes a stock 3D **draw**, which is what forces a pipeline to be built against the target's own colour format; a mismatch there is a hard native error on this pin, not a silent wrong result -- demonstrated the same day by the mip-blit pipeline bug `WEBGPU-164` found. (d) the pixel leg: a 16-bit float target cleared red is sampled back through `SpriteBatch` onto a `Color` target and read there, avoiding the typed readback entirely. **8/8 and 4/4 legs green on both renderers.** **One divergence recorded rather than encoded:** EasyGL samples an `R16Float` target back as (255,255,255) and `RG16Float` as (255,0,255), where WebGPU gives (255,0,0) for both -- GL broadcasting a one-channel texture against WGSL's `texture_2d<f32>` returning `(r,0,0,1)`. The leg asserts the RED channel only, which every format in the family carries, so it does not bake either renderer's swizzle in. **Which one XNA means is unsettled and is `WEBGPU-199`/`200`'s to decide.** |
| WEBGPU-199 | Half-float render targets: `HalfSingle`, `HalfVector2`, `HalfVector4`, `HdrBlendable`. | ✅ | **EasyGL evidence:** `HalfSingle`→R16F, `HalfVector2`→RG16F, `HalfVector4` and `HdrBlendable`→RGBA16F (`MapRenderTargetColorFormat`), the last pair sharing storage because XNA's `HdrBlendable` was RGBA16F on Windows — keep that equivalence explicit rather than inventing a third meaning. **WebGPU side:** `WGPUTextureFormat_R16Float` / `RG16Float` / `RGBA16Float` (webgpu.h 1034/1046/1065) are renderable, filterable and blendable in core WebGPU, so this is the cheaper half of the family and should land first. **Expected:** allocation at the requested format, clear with the right value type, draw into it through `WEBGPU-197`'s pipeline variant, sample it back through a stock effect, and `GetData` at the correct texel width (`WEBGPU-202`). `GraphicsCapability::HalfFloatRenderTargets` becomes true through the existing derived seam once `HdrBlendable` probes supported — do not add a second answer. **Depends on:** `WEBGPU-198`. **Tests:** a shared fixture (`WEBGPU-207`) writing values above 1.0 into an `HdrBlendable` target and reading them back unclamped — the property that distinguishes a real float target from a substituted `Color` one. **Acceptance:** all four formats allocate, render, sample and read back; the capability flips because the probe says so. **Done 2026-09-05.** Most of the row was delivered by `WEBGPU-198` the same day -- all four formats allocate at the requested format (`HalfSingle`->R16Float, `HalfVector2`->RG16Float, `HalfVector4` and `HdrBlendable` both ->RGBA16Float, the equivalence kept explicit), clear, take a stock 3D draw through `WEBGPU-197`'s pipeline variant, sample back, and `GetData` at the correct texel width. **The capability flipped by itself**, which is what the row asked for: `GraphicsDevice` derives `HalfFloatRenderTargets` from `SupportsSurfaceFormatAsRenderTargetEXT(HdrBlendable)`, so the probe answering true is the whole mechanism -- no second answer was added, and all ten `GraphicsCapabilityFloatRenderTargetTest` cases pass, including `EachCapabilityAgreesWithItsRepresentativeFormat` and `ASupportedFloatFormatReallyConstructsARenderTarget`. **The unclamped property is asserted twice, in two different shapes.** `HdrRenderTargetRoundTripTest.AHalfFloatTargetKeepsValuesAboveOne` reads the values back typed and renderer-neutrally. The new `hdr_render_target` parity fixture (`WEBGPU-207`) proves the same thing **in 8-bit output**, which needed a trick worth recording: a target holding 2.0 and one that clamped to 1.0 both read 255 through an 8-bit backbuffer, so the obvious fixture cannot see the substitution `MOD-115`/`MOD-107` exist to catch. `SpriteBatch`'s tint multiplies the sampled texel BEFORE the 8-bit write, so drawing at half tint gives 2.0 -> 255 and 1.0 -> 128; a target that clamped its own storage renders BOTH columns at 128 and fails the distinctness check, and one that dropped the tint renders both at 255 and fails the absolute check. Neither defect can produce the pair. **6/6 on both renderers and the frames are byte-identical, max diff 0.** **Deliberately NOT in the parity fixture:** the single- and dual-channel float formats. EasyGL broadcasts a one-channel texture to (r,r,r,1) where WGSL's `texture_2d<f32>` gives (r,0,0,1) -- a real divergence, recorded by `RenderTargetFormatAgreementTests` rather than papered over here. `WEBGPU-200` owns settling which one XNA means. |
| WEBGPU-200 | Full-float render targets: `Single`, `Vector2`, `Vector4`. | ✅ | **EasyGL evidence:** `Single`→R32F, `Vector2`→RG32F, `Vector4`→RGBA32F, gated by the same runtime probe. **WebGPU side:** `WGPUTextureFormat_R32Float` / `RG32Float` / `RGBA32Float` (webgpu.h 1039/1058/1066) are renderable in core WebGPU, but **sampling** one with a filtering sampler requires `WGPUFeatureName_Float32Filterable` (0x0E) and **blending** into one requires `WGPUFeatureName_Float32Blendable` (0x0F). Both are ordinary adapter features to request when present and probe for when not — neither is a reason to refuse the format, since an unfiltered/unblended float target is still a working XNA render target. **Expected:** request both features at device creation when the adapter has them; where `Float32Filterable` is absent, bind the target's view as an unfilterable-float texture with a non-filtering sampler rather than silently producing a filtered result; where `Float32Blendable` is absent, refuse a blend state on that target by name rather than ignoring it. `GraphicsCapability::FloatRenderTargets` follows the `Vector4` probe through the existing derived seam. **Depends on:** `WEBGPU-198`. **Tests:** shared fixture writing and reading exact float values, plus one asserting the refusal path on an adapter without the features (skip-with-reason if this adapter has them). **Acceptance:** all three formats allocate, render and read back exactly; every feature-dependent behaviour is either implemented or refused by name. **Done 2026-09-05.** Allocation, render and exact readback for `Single`/`Vector2`/`Vector4` came with `WEBGPU-198`; `HdrRenderTargetRoundTripTest.AFloatTargetKeepsValuesAboveOne` reads a `Vector4` target back with `EXPECT_FLOAT_EQ`, and `FloatRenderTargets` follows the `Vector4` probe through the existing derived seam with no second answer added. What this row itself owed was the two optional features. **Both are now requested at device creation when the adapter has them** -- the descriptor's feature list was a fixed one-entry array for `TextureCompressionBC` and is now built from what is actually present. **Measured on this adapter: it has BOTH**, so the happy paths are exercised for real. **Absent-feature behaviour, refused by name rather than degraded:** a non-opaque `BlendState` on a 32-bit float target throws naming `Float32Blendable`; a FILTERING sample of one throws naming `Float32Filterable` and says that `TextureFilter::Point` works without it. `GetAdditionalLimitationsTextEXT()` names whichever is missing. Neither absence refuses the format -- the targets stay renderable, drawable, clearable and readable -- which is the distinction the row insisted on. **Testing an absent feature on hardware that has it:** `DebugForceFloat32FeaturesAbsentEXT` makes the renderer report both absent. It does not remove the device features, so what runs is CNA's own decision-making, which is the part that can be wrong; without it these branches would be code no test ever executes. New `WebGPU_Float32Features`, 9/9: the present case works, each refusal fires and names its feature, both refusals are NARROW (the same float target still clears and draws opaquely; a `Color` target still blends; `Point` still samples), the limitations text names the gap, and clearing the override restores the real capability. **One placement bug this test caught, and it is the reason the test exists.** The blend refusal was first written inside `Build3DPipelineEXT` -- which only runs on a cache MISS, so it fired for the first such draw and then silently stopped firing for every later one that hit the pipeline cache. Check B blends into the same target twice and went red. Both refusals now sit at the public draw entry, where they are independent of the cache. **Not implemented, deliberately, and it is not a gap in the acceptance:** the row's alternative of binding an unfilterable-float view with a non-filtering sampler instead of refusing. That needs a second bind-group layout declaring `UnfilterableFloat`/`NonFiltering`, cannot be verified on any adapter available here, and the row's own acceptance admits "implemented **or** refused by name". A named refusal that a caller can act on (`Point` samples it) was chosen over an unverifiable bind path. |
| WEBGPU-201 | `SurfaceFormat::Rgba64` as a render target. | ✅ | **Not scheduled by the audit at all** — it appears in neither the exclusion table nor the parity matrix, despite EasyGL implementing it (C1). **EasyGL evidence:** `Rgba64`→`GL_RGBA16`/`UNSIGNED_SHORT`, 8 bytes per texel, and `ClassifyRenderTargetFormatEXT` returns `Unsupported` off desktop core rather than substituting — the comment records that FNA's adapter query promises it and that the original XNA Racing Game Kit selects exactly this format. **WebGPU side:** `WGPUTextureFormat_RGBA16Unorm` exists (webgpu.h 1061) but is **not** core WebGPU: natively it needs `WGPUNativeFeature_TextureFormat16bitNorm` (wgpu.h 0x0003000B, documented for Vulkan/DX12/Metal), and in a browser it would need the newer `WGPUFeatureName_TextureFormatsTier2` (0x14) if and when Chrome exposes it. **Expected:** request the native feature when the adapter offers it, probe as `WEBGPU-198` prescribes, allocate `RGBA16Unorm` and read back at 8 bytes per texel. **This is the one row in the family where a truthful permanent refusal is a likely outcome** — on an adapter or a browser without the feature, `ClassifyRenderTargetFormatEXT` returns `Unsupported`, `RenderTarget2D` throws by name and `GetAdditionalLimitationsTextEXT()` says which feature was missing. Record the answer per target (native Linux x86_64 vs browser) rather than one blanket verdict. **Depends on:** `WEBGPU-198`. **Tests:** allocate + render + `GetData` where supported; assert the named refusal where not. **Acceptance:** implemented where the adapter can, refused by name with the missing feature identified where it cannot — never substituted. **Done 2026-09-05, and the answer is a refusal -- but not the refusal the row predicted, and the difference only shows up because it was measured.** The row expected the outcome to turn on whether the adapter offers `WGPUNativeFeature_TextureFormat16bitNorm`. **This adapter HAS it**, and the device now requests it (native-only: the feature lives in wgpu-native's own `wgpu.h`, which the browser's emdawnwebgpu does not have, so the include and everything depending on it are behind `!__EMSCRIPTEN__` -- that split is itself the per-target answer the row asked for). And `RGBA16Unorm` still is not a render target here. **What was actually measured:** with the feature enabled, creating an `RGBA16Unorm` texture with `TextureBinding` usage SUCCEEDS while the same texture with `RenderAttachment` usage FAILS. So `Rgba64` is refused as a render target for a **usage** boundary, not a missing feature -- a distinction that a spec table or a feature check alone would have got wrong in both directions ("the adapter lacks it" is false; "the adapter has it, therefore it works" is also false). `GetAdditionalLimitationsTextEXT()` says exactly that, and says the other thing too when the feature is genuinely absent. **No special case was needed to produce this.** `Rgba64` is listed in `MapRenderTargetColorFormatEXT` unconditionally and `BytesPerTexelEXT` knows its 8-byte texel; whether it works is the PROBE's question, which is the separation `WEBGPU-198` was built around. The row's own instruction -- "probe; do not hard-code a spec/adapter matrix" -- is what produced the correct answer here. **Never substituted:** `RenderTargetFormatAgreement` asserts the query and the constructor agree for `Rgba64` like every other format, so the refusal cannot drift into a silent `Color` target. **One bug found while writing the limitations text:** it returns a single `string_view` and the new `Rgba64` clause was added as an early return in front of the existing ones, which silently hid them. `WebGPU_Float32Features` check E caught it. The text is now COMPOSED from every applicable clause and cached, because a device can be subject to several of these boundaries at once. |
| WEBGPU-202 | Non-`Color` render-target transfer: `GetData` widths, clear values, MRT and resolve. | ✅ | **Why separate from `199`–`201`:** the format rows allocate and draw; this row is everything downstream that currently assumes 4 bytes per texel and one format. `WebGPUTextureRenderer::UpdatePixelsLevel()` computes `levelW*levelH*4` unconditionally, and the render-target readback path stages through a `bytesPerRow`-aligned buffer sized the same way, so an RGBA16F (8) or RGBA32F (16) target would read back garbage or overrun. **Expected:** derive bytes-per-texel from the target's real format everywhere a transfer is sized; keep the 256-byte `bytesPerRow` alignment correct at the new widths (NPOT widths are the interesting case — see `WEBGPU-187`); make `WGPURenderPassColorAttachment.clearValue` carry the right value type; confirm what an MRT set with mixed formats does (WebGPU permits differing formats per attachment, but the pipeline's `targets[]` must match the bound set exactly — `WEBGPU-197`'s key must cover the whole set, not slot 0); and confirm resolve behaviour for a multisampled non-`Color` target, refusing by name where WebGPU does not permit the resolve. **Also:** `Texture::ValidateFormat` still admits `Color` alone for a plain `Texture2D` on **every** renderer including EasyGL, so this row does not widen plain textures — render targets only. **Depends on:** `WEBGPU-199` (first format that exercises it). **Tests:** byte-exact `GetData` round trip per format, one NPOT size each. **Acceptance:** every transfer path is sized from the format; no path fabricates or truncates bytes. **Done 2026-09-05.** **The readback width landed with `WEBGPU-198`, and not by choice** -- it had to, because refusing a float readback while the format query said the target was renderable broke five `HdrRenderTargetRoundTripTest` cases the moment they stopped skipping. `BytesPerTexelEXT` sizes every render-target transfer from the format (2/4/8/16), the 256-byte `bytesPerRow` staging alignment is computed at that width, and the BGRA swizzle applies to BGRA8 alone since reordering a float texel's bytes would corrupt it. **The MRT half was a real bug this row found.** `WEBGPU-197` folded slot 0's colour format into the pipeline key and nothing else, so two MRT sets differing only in slot 1 shared one pipeline. Measured, not reasoned: with the fix reverted the new test aborts with `"Incompatible color attachments at indices [1]: the RenderPass uses [Bgra8Unorm, Rgba16Float] but the RenderPipeline uses [Bgra8Unorm, Bgra8Unorm]"`. Every extra slot's format is now in the key, folded only when there are extra slots so no single-target key moved. **Clear values** needed nothing: `WGPURenderPassColorAttachment.clearValue` is four doubles, so a 2.0 clear reaches an RGBA16Float target unclamped -- proven by `HdrRenderTargetRoundTripTest.AHalfFloatTargetKeepsValuesAboveOne` and the `hdr_render_target` parity fixture rather than by reading the type. **Resolve** likewise: `AMultisampledFloatTargetResolvesWithoutClamping` passes, so a multisampled float target resolves here and no refusal was needed. **`UpdatePixelsLevel()`'s `levelW*levelH*4` is deliberately untouched.** It belongs to a plain `Texture2D`, and `Texture::ValidateFormat` still admits `Color` alone for those on every renderer including EasyGL -- so the assumption is currently true, and the row says explicitly that this does not widen plain textures. Widening it would be unreachable code. **Tests:** `AFloatTargetReadsBackExactlyAtNonPowerOfTwoSizes` reads a `Vector4` target back at 13x7, 17x3, 5x11 and 64x1 with `EXPECT_FLOAT_EQ` -- exact, not tolerant, because a readback is a byte transfer and a value that survives it approximately went through something it should not have. NPOT is the point: at 16x16 an RGBA32Float row is exactly 256 bytes and needs no padding, so the bug hides; at 13x7 it is 208 and the padding is real. All four sizes 100% exact. `AnMrtSetKeyedOnEverySlotsFormat` binds `{Color, Color}` and then `{Color, HdrBlendable}` in one frame and **draws into each** -- a clear needs no pipeline, so a clear-only leg would have passed whatever the key contained. |
| WEBGPU-165 | Honour the per-instance `multiSampleCount` on `RenderTarget2D` / `RenderTargetCube`. | ✅ | **Missing behaviour:** both target classes ignore the constructor's `multiSampleCount` and unconditionally mirror `WebGPURenderer::sampleCount_` (`WebGPURenderer.cpp` 1823/2307), because all twelve pipeline families bake one renderer-global `WGPUMultisampleState.count` and a target that opted out would be pipeline-incompatible. **Why the audit's "measure, then implement or document equivalence" was withdrawn (C3):** the divergence is observable through the **public property**, with nothing rendered. `RenderTarget2D`'s constructor overwrites `multiSampleCount_` with `rtRenderer_->GetMultiSampleCount()` (`RenderTarget2D.cpp` 114) and WebGPU returns `appliedMultiSampleCount_`, so on a device where `PickSampleCount` chose 4, `RenderTarget2D(device, w, h, false, Color, None, 0)` — an explicit request for no multisampling — reports `MultiSampleCount == 4`. One fixture showing no pixel difference cannot make that equal. **EasyGL evidence:** `EasyGLRenderTargetRenderer`/`EasyGLRenderTargetCubeRenderer` honour the per-instance value, clamp it to `GL_MAX_SAMPLES` and report the clamped result (`EasyGLRenderer.cpp` 3284-3289, 3713-3718); `easygl_rendertarget2d_msaa_test`, `easygl_rendertarget2d_properties_test`, `easygl_msaa_change_test`. **Expected:** carry the bound target's sample count in the pipeline key (`WEBGPU-197`) and select the target-compatible variant per bound target, as Vulkan's `RecordCommandBuffer` already does; allocate the target's MSAA colour attachment and resolve destination at ITS count, not the renderer's. **What measurement still legitimately decides:** the clamping rule; which counts this adapter actually accepts (`Supports4xMsaa()` probes only 4 — probe the set rather than assuming `{1,4}`); and the resolve behaviour when the requested count is clamped. It does not decide whether ignoring the caller is acceptable. **Depends on:** `WEBGPU-197`. **Tests:** a shared fixture (`WEBGPU-207`) with a 4× target and an explicitly single-sample target alive at once, asserting both the property and the edge pixels. **Acceptance:** the requested count is honoured, or clamped by a stated rule with the applied value reported; a documented limitation is admissible only for a count the adapter genuinely refuses, never for "the pipeline cache is global". **Done 2026-09-05, after the blocker it depended on was re-measured rather than assumed.** **The gate first.** `WEBGPU-197` left this row blocked on a recorded finding: `WEBGPU-58` measured that a shader module / bind-group layout / pipeline layout reused across a sample-count change silently renders wrong on this pin, which is exactly what per-target counts would do. That was **re-measured, and it does not reproduce**: new `modules/renderers/webgpu/examples/webgpu_msaa_module_reuse_probe_test.cpp` (`WebGPU_MsaaModuleReuseProbe`) drives wgpu-native off the renderer's own device, uses one module set for a 1-sample pipeline and then a 4-sample one, and compares that frame against a 4-sample frame from a FRESH module set. Measured stable over repeated runs: A1 496 lit / 0 partial, A4 528 / 32, B4 528 / 32, **max channel difference 0**. Non-vacuity is asserted separately -- two empty frames compare equal -- and the probe asserts the equality rather than printing it, so the hazard returning on a new pin goes red instead of this row silently producing wrong pixels. **One methodological note worth keeping:** the first version of that probe reported a confident wrong answer because mapping the readback buffer is not enough here -- the map callback can fire while the copy filling it is still queued, giving an all-zero frame, which is precisely the "pipeline fine, draw fine, no validation error, only the pixels wrong" signature `WEBGPU-58` recorded. Waiting on `wgpuQueueOnSubmittedWorkDone` first is what made the answer trustworthy. That is a plausible explanation for the original finding, not a demonstrated one. **The implementation.** Both target classes take their constructor's `multiSampleCount`, clamp it through `PickSampleCount`, allocate their MSAA colour attachment (and their depth attachment, which must agree) at THAT count, and report it. `CreateRenderTarget2D`/`CreateRenderTargetCube` pass it instead of discarding it. **The clamping rule was wrong and is fixed:** `PickSampleCount` answered 4 for ANY request of 2 or more, which is an increase, not a clamp -- a caller asking for 2 got 4. Nothing measured it while every target mirrored the global count; the shared contract (`rendertarget_msaa_depth_contract`) asserts `applied <= requested`, and its Depth24/2 and Depth24/3 legs are exactly the ones a round-up fails. It now rounds DOWN to a probed count. **Four stale declarations flipped**, all of which described the discarded-request behaviour: `msaaCubeTargets` in `rendertargetcube_usage_test` and `rendertargetcube_getdata_contract_test`, `msaaEngages` in `rendertargetcube_msaa_face_test`, and `webgpu_rendertarget2d_test` Check F. **Tests -- the row's own acceptance, measured:** `webgpu_msaa_test` Check D was inverted from "mirrors the global regardless of its own request" to the real contract and now runs three ways in ONE frame with the backbuffer at 4x: a target requesting 0 reports 0 **and renders a binary diagonal**, a sibling requesting 4 reports 4 **and blends its diagonal**. Both property and edge pixels, both directions, two live targets at two counts -- which also exercises the re-measured module-reuse path for real. 7/7. The whole `WebGPU` label, 116 tests, passes with only the two long-standing XNA-pixel-centre failures. |
| WEBGPU-166 | Compiled XNA Effects, step 1 — the **native** SPIR-V spike (preferred route, not the only route). | ⬜ | **Why this exists:** `plan_fx.md` §10.3 files `WEBGPU` under "programmable, but not through anything MojoShader emits … reconsidered only if a SPIR-V route into wgpu-native is proven". The pinned package has that route: `WGPUShaderSourceSPIRV` + `WGPUSType_ShaderSourceSPIRV` + `WGPUInstanceFeatureName_ShaderSourceSPIRV` (`webgpu.h` 2799-2819) and `wgpuDeviceCreateShaderModuleSpirV` (`wgpu.h` 1263/1490); MojoShader's pin enables the `SPIRV` profile unconditionally. **This task is the spike, in `spikes/webgpu-spirv-spike/`** (per `CLAUDE.md`'s existence-gate rule — never in the scratchpad): translate one `plan_fx.md` conformance fixture's vertex/pixel pair with MojoShader's SPIR-V profile, create both modules through `wgpuDeviceCreateShaderModuleSpirV`, build a pipeline, draw one triangle. `cna_mojoshader_vulkan_probe` (`FX-064`) is the working precedent for the nine-function `MOJOSHADER_effectShaderContext` backend and should be the starting point. **Record:** whether the instance feature must be requested; whether MojoShader's SPIR-V descriptor-set/binding model maps onto WebGPU's bind-group model, and at what cost; and the uniform/register layout it emits. **Gate semantics — changed 2026-09-04, second pass (C2):** a negative answer **activates `WEBGPU-203`**; it does **not** close `167`–`171` and does not license `SupportsCompiledEffects()` staying false. Failure here disproves one route — XNA bytecode → MojoShader SPIRV → wgpu-native SPIR-V extension — not the proposition that WebGPU can run compiled XNA Effects. Note also that a **positive** answer does not finish the question: browser WebGPU accepts WGSL only, so `WEBGPU-203` runs regardless for the Emscripten target. **Blocks:** `WEBGPU-204` (which selects the route), not `167` directly. **Acceptance:** a committed `.cpp` + `README.md` in the spike directory stating the answer with the evidence, and an amendment to `plan_fx.md` §10.3's WebGPU verdict. |
| WEBGPU-203 | Compiled XNA Effects, step 1b — evaluate the alternate translation route(s), including the one the browser needs either way. | ⬜ | **Created 2026-09-04, second pass (C2)**, because `WEBGPU-166` alone could not decide the question it was being asked to decide. **Runs when:** `WEBGPU-166` returns negative (the native SPIR-V route is unsuitable) **or** positive but the Emscripten target still needs a WGSL path — which is always, since browser WebGPU accepts `WGPUShaderSourceWGSL` only. So this row runs in both branches; only its weight differs. **The architecture to evaluate, conceptually:** XNA/D3D9 shader bytecode → MojoShader intermediate output → SPIR-V or another suitable intermediate → WGSL → WebGPU. A second candidate shape is a MojoShader profile that emits WGSL directly; MojoShader's profiles are pluggable and `profiles/mojoshader_profile_spirv.h` is the precedent for adding one. **No library is prescribed here, deliberately.** When this task runs, investigate what this repository can actually build and maintain — start from `cmake/ThirdPartyFNA3D.cmake`'s `cna_configure_mojoshader()` (the pin, the `SPIRV` profile and the five committed patches), the emdawnwebgpu package already used by the web target, and whatever SPIR-V→WGSL translator is obtainable — and judge each candidate on build cost, licence, whether it must run offline (asset-time) or in-process, and whether the same route can serve native and browser so the two do not diverge. **Record for each candidate:** feasible / infeasible, with the specific construct that fails if infeasible. **Deliverable:** a written route comparison in the spike directory, not a chosen implementation. **Acceptance:** every candidate has a verdict with evidence; "we did not try" is not a verdict. |
| WEBGPU-204 | Compiled XNA Effects, step 1c — select the route, or name the blocker. | ⬜ | **The gate that `WEBGPU-166` was wrongly given (C2).** Reads `166`'s and `203`'s evidence and does exactly one of two things. **(a) Select a route:** record which translation architecture `167`–`171` are implemented against, per target (native and browser may legitimately differ, e.g. SPIR-V natively and a WGSL route in the browser, provided both are stated and both are tested), and rewrite `167`–`171`'s notes against it. **(b) Declare a fundamental blocker:** permitted only when this row **names the thing that cannot be translated** — a specific XNA/D3D9 shader-model construct with no WGSL expression, a licence or platform constraint that makes every evaluated route unbuildable here, or an equivalent concrete obstacle — and states what evidence from `166`/`203` establishes it. "No implementation yet", "MojoShader emits no WGSL" and "the browser accepts WGSL only" are **not** blockers; the first is the gap itself and the other two are the translation problem restated. **Rule this row enforces for the whole phase:** `SupportsCompiledEffects()` may remain permanently false only after (b) is written and dated. Until then compiled effects are an open implementation gap, and the parity matrix says so. **Depends on:** `WEBGPU-166`, `WEBGPU-203`. **Blocks:** `WEBGPU-167`. **Acceptance:** either a named route in this row and `167`–`171` rewritten against it, or a named blocker with its evidence, amended into `plan_fx.md` §10.3 and `docs/webgpu-renderer.md`. |

Gate it behind `CNA_WEBGPU_COMPILED_EFFECTS`, matching the SDL_GPU/EasyGL/Vulkan pattern. **Depends on:** `WEBGPU-166`. **Tests:** reflection-only sections of the `FX-060` shared suite (`tests/support/CNA/TestSupport/CompiledEffectConformance.hpp`). **Acceptance:** parameters, techniques, passes and annotations reflect correctly; capability stays false until `WEBGPU-171`. |

Vulkan's four-fixed-descriptor-set design (`FX-065`) is the closest precedent; WebGPU's per-draw transient buffer pool (`WEBGPU-12`/`59`) is the natural allocator. **Depends on:** `WEBGPU-167`. **Tests:** the shared suite's value/clone/technique sections. **Acceptance:** a parameter set before a draw reaches the shader; a clone's values are isolated. |

| WEBGPU-172 | `MultiStreamVertexInput` — bind more than one `VertexBufferBinding` of the same input rate. | ✅ | **Missing behaviour:** `WebGPURenderer.cpp` contains **no reference to `params.vertexStreams`** outside the instanced route's `FirstInstanceStream`/`FirstPerVertexStream`. The capability falls through to the shared `false` default, so `GraphicsDevice::ValidateVertexStreamCapability()` refuses the draw before submission — truthful, but a refusal of a normal XNA shape. **EasyGL evidence:** REMED-GFX-201/202 — `EasyGLRenderer` binds every per-vertex stream into the VAO at continuing locations with its own VBO/stride/offset and restores the single-stream layout afterwards; `OrdinaryDrawMultiStreamTests`, `InstancedDrawMultiStreamTests`. **Expected:** one `WGPUVertexBufferLayout` per stream (WebGPU's native model — this is *easier* here than in GL), honouring each stream's `strideInBytes`, `vertexOffset`, `combinedByteBase` and `instanceFrequency`; report the capability true and `GetMaxVertexStreams()` from the device limit. **Depends on:** `WEBGPU-155` (the attributes come from the declarations either way), which landed 2026-09-04 and makes this cheap: the resolver already locates a semantic in any declaration, so the remaining work is one `WGPUVertexBufferLayout` per stream. **`WEBGPU-154` adds one requirement:** this route must also perform `WEBGPU-153`'s wireframe edge expansion when it starts accepting draws — today it is refused before submission, so there is no silent solid fill, and that must stay true. **Tests:** the two shared multi-stream suites plus a WebGPU pixel test splitting one declaration across two buffers and asserting the same image as the combined buffer. **Acceptance:** both shared suites pass; several per-instance streams at different frequencies also work (REMED-GFX-202). **Done 2026-09-05.** The capability is true and the implementation is WebGPU's native model, not an emulation: one `WGPUVertexBufferLayout` per **resolved** stream, each with that stream's own `arrayStride` and step mode. `ResolveStockVertexLayoutAcrossStreamsEXT` (renderer-neutral, in `StockVertexSemantics.hpp`) searches every bound declaration for each of the chosen program's inputs, so an input binds to whichever buffer declares its semantic and a stream supplying nothing the program consumes never reaches the pipeline. **One deliberate divergence from `GpuVertexStreamBinding::slot`'s own doc comment,** which calls the public slot "the native input slot ... WebGPU vertex-buffer index": native slots are assigned **densely over the streams a draw actually reads**. XNA lets a draw bind slots 0 and 15 while using two streams, and taking the public slot literally would need sixteen native buffers to describe a two-buffer draw -- more than this device's `maxVertexBuffers` of 8. The neutral record moves to the slot after the real streams, which is slot 1 for a single-stream draw, exactly where it was. **Program selection widened with it:** "is this a lit vertex?" is now "does SOME bound stream declare a Normal?" -- for the canonical split (position-only stride 12 + colour-only stride 4) neither stream alone is a layout any renderer recognises, so asking slot 0 alone renders nothing. **The instanced route was converted in the same task,** which the row's estimate did not anticipate but the capability made mandatory: one flag gates both routes, so leaving the instanced family stride-derived would have promised at the capability and refused at the draw -- the exact shape `WEBGPU-163` had just closed. Its per-vertex inputs resolve by semantic like every other family; its per-instance world-matrix columns resolve **POSITIONALLY** -- the k-th element across the concatenated per-instance declarations feeds column k. That asymmetry is not a shortcut, it is the measured answer: the shared oracle spells the columns `TEXCOORD1`-`TEXCOORD4` while this renderer's OWN instanced examples spell them `POSITION1`-`POSITION4`, and a first attempt that resolved them by semantic passed the oracle and broke `WebGPU_Instanced3D` and `WebGPU_InstancedOffsetFrequency_Cardinality`. The reference renderer settles it: `EasyGLRenderer::PlaceInstanceStreams` assigns consecutive locations from `kStockInstanceBaseLocation` in declaration order, whatever semantic each element names. A world matrix has no XNA-defined semantic to be faithful to. Columns may still be split across buffers (the oracle splits them 48 + 16). `StockProgramInput` gained an optional `explicitLocation` so `instanced3d.wgsl`'s existing 0/1/4-7 numbering needed no shader edit. A classic instanced draw resolves to exactly two streams and produces the binding pair the family always built by hand. **A buffer with no declaration at all** -- what the low-level `CreateVertexBuffer(count)` entry point these examples use produces -- gets a canonical one from `SynthesizeMissingStreamDeclarationsEXT`: the stride table's layout for a per-vertex stream (position-only for an unlisted stride, this route's own long-standing assumption) and the four `Vector4` columns at 0/16/32/48 for a per-instance one. **`InstanceFrequency` > 1 has no native counterpart** -- wgpu-native v29.0.1.1's `WGPUVertexBufferLayout` carries only nextInChain/stepMode/arrayStride/attributes and no `WGPUNativeFeature` adds a step rate -- so it is honoured by MATERIALIZING the records the draw reads (source record `VertexOffset + i / frequency`, repeated `frequency` times at queue time). The frequency never reaches the pipeline or its key. This generalizes the divisor loop the route already had for its single instance stream. **`GetMaxVertexStreams()`** = device `maxVertexBuffers` (read once via `wgpuDeviceGetLimits`) less the neutral slot, clamped to the resolver's table -- **7** here, never a constant. **`WEBGPU-154`'s requirement holds:** the wireframe expansion rewrites indices only and is untouched by the stream count, so a multi-stream draw wireframes like any other; `EveryPublicDrawRouteWireframesAndAcceptsSolid` still passes. **Still refused, by name (`RequireSingleStreamRouteEXT`):** a split vertex on the skinned/PBR families or under a custom WGSL `ShaderEffect`. Those still derive their layout from one byte stride and would read stream 0 alone; `WEBGPU-177` converts the first pair. This is a named refusal on a route, not a false capability. **Three bugs found by the existing suites, each caught by a test that predates this task:** (1) `BuildStockVertexStateEXT` initially set `WGPUVertexStepMode_Vertex` for every stream, binding the instance matrix per-vertex and failing all 28 instanced cases including the previously-green classic ones. (2) Resolving the instance columns by semantic broke both low-level instanced examples, because CNA has two spellings for the same matrix -- that is what produced the positional rule above. (3) `HashResolvedStockVertexLayoutEXT` mixed each stream's `instanceFrequency` VALUE into the pipeline key, so the same geometry at frequency 2 compiled a second identical pipeline; `WebGPU_InstancedOffsetFrequency_Cardinality` counts variants and caught it. The key now carries the input RATE, which is all a native vertex-buffer layout expresses. **Tests:** both shared oracles green (`OrdinaryDrawMultiStreamTests` 20/20, `InstancedDrawMultiStreamTests` 28/28), plus a new parity fixture `multi_stream_split` (`WEBGPU-207`) whose EasyGL and WebGPU frames are **byte-identical, max diff 0** across its three columns: one packed stride-16 buffer, the same vertex split 12 + 4, and the same split again with each binding skipping its own decoy prefix so the two `VertexOffset`s differ in bytes while naming one element. `WebGpuWireFrameContract.CapabilityQueryAnswersForWireFrame` was updated from `EXPECT_FALSE` to `EXPECT_TRUE` and now also prints and bounds `MaxVertexStreams`, and a new `WebGpuWireFrameContract.AMultiStreamDrawWireframesLikeASingleStreamOne` MEASURES the `WEBGPU-154` claim rather than arguing it: a split-vertex triangle under `FillMode::WireFrame` leaves an empty interior, covers under a quarter of the solid draw's pixels, and produces a frame byte-identical to the one-buffer wireframe. |
| WEBGPU-206 | Block-compressed `TextureCube` — the parity implementation `WEBGPU-163` only made safe. | ✅ | **Reclassified 2026-09-04, second pass (C5):** the audit let honest refusal close this family. It cannot, because the resource is representable: in WebGPU a cube is a 2D texture with six array layers plus a `WGPUTextureViewDimension_Cube` view, and `WGPUFeatureName_TextureCompressionBC` does not restrict BC to non-array 2D. The 2D path already has the pieces — `ClassifyWebGPUTextureFormat` maps `Dxt1`/`Dxt3`/`Dxt5`/`Dxt5SrgbEXT`/`Bc7EXT`/`Bc7SrgbEXT` to `WGPUTextureFormat_BC*` with block sizes 8/16/16/16/16/16, and `bcSupported_` gates them on the device feature. What is missing is that `WebGPURenderer::CreateTextureCube` discards `surfaceFormat` and `WebGPUTextureCubeRenderer` takes only `(owner, size, mipMap)`. **EasyGL evidence:** `IsCompressedCubeTransferFormatEXT()` returns the same answer as the 2D one and `EasyGLTextureCubeRenderer` genuinely stores blocks (or decodes them). **Expected — all five, none optional:** (1) cube allocation in the requested BC format, with the 4-texel block multiple enforced per face and the mip chain stopping where blocks do; (2) per-face, per-mip `SetData` of raw blocks through the compressed transfer route, with `IsCompressedCubeTransferFormatEXT()` overridden to match; (3) `GetData` per CNA's contract — return the stored blocks, or refuse by name if this renderer cannot read a compressed level back, but do not fabricate decoded pixels; (4) content loading without a decompression mismatch — `KeepCompressedOnLoadEXT` currently consults the **2D** classifier, so a DDS/XNB cube asset can be kept compressed for a path that cannot take it; (5) sampling through the normal XNA effect path (`EnvironmentMapEffect`'s `texture_cube<f32>` binding in `env_map3d.wgsl`). **Depends on:** `WEBGPU-163` (which stops the false promise first). **Tests:** construct + per-face `SetData` + `GetData` + sample a `Dxt1` cube, and one DDS/XNB cube content load, as shared fixtures (`WEBGPU-207`). **Acceptance:** a BC cube works end to end wherever the device has the BC feature; `163`'s refusal survives only on a device without it, and says which feature was missing. **Done 2026-09-05. All five, none skipped.** **(1) Allocation:** `CreateTextureCube` passed the format through instead of discarding it, and `WebGPUTextureCubeRenderer` classifies it with the SAME `ClassifyWebGPUTextureFormat` the 2D path uses -- a cube in WebGPU is a six-layer 2D texture plus a cube view, and the BC feature does not restrict BC to non-array 2D, so no cube-specific mapping was needed. The 4-texel block multiple is enforced at construction, by name; `RenderAttachment` usage is dropped for a compressed cube exactly as `WebGPUTextureRenderer` drops it for a compressed `Texture2D`. **(2) Upload:** `SetCompressedDataEXT` overridden -- and finding that was the first correction: the block path was written into `SetData` first, which is the RGBA8 route and NOT what `TextureCube::SetData`'s compressed overload calls, so all six formats reported "did not store the complete compressed cube face region". Whole-level only, deliberately: a block-aligned sub-rectangle is expressible natively, but the block store kept for readback is per level and accepting a partial write would make the two disagree. `WEBGPU-144`'s block-aligned copy extent applies per face unchanged. **(3) Readback -- and the row's "or refuse by name" was the WRONG branch, which the shared suite settled.** `TextureCube` has no block-shaped `GetData` (unlike `Texture2D`'s `GetCompressedDataBytes`), so refusing looked right. But `TextureCubeTest.SetDataCompressedBytesUploadsAnEntireDxt1Face` -- SKIPPED here until this row and running the moment `IsCompressedCubeTransferFormatEXT` turned true -- uploads DXT1 blocks and expects `GetData(Color*)` to return DECODED texels. That is the contract, and decoding stored blocks is not fabrication, so it decodes, through the framework's own `DxtUtil`/`Bc7Util` shared with every other renderer that stores blocks. **(4) Content:** nothing to change. `TextureCubeContentTypeReader` has always gated `keepCompressed` on `IsCompressedCubeTransferFormatEXT` rather than the 2D query (verified during `WEBGPU-163`), so overriding that query is what makes a DDS/XNB cube keep its blocks. 13 cube content tests pass. **(5) Sampling:** the new `compressed_cube` parity fixture (`WEBGPU-207`). **`WEBGPU-163`'s refusal is now correctly conditional:** `ClassifyTextureCubeFormatEXT` returns the 2D verdict, which carries the device BC-feature gate -- so it still refuses, by name, on a device without BC, and only there. **Two fixture-design corrections worth recording.** The sampling legs first drew each cube's quad straight into its own column; a reflection depends on surface POSITION and two columns are at different positions, so the two cubes sampled two different faces (measured: green against red) and the comparison was meaningless. Each quad is now rendered offscreen at identical clip-space geometry and blitted into its column, so only the cube differs. And the non-vacuity leg compared against a region that the blits had stopped being background; it now measures against the clear colour directly. **This is the first parity fixture whose frames are NOT byte-identical across renderers, and that is correct.** Its oracle is INTERNAL -- within one renderer, the BC cube must match the RGBA8 cube, and both do exactly (WebGPU 128,128,64 vs 128,128,64; EasyGL 125,130,64 vs 125,130,64). Across renderers the reflection DIRECTION differs, which is the pixel-centre convention `WEBGPU-187` owns and the one thing this fixture deliberately does not test. Recorded in the fixture and the docs so nobody later widens a tolerance until a frame diff passes. **Tests:** all 55 `TextureCube*` cases green including the two that had been skipping since they were written; `TextureCubeCompressedFormatAgreement` now reports "constructed, SetData accepted" for all six BC formats on WEBGPU, which is the same success arm EasyGL was already taking; the whole `WebGPU` label (119 tests) passes but for the two long-standing pixel-centre failures. |
| WEBGPU-173 | `BasicEffect` combination coverage. | ✅ | **Gap:** 20 EasyGL programs vs ~7 WebGPU ones. Untested on WebGPU: `EmissiveColor`; `AmbientLightColor`; the default three-light rig (`EnableDefaultLighting`); one light vs three; specular colour/power; `VertexColorEnabled` on and off with and without a texture; the vertex-colour clamp; `DiffuseColor`×`Alpha` composition; large world-scale precision. **EasyGL evidence:** `easygl_basiceffect_{emissive,default_lighting,one_light,multilight_emissive,specular,texture_enabled,texture_vertexcolor_enabled,vertex_color_clamp,vertexcolor_enabled,vertexcolor_disabled,world_scale_precision,combinations,combined,golden}_test`, plus `emissive_ambient_composition` and `viewspace_fog`. **Expected:** each combination produces the same pixels as EasyGL within the parity tolerance. **Depends on:** `WEBGPU-156`/`157` for the untextured and stride-36 shapes. **Tests:** one WebGPU example per behaviour cluster (not one per EasyGL file), each with a discriminating A/B. **Acceptance:** every listed property is proven to change the output in the expected direction. **Done 2026-09-05.** Three shared parity fixtures (`WEBGPU-207`), one per behaviour cluster as the row asks rather than one per EasyGL file, and **all three byte-identical between EasyGL and WebGPU, max diff 0**. **`basic_effect_light_terms`** puts each lighting term in its OWN colour channel -- emissive red, ambient green, the second and third lights blue, specular white -- against a shared one-light baseline. A brightness check alone cannot tell a term routed through the wrong slot from a correct one, so each column asserts its channel moved AND that the other two did not drift. Measured: baseline (89,89,89) = 0.35x255 exactly; emissive +115 = 0.45x255; ambient +89, which is `DiffuseColor * AmbientLightColor` and so proves ambient enters INSIDE the light sum rather than being added after; the extra two lights +89 together, proving the rig is summed; specular +38 on all three channels together. 7/7. **`basic_effect_vertex_color`** gives all five columns ONE vertex layout and ONE mesh, every vertex carrying the same non-white COLOR0, and varies only `VertexColorEnabled`/`TextureEnabled`. So a renderer that always multiplies the vertex colour fails the columns where it is off, and one that never multiplies fails the columns where it is on -- neither can hide behind convenient vertex data. The expected values are computed from the FORMULA in the fixture, not copied from a passing run. The vertex colour is (255,128,64), three different channels, so a luminance multiply or a swizzle matches nothing. Plus the clamp: red and green driven past 1.0 saturate while BLUE is held below 1.0 as the control proving the clamp is per channel rather than the fragment being forced to white. 10/10. **`basic_effect_alpha_scale`** covers `EnableDefaultLighting`, `Alpha` and world-scale precision. The alpha leg is drawn under an **opaque** blend on purpose: XNA premultiplies (`EffectHelpers.SetMaterialColor` sets `diffuse.rgb = DiffuseColor * Alpha`), so `Alpha` darkens the RGB even where the alpha channel is discarded -- a renderer that routed `Alpha` to the alpha channel alone renders both columns identically and is caught. Measured (204,153,102) at 1.0 halving to (102,76,51) at 0.5. The precision leg scales the world by 1000 over thousand-fold-smaller geometry and must reproduce the identity-transform column exactly; it does. 5/5. **A SHARED CNA DEFECT was found and deliberately NOT fixed here: `plans/plan_graphics.md` row 1116.** On the LIT vertex-colour path, `EmissiveColor` is dropped entirely -- on EasyGL and WebGPU alike, so WebGPU is not cloning an EasyGL bug. FNA's `VSBasicVertexLightingVc` is `ComputeCommonVSOutputWithLighting(...)` (whose `Lighting.fxh:43` adds `+ EmissiveColor`) followed by `vout.Diffuse *= vin.Color`, so XNA's answer is `(diffuse + emissive) * vertexColor`; for the fixture's inputs that is green 243 and both renderers return 128. The fixture steers around it with a comment naming the row, so nothing asserts the broken behaviour as correct, and fixing it means changing shared stock-effect shaders on every renderer -- a graphics-layer change with its own cross-renderer regression surface, not this row's to widen into. **One of my own claims corrected mid-task, worth recording:** I first concluded emissive was ignored whenever lighting was off. Reading `EffectHelpers.SetMaterialColor` showed the opposite -- with lighting off XNA folds `(DiffuseColor + EmissiveColor) * Alpha` on the CPU, so emissive reaches the shader inside `DiffuseColor` and both renderers reproduce it exactly. The defect is specific to the lit path, where the shader itself must add the term. |
| WEBGPU-174 | `AlphaTestEffect` coverage. | ✅ | **Gap:** 6 vs 2. Untested: the full `CompareFunction` sweep (`Always`/`Never`/`Less`/`LessEqual`/`Equal`/`GreaterEqual`/`Greater`/`NotEqual`) against `ReferenceAlpha`; a null `Texture`; `VertexColorEnabled` combined with `DiffuseColor`. **EasyGL evidence:** `easygl_alphatest_{comparefunction_sweep,modes,null_texture,vertexcolor_diffuse}_test`, `alphatesteffect_golden`. WebGPU's `alphaTest[4]` encoding (`refVal`/`tolerance`/`passWeight`/`failWeight`) already claims to express all of them — this proves it. **Acceptance:** all eight comparisons, each discriminating. **Done 2026-09-05.** Two shared parity fixtures (`WEBGPU-207`), **both byte-identical between EasyGL and WebGPU, max diff 0**. **`alpha_test_sweep`** is an 8×3 signature matrix, not eight pass/fail checks: one alpha value cannot separate the eight functions (at `alpha == reference`, `Always` and `GreaterEqual` both draw and `Never` and `Less` both discard), and two still leave four pairs tied, so each function is run against texel alpha BELOW, EXACTLY ON and ABOVE the reference. The resulting draw/discard triples — `DDD`, `...`, `D..`, `DD.`, `.D.`, `.DD`, `..D`, `D.D` — are pairwise distinct, so a renderer that confused ANY function with ANY other fails at least one of the 24 cells. All eight matched on both renderers, and the table is asserted balanced (12 draw, 12 discard) so neither always-draw nor always-discard can pass it. **Negative control:** collapsing `useTolerance` to `false` in the AlphaTest3D WGSL turned exactly `Equal` and `NotEqual` red — degrading into `Less` and `GreaterEqual` respectively, the two functions that need the tolerance branch — and left the other six green. That is what proves the four-float encoding is really being exercised rather than the fixture agreeing with itself. **`alpha_test_sources`** covers the row's other two items. The vertex-colour row pins that the surviving RGB is the full product `texture × vertexColor × DiffuseColor` — measured (128,128,16) from (255,255,255)×(255,128,64)×(0.5,1,0.25), a value no dropped factor reaches — and, separately, that the vertex ALPHA gates the fragment WITHOUT tinting it: the same geometry at vertex alpha 64 and 192 shades identically once both survive, because XNA premultiplies a raw vertex colour nowhere (only `SpriteBatch` does). **A real WebGPU defect was found and fixed by this row.** `AlphaTestEffect` with a **null `Texture`** did not render on WebGPU — it aborted with `std::invalid_argument: DrawColoredPrimitives requires a stride-16 (VertexPositionColor) vertex buffer`, where EasyGL renders. Cause: `SelectStockVertexShapeEXT` gated the alpha-test family on `params.texture0 != nullptr`, so a null texture dropped the draw out of the family and into the stride-derived ladder, which rejects a Position+Colour+UV record outright. But whether a texture is BOUND is not a question about the vertex shape, and `QueueAlphaTestDraw` has answered it since `plans/plan_gltf.md` GLTF-474 by sampling the neutral-white default — the identity for `tex * colour`, and exactly the fallback `EasyGLRenderer::EnsureDefaultWhiteTexture()` binds on unit 0.
