## Phase 59 — WebGPU backend: render pipeline creation

| #   | Task                                                                                                          | Status | Notes                                                                 |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| WEBGPU-29 | `WGPURenderPipelineDescriptor` builder helper: vertex state, primitive state, depth-stencil state, multisample state, fragment state | ✅ | **DONE 2026-08-28.** `WebGPURenderer::Build3DPipelineEXT(const Pipeline3DDescEXT&)` is the one shared `WGPURenderPipelineDescriptor` assembly for all 12 `GetOrCreatePipeline*3D` families: it builds the colour target (`surfaceFormat_` + `CurrentWriteMask()` + MRT via `InitStockColorTargetsEXT`), the `vs_main`/`fs_main` entry points, CCW front face, `ToWGPUCullMode`, the baked blend (`WEBGPU-78`), the renderer-global multisample state (`WEBGPU-58`), the depth-bias scale (`WEBGPU-41`/`79`) and the depth-format + stencil gating (`WEBGPU-39`/`83`), then creates the pipeline. Each caller now passes only its per-family bits (`Pipeline3DDescEXT`: vertex-buffer layout(s), shader module(s), pipeline layout, label) and keeps its own cache key/map. The former block -- character-identical across all 12 (verified field-by-field before extraction) -- is gone: −318 lines net. Behaviour-preserving: the full WebGPU suite is still **104/104** on `:131`, including every 3D/effect/instancing/skinned/pbr pixel test. Earlier steps toward this (`Make3DPipelineKey()`/`FillWGPUBlendState()`/`ToWGPUCullMode()` — `WEBGPU-30`/`41`/`78`/`79`/`82`) already shared the blend/cull/depth-bias logic; this closes the row by factoring the descriptor assembly itself. The SpriteBatch and MipBlit pipelines keep their own builders (they are not 3D families -- SpriteBatch uses a per-snapshot sample count and no depth bias; MipBlit is single-sample colour-only). |
| WEBGPU-30 | Pipeline cache: `std::unordered_map<uint64_t, WGPURenderPipeline>` with MakeKey(topo, depth, blend, cull, stride, wireframe, msaa) | ✅ | Verified 2026-07-17: `Make3DPipelineKey()` (shared anonymous-namespace helper) builds exactly this shape — topology, depthTest/Write/Func, blend enable+real factors (collapsed to fixed bits when disabled, matching `VulkanGraphicsBackend::PackBlendBits()`'s own precedent so different disabled `BlendState`s don't create duplicate pipelines), cullMode, wireframe, depthBias/slopeScaleDepthBias (`std::bit_cast` into the key — wgpu-native has no dynamic depth-bias override, so this is a real, honestly-documented pipeline-permutation cost, not hidden), plus a per-family salt (stride for `AlphaTest3D`/`DualTexture3D`). All 15 pipeline cache maps switched from `unordered_map<int,...>` to `unordered_map<std::uint64_t,...>`. `msaa` is not part of this backend's key — this backend has no MSAA pipeline variant at all yet (unlike Vulkan's own `MakeKey`), a real, narrower scope, not silently pretended otherwise. |

See `WEBGPU-26`. |
| WEBGPU-38 | `GetOrCreatePipelineInstanced3D()` — per-instance binding variant | ✅ | Verified 2026-07-18: two `WGPUVertexBufferLayout`s — binding 0 per-vertex (`WGPUVertexStepMode_Vertex`, only position read regardless of the buffer's real stride, matching Vulkan's own shader), binding 1 per-instance (`WGPUVertexStepMode_Instance`, a mat4 world transform as 4 `Float32x4` columns at locations 4-7). Cached by `(pvStride, instVbStride, topology, depthFunc, depthTest, depthWrite, blend, cullMode, wireframe, depthBias, slopeScaleDepthBias)` — a real, deliberate improvement over `VulkanGraphicsBackend`'s own instanced pipeline, which hardcodes a 64-byte instance-binding stride regardless of the instance vertex buffer's own declared stride (latent, harmless only because every existing caller happens to always use a plain mat4-only instance declaration); this backend's own binding genuinely matches whatever stride the caller's instance buffer declares. See `WEBGPU-27`. |

The pass's depth attachment, the pipeline `depthStencil` (null for `None`), and the stencil load/store ops (named only on a stencil-carrying format) are all threaded from the target's real format via `replayDepthFormat_`/`replayDepthHasStencil_`, and the format is part of `Make3DPipelineKey`/`SpritePipelineKey` so incompatible passes get their own pipeline. Observably verified by `WebGPU_DepthFormat` (6/6): `None`→no depth test (a farther fragment overwrites), `Depth16`/`Depth24`→depth test works with no stencil op emitted, `Depth24Stencil8`→depth test + a working stamp/gate stencil round trip on the target's own attachment. `WebGPU_SpriteBatch_BlendState` was updated to expect the depth-format-keyed sprite pipeline (a `None` RT and the `Depth24Stencil8` backbuffer now legitimately hold distinct sprite pipelines). |

Proven on the real RADV GPU (`:0`), all Passed: `WebGPU_SpriteBatch_BlendState`, `WebGPU_AdditiveBlendContract`, `WebGPU_ColorWriteChannels` (the earlier "complete XNA BlendState mapping remains open" was stale). (`WebGPU_Clear_Readback`'s 50%-alpha Check G, which had asserted non-FNA behaviour -- `WEBGPU-141` (B) -- was corrected to use `NonPremultiplied` and passes; it was never a blend-mapping defect, and the dedicated blend tests above confirm the mapping.) |
| WEBGPU-41 | Rasterizer: `WGPUPrimitiveState` mapping `CullMode`, `FillMode` (WireFrame via `topology=LineStrip` fallback or unsupported) | ✅ | Verified 2026-07-17: `CullMode`→`WGPUCullMode` via `ToWGPUCullMode()` (shared anonymous-namespace helper), baked into every one of the 10 3D `GetOrCreatePipeline*()` functions' `pipeline.primitive.cullMode` and folded into each family's pipeline cache key (`Make3DPipelineKey()`, `WEBGPU-30`). `FillMode::WireFrame` is stored (`fillModeWireframe_`) but reaches no pipeline state — wgpu-native has no polygon-mode API at all. Since `WEBGPU-115` (2026-08-04) that gap is a truthful boundary rather than a silent substitution: the capability query reports `false` and a polygon draw that would consume the fill mode throws `System::NotSupportedException` before anything is queued. The "`topology=LineStrip` fallback" this row originally speculated was not implemented, matching Vulkan's own no-fallback precedent. Runtime-verified by `WebGPU_GraphicsState`'s Checks A/B: a hand-derived-winding quad is culled under `CullClockwiseFace` and stays visible under `CullCounterClockwiseFace` (differential pixel-readback proof, not just "a pipeline was created") — the correct `WGPUCullMode_Front`/`Back` pairing was only confirmed by running this test; a first, purely-derived guess from NDC/raster-space reasoning was backwards, matching this project's established "empirically verify, don't just derive" rule for winding subtleties (see `VulkanGraphicsBackend::FillDepthStencilState()`'s own front/back-swap precedent). |

## Phase 60 — WebGPU backend: vertex and index buffers

| #   | Task                                                                                                          | Status | Notes                                                                 |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| WEBGPU-42 | `WebGPUVertexBufferBackend`: create `WGPUBuffer` (vertex, size=capacity×stride) with `COPY_DST` usage | ✅ | Lazily sized VERTEX\|COPY_DST buffer with capacity validation. Runtime-validated 2026-08-26 on the real RADV GPU (`:0`): the whole pixel-asserted 3D suite (`WebGPU_Colored3D`/`Textured3D`/`ColoredTextured3D`/`LitTextured3D`/`AlphaTest3D`/`DualTexture3D`/`Instanced3D`, all Passed) uploads its geometry through this buffer and draws from it. |
| WEBGPU-43 | `SetData()`: upload via `wgpuQueueWriteBuffer(queue, buffer, 0, data, byteSize)` | ✅ | `wgpuQueueWriteBuffer` upload. Runtime-validated 2026-08-26 (`:0`): every vertex in the pixel-asserted 3D suite reaches the GPU through this path (all 3D-suite CTests Passed). |
| WEBGPU-44 | `SetDataWithOptions()`: `Discard` = reallocate buffer; `NoOverwrite` = `wgpuQueueWriteBuffer` at offset | ✅ | Done 2026-08-26 -- but the honest resolution is NOT the row's original sketch, because that sketch does not fit WebGPU's model. `Discard` exists so an immediate-mode renderer can orphan a buffer the GPU is still reading; `NoOverwrite`-at-offset needs an offset this API has no parameter for. Neither hazard reaches this renderer: **every draw snapshots its own vertex/index bytes into its `ColoredDrawCommand`/`InstancedDrawCommand` at queue time** (`QueueColoredDraw`/`DrawInstancedPrimitivesEx` copy `ShadowData()`) and uploads that snapshot into a fresh per-draw `WGPUBuffer` at replay -- so two draws issued between two `SetData` calls already hold independent copies, a stronger guarantee than orphaning -- and `wgpuQueueWriteBuffer` is a non-stalling queue-ordered copy, so there is no stall for `Discard` to avoid. All three options therefore produce identical, correct results (mirrors `VulkanRenderer`, which also does not branch buffer uploads on the option). Documented at both `SetDataWithOptions` sites in code. New test `WebGPU_SetDataOptions` (`webgpu_setdataoptions_test.cpp`), 4/4 on the real RADV GPU (`:0`): None/Discard/NoOverwrite each upload+draw the right colour, and a `Discard` re-upload issued between two already-queued draws leaves the first draw's snapshot intact (Check D -- the exact immediate-mode hazard, proven safe here). |
| WEBGPU-45 | `WebGPUIndexBufferBackend`: 16-bit and 32-bit index buffers via `WGPUIndexFormat` | ✅ | 16- and 32-bit index buffers. Runtime-validated 2026-08-26 (`:0`): the indexed 3D-suite draws dispatch through these (all Passed). |
| WEBGPU-46 | `SetData16()` / `SetData32()`: `wgpuQueueWriteBuffer` | ✅ | Both index-upload paths. Runtime-validated 2026-08-26 (`:0`) by the indexed 3D-suite CTests (all Passed). |
| WEBGPU-47 | Disposed guard in all SetData methods (throw `ObjectDisposedException`) | ✅ | Done 2026-08-26. The guard lives at the XNA GraphicsResource layer -- `VertexBuffer.cpp`/`IndexBuffer.cpp` check `getIsDisposedProperty()` and throw `System::ObjectDisposedException` before any renderer call (so it is renderer-agnostic, exactly as every other backend relies on; `VulkanRenderer` has no renderer-local guard either). New test `WebGPU_DisposedGuard` (`webgpu_disposed_guard_test.cpp`) proves the WebGPU device path leaves it intact: on the real RADV GPU (`:0`) a live `VertexBuffer`/`IndexBuffer.SetData` succeeds (Checks A/C) while the same call after `Dispose()` throws `ObjectDisposedException` specifically (Checks B/D) -- 4/4 Passed. |
| WEBGPU-48 | `SetVertexBuffer(wgpuRenderPassSetVertexBuffer)` + `SetIndexBuffer(wgpuRenderPassSetIndexBuffer)` in draw dispatch | ✅ | `wgpuRenderPassEncoderSetVertexBuffer`/`SetIndexBuffer` are bound on every draw route. Verified 2026-08-26 (`:0`): the 3D-suite CTests (indexed and non-indexed) all Passed, so both bindings are exercised end to end. |

## Phase 61 — WebGPU backend: textures

| #   | Task                                                                                                          | Status | Notes                                                                 |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| WEBGPU-49 | `WebGPUTextureBackend`: `WGPUTexture` (2D, RGBA8Unorm, COPY_DST + TEXTURE_BINDING) + `WGPUTextureView` | ✅ | RGBA8Unorm Texture2D + view are runtime-verified: `WEBGPU-126` (`player.png`) and `WEBGPU-130` (`../mobile-eggbert`'s own UI/background textures) both sample correctly with no WebGPU validation errors. |
| WEBGPU-50 | `SetData()`: `wgpuQueueWriteTexture()` with `WGPUImageCopyTexture` + `WGPUTextureDataLayout` | ✅ | Level-upload is runtime-verified by the same two texture uploads above. |
| WEBGPU-51 | `Texture2D::GetData()`: staged MAP_READ copy with aligned rows and asynchronous map/poll completion | ✅ | Implemented 2026-07-18: `WebGPUTextureBackend::GetData()` reuses the exact staged MAP_READ-buffer/aligned-row/async-map-and-poll technique `WEBGPU-91`'s `ReadBackbuffer()`/`WebGPURenderTargetBackend::GetData()` already established — copies the whole requested mip level, then extracts the `x,y,w,h` sub-rectangle from the mapped memory (always `RGBA8Unorm`, so no BGRA swap needed, unlike the swapchain). Required adding `WGPUTextureUsage_CopySrc` to `WebGPUTextureBackend`'s texture descriptor (missing before this, since nothing previously read a plain `Texture2D` back). Note: `Texture2D::GetData()` at the XNA layer is itself CPU-shadow-based (`cpuPixels_`) and only calls through to this method for a `RenderTarget2D`-backed instance (which already uses the separate `WebGPURenderTargetBackend`) — so this was tested directly against `IGraphicsBackend`/`ITextureBackend` (matching `examples/webgpu_instanced3d_test.cpp`'s established convention for a backend capability with no XNA-layer round trip of its own), not through `Texture2D` itself. `examples/webgpu_texture2d_getdata_test.cpp` (`WebGPU_Texture2D_GetData` CTest), 3/3: full-texture round trip (exact byte match against an uploaded gradient), a non-origin sub-rectangle read, and a mip level 1 round trip distinct from level 0. |
| WEBGPU-52 | Mip levels: generate via `wgpuCommandEncoderCopyTextureToTexture` per level or leave as mip=1 (document) | ✅ | 2026-07-18 (mips/cube/compressed-textures cluster): real, genuinely-LINEAR-filtered mip generation, closing this row for both `Texture2D` (`WebGPUTextureBackend`) and `TextureCube` (`WebGPUTextureCubeBackend`). Investigated first, per the task brief: confirmed against this project's pinned wgpu-native v29.0.1.1 `webgpu.h` that `wgpuCommandEncoderCopyTextureToTexture` (this row's own originally-suggested technique) is a raw same-size copy — there is no vkCmdBlitImage-equivalent filtered-downsample primitive in wgpu-native at all, so the row's own alternative technique had to be used instead: a NEW `WebGPURenderer::GenerateMipsForLayer()` (thin wrappers `GenerateMips2D()`/`GenerateMipsCubeFace()`) renders one pass per mip level, each drawing a full-screen triangle (the standard 3-vertex, no-vertex-buffer `@builtin(vertex_index)` trick) that samples the PREVIOUS level through a real `WGPUFilterMode_Linear` `WGPUSampler` into the NEXT level's own single-mip render-attachment view — a genuine filtered downsample, not a nearest-neighbor copy or a raw same-size `CopyTextureToTexture`. **IMPORTANT, DELIBERATE, DOCUMENTED DIVERGENCE from FNA and every sibling CNA backend** (found during investigation, not silently introduced): `VulkanGraphicsBackend`/`EasyGLGraphicsBackend` only ever regenerate mip content for a RENDER TARGET being unbound (`vkCmdBlitImage`/`glGenerateMipmap`-on-unbind, matching real FNA3D `OPENGL_ResolveTarget` semantics) — neither backend, nor real FNA/XNA itself, auto-generates mip content for a PLAIN `Texture2D`/`TextureCube` at all (mip levels beyond 0 are always user/content-pipeline-supplied via explicit per-level `SetData()`, matching how a real XNA content-pipeline-processed `.xnb` already carries every level pre-baked). This WebGPU backend's `GenerateMips2D()`/`GenerateMipsCubeFace()` DOES generate real content automatically instead, triggered whenever content is written to level 0 (`WebGPUTextureBackend::UpdatePixels()` — covers both the constructor's initial upload AND any later `Texture2D::SetData(level=0,...)` call — and `WebGPUTextureCubeBackend::SetData(face, level=0, ...)`, per-face). This makes WebGPU's plain-texture mip behaviour strictly better (never garbage/undefined content at level>0) but genuinely DIFFERENT from FNA and every other CNA backend for the identical public `Texture2D`/`TextureCube(mipMap=true)` constructor call — explicit per-level `SetData(level>0,...)` calls made AFTER a level-0 write are, as before, not retroactively protected (a subsequent level-0 write will regenerate over them). `RenderTarget2D`/`RenderTargetCube` mip regeneration (the FNA-equivalent feature, matching Vulkan/EasyGL's own render-target-only precedent) remains its own separate, already-tracked scope cut (`WEBGPU-53`/`54`/`114`'s own rows), NOT closed by this row. `examples/webgpu_mipgen_test.cpp` (`WebGPU_MipGen` CTest), 9/9: a hard-edged red/blue vertical stripe (deliberately NOT power-of-2-aligned with the 2:1 downsample's texel pairing, so a straddling destination pixel actually exists) proves a genuine ~50/50 LINEAR blend `(128,0,128)` at the boundary — not a hard nearest-neighbor edge — for both `Texture2D` level 1 and one `TextureCube` face's level 1; a chain-downsampled level 2 (sourced from level 1, not level 0) has real, plausible, non-garbage content; and `mipMap=false` construction with non-empty pixel data does not crash (the `mipLevels<=1` no-op guard). Full WebGPU CTest suite (23 tests) still passes with no regressions. |
| WEBGPU-53 | `WebGPURenderTargetBackend`: `WGPUTexture` (RENDER_ATTACHMENT + TEXTURE_BINDING) + depth texture | ✅ | Runtime-verified 2026-07-18 (`WebGPU_RenderTarget2D`, 8/8). Colour texture always uses the swapchain's own chosen format (`surfaceFormat_`) rather than `RGBA8Unorm` so every existing `GetOrCreatePipeline*3D()` (each hardcodes `target.format = surfaceFormat_`) renders into it unchanged, with zero new pipeline-cache dimensions; the depth texture is created in the exact format the requested `DepthFormat` maps to (`WEBGPU-39`, `MapDepthFormatEXT()`: `None`→no depth texture, `Depth16`→`Depth16Unorm`, `Depth24`→`Depth24Plus`, `Depth24Stencil8`→`Depth24PlusStencil8`), matching what `VulkanRenderer::PickDepthFormat()`/EasyGL/Bgfx do; the pipeline's `depthStencil` state and the pass attachment are threaded from that real format (null/absent for `None`). Real per-instance `GetData()` readback (own `WGPUBuffer` MAP_READ + `wgpuCommandEncoderCopyTextureToBuffer`, mirroring `ReadBackbuffer()`'s pattern). Deliberately deferred, with a clear thrown error rather than a silent under-delivery: `mipMap=true` (mip-chain regeneration, `WebGPU_RenderTarget2D` Check G). `multiSampleCount>0` (MSAA) was attempted 2026-07-18 in `WEBGPU-58`: `WebGPURenderTargetBackend::GetMultiSampleCount()` is now overridden and unconditionally mirrors the backend's own global sample count (not its own constructor's requested value) once MSAA is engaged on the backbuffer — `WebGPU_RenderTarget2D` Check F still sees 0 in that test specifically because that test's `GraphicsDeviceManager` never enables `PreferMultiSampling`; see `WEBGPU-58`'s own row for the honest, still-open status of whether the resulting multisampled render actually blends correctly (it does not, yet). Also introduced `IWebGPUSamplable` (mirrors `VulkanGraphicsBackend`'s `IVulkanSamplable`) so a `RenderTarget2D` sampled back as an ordinary texture (`SpriteBatch`/`BasicEffect.Texture`) resolves safely via `dynamic_cast` everywhere a `GpuDrawParams::textureN` is consumed, replacing ~16 previously-unchecked `static_cast<const WebGPUTextureBackend*>` call sites that would have been undefined behaviour the first time any of them actually received a `WebGPURenderTargetBackend*` (verified safe by the full existing `WebGPU_*3D` suite, 15/15, plus `WebGPU_RenderTarget2D` Check D). |
| WEBGPU-54 | `SetRenderTarget(rt)` / `SetRenderTarget(nullptr)`: switch render pass target between RT and swapchain view | ✅ | Runtime-verified 2026-07-18 (`WebGPU_RenderTarget2D`, 8/8, esp. Check E). `SetRenderTarget2D()` eagerly flushes/renders whatever is queued for the OUTGOING target the instant the target actually changes (closing out that render pass immediately and starting fresh accumulation for the new target) rather than tagging every queued draw command with a target and replaying grouped-by-target at final-flush time (`VulkanGraphicsBackend`'s own deferred/replay model) — chosen as the smaller change for a backend with no pre-existing per-draw deferred/replay infrastructure to extend; see `SetRenderTarget2D()`'s own comment for the full design discussion. `WebGPU_RenderTarget2D` Check E is the critical architecture proof: a `RenderTarget2D`-targeted `Clear()` sandwiched between two backbuffer `Clear()`/readback calls does NOT leak into the backbuffer's own render pass. `SetRenderTargets(vector<...>)` (MRT, `WEBGPU-85`/`86`/`87`) is explicitly out of scope; `IGraphicsBackend`'s own default already forwards a single-element call to `SetRenderTarget2D()` unchanged. |
| WEBGPU-55 | `GetBackBufferData()`: readback via MAP_READ buffer + `wgpuCommandEncoderCopyTextureToBuffer` | ✅ | This *is* `WEBGPU-91`'s implementation (`WebGPURenderer::ReadBackbuffer()`/`CaptureReadback()`) — verified 2026-07-12 via `WebGPU_Clear_Readback`, see `WEBGPU-91`'s own row for the full detail. |
| WEBGPU-56 | `WebGPUTextureCubeBackend`: `WGPUTexture` (dimension=2D, arrayLayerCount=6, CUBE_COMPATIBLE) | ✅ | Verified 2026-07-18: a minimal, read-only-after-upload cube-map texture backend exists (`WebGPUTextureCubeBackend`), just enough for `EnvironmentMapEffect.EnvironmentMap` (see `WEBGPU-25`/`74`) — `WGPUTexture` (`WGPUTextureDimension_2D`, `depthOrArrayLayers=6`; wgpu-native/WebGPU has no distinct `CUBE_COMPATIBLE` usage flag the way Vulkan/D3D need one, the *view* alone selects `WGPUTextureViewDimension_Cube`) + `SetData()` per face/level via `wgpuQueueWriteTexture` with `origin.z=face`, mip-level pre-allocation mirroring `EasyGLTextureCubeBackend::CalculateCubeMipLevels()`. 2026-07-18 (same day, follow-up task): real `GetData()` added — see `WEBGPU-113`'s own row for the full detail. 2026-07-18 (follow-up task, mips/cube/compressed-textures cluster): the two remaining gaps both closed — real automatic mip-chain regeneration from level 0 (see `WEBGPU-52`'s own row) and `RenderTargetCube` (a separate sibling class, `WebGPURenderTargetCubeBackend` — see `WEBGPU-114`'s own row). Now ✅. |
| WEBGPU-57 | `WebGPUTexture3DBackend`: `WGPUTexture` (dimension=3D) | ✅ | Implemented 2026-07-18: `WebGPUTexture3DBackend`/`WebGPURenderer::CreateTexture3D()` — this backend's first `Texture3D` (volume texture) support (previously `IGraphicsBackend`'s own `nullptr`-returning default, so `Texture3D::backend_` was always null and every `SetData`/`GetData` call silently no-op'd). `WGPUTextureDimension_3D`, `size.depthOrArrayLayers=depth`; `SetData()`/`GetData()` use a single `wgpuQueueWriteTexture`/`wgpuCommandEncoderCopyTextureToBuffer` call each covering the full 3D sub-volume (origin+extent), not a per-depth-slice loop — wgpu-native's `WGPUTexelCopyBufferLayout::rowsPerImage` already describes a 3D buffer layout directly. Mip level COUNT mirrors `Texture3D.cpp`'s own width/height-only `CalculateMipLevels()` (depth does not participate in the count, matching FNA); wgpu-native still halves the actual per-level depth extent automatically like every other 3D-texture mip rule. `Texture3D`'s XNA-layer `SetData`/`GetData` have no CPU-side shadow at all (unlike `Texture2D`) and always call straight through to `ITexture3DBackend`, so this is a genuine end-to-end XNA-layer test. `examples/webgpu_texture3d_test.cpp` (`WebGPU_Texture3D` CTest), 3/3: a 2x2x4 volume with each of the 4 depth slices given its own distinct solid colour, full-volume + single-voxel (non-origin) readback, and a mip level 1 round trip distinct from level 0. |
| WEBGPU-58 | MSAA: `WGPUTexture` with `sampleCount=4`; resolve in render pass via `resolveTarget` | ✅ | Attempted 2026-07-18, resolved 2026-07-18. **Infrastructure implemented and verified (`WebGPU_Msaa`, now 6/6 PASS):** MSAA is a single BACKEND-GLOBAL `sampleCount_` (not a pipeline-cache-key dimension), mirroring `VulkanGraphicsBackend`'s own precedent — every one of the ~10 `GetOrCreatePipeline*3D()` families plus both SpriteBatch pipelines read the same `sampleCount_` for `WGPUMultisampleState.count`. `ApplyMultiSampleCount()` invalidates and lazily rebuilds every pipeline cache (`ClearAllPipelineCaches()`, see below) and recreates the backbuffer's depth texture and a new multisampled colour texture (`RecreateMsaaColorTexture()`) at the new sample count. `PickSampleCount()`/`Supports4xMsaa()` empirically probe real device capability via `wgpuDevicePushErrorScope`/a scratch 4-sample texture create/`wgpuDevicePopErrorScope` (never assumed) — on this machine/adapter the probe genuinely found exactly 4x supported, nothing higher (wgpu-native itself only defines sample counts 1 and 4, unlike Vulkan's 1/2/4/8/16/32/64 range), and `ApplyMultiSampleCount(8)`'s clamped-return-value contract is verified to return 4, never the raw unsupported request (Check C). `WebGPURenderTargetBackend` unconditionally mirrors the backend's global `sampleCount_` at construction time with its own multisampled colour+depth textures and a real single-sample resolve target, verified to report the SAME real applied value as the backbuffer regardless of its own constructor's requested count (Check D, 1 of 2). Backbuffer's `WGPURenderPassColorAttachment.resolveTarget` is the swapchain's own current texture (wgpu-native auto-resolves at render-pass end, no explicit resolve command) when `sampleCount_ > 1`. `ClearAllPipelineCaches()` fully tears down and recreates every 3D/sprite shader module, bind group layout, pipeline layout AND pipeline cache on every sample-count change (not merely the `WGPURenderPipeline` objects) — a real, independently-defensible fix from the initial 2026-07-18 investigation, kept. **ACTUAL ROOT CAUSE of the previously-reported "Checks B/D-2/E FAIL, genuine multisample rendering does not work end-to-end" symptom (found 2026-07-18, same day, by a follow-up investigation): it was never an MSAA bug at all — it was a test-authoring defect in `examples/webgpu_msaa_test.cpp` itself.** `BasicEffect.Apply()` leaves whatever `RasterizerState` the device already has (the XNA default, `CullCounterClockwiseFace`), and this test's diagonal triangle — unlike every other WebGPU 3D test in this suite (`webgpu_colored3d_test.cpp`, `webgpu_graphicsstate_test.cpp`, etc., all of which explicitly set `RasterizerState::CullNone` before drawing) — never overrode that default. The triangle's winding is a genuine XNA **back face** under `WebGPURenderer::ToWGPUCullMode()`'s mapping, which is itself correct and independently verified by `WebGPU_GraphicsState`'s own non-vacuous, real differential cull-mode checks (Check A/B there: a hand-derived-winding quad is culled under `CullClockwiseFace` and stays visible under `CullCounterClockwiseFace` — both directions genuinely exercised, not vacuous). So the triangle was being legitimately backface-culled on **every** sample count, not just under MSAA — completely unrelated to `sampleCount_`, `resolveTarget`, pipeline caching, or any other MSAA-specific mechanism. Check A's own assertion (`IsBinary()`, which only rejects intermediate/antialiased pixel values) could not detect this: an entirely-background row (nothing drawn at all) is trivially "binary", so Check A was passing **vacuously** the whole time — this is exactly why the original investigation's extensive backend-side elimination (surface/texture format, depth-clear timing, storeOp, viewport/scissor, cull mode *value* though not the missing override, blend state, resource freshness, error-scope state) found nothing: there was nothing wrong to find there. Confirmed via direct experiment: forcing `cullMode=None` for the exact same draw (no other change) immediately produced all 6/6 PASS with genuine, non-vacuous blended pixel values; adding an explicit `device.setRasterizerStateProperty(RasterizerState::CullNone)` call (matching the established pattern) in both `RenderBackbufferRow()` and `RenderRTRow()` in `examples/webgpu_msaa_test.cpp` is the actual, minimal, test-only fix — `modules/renderers/webgpu/src/WebGPURenderer.cpp` is untouched by this fix, confirming the production MSAA implementation was already correct. `CreateRenderTarget2D()`'s per-instance `multiSampleCount` parameter is still intentionally ignored (RT always mirrors the backend's global state) — an unrelated, still-open design note, not a bug. Full suite re-verified: `ctest -L WebGPU`, 16/16 PASS (15 pre-existing + `WebGPU_Msaa`). |

## Phase 62 — WebGPU backend: 2D rendering (SpriteBatch)

| #   | Task                                                                                                          | Status | Notes                                                                 |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| WEBGPU-59 | `WebGPUSpriteBatchBackend`: dynamic vertex buffer ring (3 frames) for sprite quads | ✅ | **DONE 2026-08-28.** The SpriteBatch dynamic vertex buffer is now a THREE-slot ring (`spriteVertexRing_[3]`): each bind cycle's `UploadSpriteVertices()` rotates to the next slot before writing, so the buffer written this cycle is never the one the previous two submissions are still reading; each slot still grows in place when a cycle needs more than it holds. (Uploads use `wgpuQueueWriteBuffer`, which the queue already serialises, so the ring is defensive/parity with the reference renderers rather than fixing a live stall.) `spriteVertexBuffer_` is a non-owning alias of the active slot so the write/bind sites are unchanged; the ring owns and releases the three buffers. Exercised by `WebGPU_BufferPoolStress` (many frames of sprites rotate the ring) and the full existing SpriteBatch suite. |
| WEBGPU-60 | Upload sprite quads via `wgpuQueueWriteBuffer` per batch | ✅ | Runtime-verified by every `WEBGPU-126`/`130` frame (flattened sprite upload → draw, no validation errors). |
| WEBGPU-61 | Per-batch draw: set pipeline, bind groups (UBO + texture), vertex buffer, draw | ✅ | Runtime-verified the same way — `WEBGPU-130`'s `../mobile-eggbert` run alone issues many real per-frame SpriteBatch draws (title, UI panels, animated character, progress bar, fade overlay) with no WebGPU validation errors. |
| WEBGPU-63 | Verify SpriteBatch sort modes: Immediate, Deferred, Texture, FrontToBack, BackToFront. | ✅ | Done 2026-08-26. New test `WebGPU_SpriteBatch_SortMode` (`webgpu_spritebatch_sortmode_test.cpp`), 6/6 on the real RADV GPU (`:0`): two overlapping solid sprites (red@layerDepth 0.2, blue@0.8) into a `RenderTarget2D`, reading which colour lands on top. Each depth-sorted mode is submitted in the OPPOSITE order to its sorted result, so a pass can only happen if the shared-layer sort actually reaches the WebGPU draw submission -- `BackToFront` -> red on top, `FrontToBack` -> blue on top (both overriding submission order); `Deferred` proven order-based both ways; `Immediate` in submission order; `Texture` (one texture group) order-preserving. As the row intended, the sort itself is shared `SpriteBatch` behaviour and is not reimplemented -- this validates the WebGPU submission path preserves it. |

| WEBGPU-66 | `DrawPrimitivesEx()`: dispatch by `GpuDrawParams` (stride, textureEnabled, lightingEnabled, dualTexture, skinned, instanced) | ✅ | Verified 2026-07-12 for stride 16 (`colored3d.wgsl`), stride 20 (`textured3d.wgsl`, requires `params.texture0 != nullptr`), stride 24 (`colored_textured3d.wgsl`, same texture requirement), stride 32 (`lit_textured3d.wgsl`, same texture requirement, real Blinn-Phong lighting when `LightingEnabled`, transparent unlit fallback otherwise), `AlphaTestEffect` on strides 20/24/32 (`alpha_test3d.wgsl`/`alpha_test_colored3d`, priority over the other stride dispatches — see `WEBGPU-72`), `DualTextureEffect` on strides 20/24 (`dual_texture3d.wgsl`/`dual_texture_colored3d`, priority below alpha test but above env map/skinned — see `WEBGPU-73`), `PbrEffect` on stride 48 (`pbr3d.wgsl`), and — plan_cnj.md Phase 14J — `SkinnedPbrEffect` on stride 68 and `SkinnedEffect` on strides 52/56 (`skinned_pbr3d.wgsl`/`skinned3d.wgsl`, priority order: pbr&&skinned combo first, then pbr-only, then skinned-only, matching `EasyGLGraphicsBackend::SelectProgram()`'s own precedence): all forward the caller's real `GpuDrawParams` via `FillExtUniforms()`/`FillLitLightUniforms()`/`FillAlphaTestUniforms()`/`FillSkinningParams()` (mirrors Vulkan's push-constant fillers field-for-field), instead of the `DrawColoredPrimitives` fallback's hardcoded white/true/no-texture. `WebGPU_DrawPrimitivesEx`/`WebGPU_Textured3D`/`WebGPU_ColoredTextured3D`/`WebGPU_LitTextured3D`/`WebGPU_AlphaTest3D`/`WebGPU_DualTexture3D`/`WebGPU_Pbr3D`/`WebGPU_Skinned3D`/`WebGPU_SkinnedPbr3D` CTests prove this concretely. Re-audited 2026-08-26: the earlier "Only `EnvironmentMapEffect`/instancing remain open" is now stale -- both shipped and Passed on the real RADV GPU (`:0`): `EnvironmentMapEffect` via `WebGPU_EnvMap3D` (+ `WebGPU_EnvMapEmissive`/`WebGPU_EnvironmentMapEffect_Fog`) and instancing via `WebGPU_Instanced3D` (+ `WebGPU_InstancedOffsetFrequency_Cardinality`/`WebGPU_InstancedVertexColor_Cardinality`). All GpuDrawParams dispatch families are now verified. |
| WEBGPU-67 | `DrawUserPrimitives()`: transient `WGPUBuffer` (COPY_DST + VERTEX, mappedAtCreation=false); upload + draw + release | ✅ | `RenderColoredDraws()` creates a transient `WGPUBuffer` (`Vertex\|CopyDst`) per draw, `wgpuQueueWriteBuffer`s the CPU shadow-copy into it, draws, and releases it after the frame's command buffer is submitted (not `mappedAtCreation` — uses the same queue-write pattern as every other buffer upload in this backend, an equally valid approach). |
| WEBGPU-68 | `DrawInstancedPrimitivesEx()`: second vertex buffer binding (per-instance mat4 world transforms) | ✅ | Verified 2026-07-18: real override (previously `IGraphicsBackend`'s own default, which throws). `params.instanceVb == nullptr` falls back to a real `DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx()` draw, matching `VulkanGraphicsBackend`'s own identical fallback. `WebGPU_Instanced3D` CTest (5/5): 3 instances in ONE draw call each paint their own small quad at their own independently-predicted screen location with the shared `DiffuseColor` (proves the per-instance buffer is genuinely read per-instance, not e.g. always instance 0), a region far from all 3 quads stays untouched, and the `instanceVb==nullptr` fallback renders correctly rather than throwing. See `WEBGPU-27`/`38`. |
| WEBGPU-69 | PrimitiveType mapping: TriangleList→`WGPUPrimitiveTopology_TriangleList`, TriangleStrip→Strip, LineList→LineList, LineStrip→LineStrip, PointList→PointList | ✅ | All 5 `PrimitiveType` values map via the existing `ToTopology()` (unchanged); now genuinely exercised by real 3D draw dispatch (`WEBGPU-64`/`65`), not just present in source. `WebGPU_Colored3D` only exercises `TriangleList` directly, but the pipeline cache is keyed by topology so the other 4 follow the same, now-proven code path. |
| WEBGPU-70 | `vertexStart` / `startIndex` / `baseVertex` support in draw calls | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-27: the reachable routes are now directly tested, and the one unapplied case is unreachable-by-design (option "remove the unreachable requirement").** New test `WebGPU_VertexStart` (`webgpu_vertexstart_test.cpp`), 5/5 on the real RADV GPU (`:131`): two identically-placed triangles (red at vertices 0..2, blue at 3..5) prove `DrawPrimitives(vertexStart=0/3)` selects red/blue (the `vertexStart*stride` byte offset), and `DrawIndexedPrimitives` with `baseVertex=0/3` and `startIndex=0/3` each shift to the correct triangle (`baseVertex` added to every decoded index, `startIndex` the first index element) — a wrong/ignored offset flips the sampled colour. The only route that ignores `vertexStart` is `DrawInstancedPrimitivesEx`, and that is **unreachable through any XNA API** (`DrawInstancedPrimitives` has no `vertexStart` parameter; `GpuDrawParams::vertexStart` is populated only by the ordinary paths), so there is nothing to fix or test there — the requirement is removed for the instanced route rather than force-fixed on untestable code. Historical note follows. `DrawColoredPrimitives`/`DrawIndexedColoredPrimitives` always draw from vertex/index 0 (`wgpuRenderPassEncoderDraw(pass, vertexCount, 1, 0, 0)` / `DrawIndexed(..., 0, 0, 0)`) — matches every real call site today (`GraphicsDevice.cpp`'s two `DrawColoredPrimitives`/`DrawIndexedColoredPrimitives` callers always build a dedicated, zero-based temporary buffer per call), but a genuine sub-range draw would silently ignore a non-zero start/base. Real gap, not yet needed by any current caller. **Historical note (2026-08-26 audit, when this row was 🟨): the gap is UNREACHABLE through any XNA API, so it stayed 🟨 and was not force-fixed (touching working instanced-replay code for an untestable path is the wrong trade):** XNA's only instanced entry point, `GraphicsDevice::DrawInstancedPrimitives(primitiveType, baseVertex, minVertexIndex, numVertices, startIndex, primitiveCount, instanceCount)`, has no `vertexStart` parameter at all -- it controls the geometry stream with `baseVertex` (added to every decoded index) and `startIndex` (first index), both of which this renderer's `DrawInstancedPrimitivesEx` already applies (`command.baseVertex = params.baseVertex + perVertexOffset`, `command.firstIndex = params.startIndex`). `GpuDrawParams::vertexStart` is populated only by the ordinary `DrawPrimitives`/`DrawIndexedPrimitives` paths, never for an instanced draw. So the only way to reach the unapplied-`vertexStart` branch is a hand-built `GpuDrawParams` passed straight to the renderer interface -- no game and no XNA-layer path can, and there is therefore no API-level test that would exercise it. |

| WEBGPU-71 | `WebGPUEffectBackend`: `BasicEffect` wires to `FillGpuDrawParams` → UBO upload | ✅ | Corrected 2026-07-18 (stale bookkeeping, no new code needed): this row's literal `WebGPUEffectBackend` class was never built — matching `VulkanGraphicsBackend`'s own architecture, there is no separate effect-backend class on this backend either. `BasicEffect::FillGpuDrawParams()` → `WebGPURenderer::DrawPrimitivesEx()`/`DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx()` → `FillExtUniforms()`/`FillLitLightUniforms()` → real UBO upload is the actual, already-real wiring, and has been since `WEBGPU-66` (`🟨`, stride 16/20/24/32 real dispatch) — confirmed via the passing `WebGPU_Colored3D`/`WebGPU_Textured3D`/`WebGPU_ColoredTextured3D`/`WebGPU_LitTextured3D`/`WebGPU_BasicEffect_PreferPerPixelLighting` CTests, all of which exercise real `BasicEffect` draws end-to-end with real (non-hardcoded) `DiffuseColor`/lighting/texture parameters. |

Both `EnvironmentMapEffect::Texture` and `::EnvironmentMap` are genuinely optional (unlike every other stride-32+ effect family's `texture0 != nullptr` dispatch gate) — a null texture/cube map falls back to a 1×1 white default at render time (`EnsureEnvMapDefaultTextures()`), matching `VulkanGraphicsBackend`'s own `defaultWhiteView_`/`defaultWhiteCubeView_` fallback. `WebGPU_EnvMap3D` CTest (4/4): hand-derived geometry (`View`=`World`=Identity, quad at z=0.5, `Normal`=(0,0,-1)) makes the reflection vector land exactly on `CubeMapFace::NegativeZ` — proven by painting each of the 6 faces a distinct solid colour and asserting the correct one appears; a differential Fresnel check (same scene, only `FresnelFactor` differs) proves the Fresnel edge-weighting term genuinely gates the blend, not just present-but-inert; `EnvironmentMapAmount=0` proves the amount independently gates the blend; the indexed-draw path is also exercised. See `docs/webgpu-renderer.md` for a fuller writeup if that doc is updated alongside this task. |
| WEBGPU-75 | `SkinnedEffect`: bone palette as large UBO (72 × mat4 = 4608 bytes) in separate bind group | ✅ | Implemented as a `SkinningParams` UBO (a `WeightsPerVertex` vec4f header + `array<mat4x4f, 72>`, 4624 bytes total including the header) in its own bind-group slot (`skinnedBindGroupLayout_` binding 2, `skinnedPbrBindGroupLayout0_` binding 3) — comfortably under the 65536-byte minimum guaranteed limit this row anticipated. See `WEBGPU-26`. |
| WEBGPU-76 | `ShaderEffect` (custom WGSL): `wgpuDeviceCreateShaderModule` from user-provided WGSL source string | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-27, on the 3D `DrawPrimitivesEx`/`DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx` route (the EasyGL/Task-1079 model), verified on the real AMD RADV GPU (`:0`).** New `WebGPUEffectRenderer : IEffectRenderer` (a friend sibling of the renderer, mirroring `WebGPUOcclusionQueryRenderer`): `CompileProgram` builds a vertex + a fragment `WGPUShaderModule` (`vs_main`/`fs_main`) inside a validation error scope, so an invalid source becomes a catchable `GetCompileError()` rather than a device error (the tolerant neutral `ShaderEffectTests` clone survives). WGSL has no loose uniforms, so — unlike EasyGL — `DeclareUniformBlockEXT(size, names, offsets, count)` is implemented as the name→byte-offset bridge and every `SetUniform*` writes into a CPU staging block that a draw uploads to a `@group(0) @binding(0)` uniform buffer; a source-scan for `texture_2d` decides whether the bind-group layout also carries the reserved `@binding(1)` sampler + `@binding(2)` texture. `CreateEffectRenderer` returns it; `ExecutesShaderEffectSourceEXT()` → **true** and `GetShaderDialectEXT()` → **`Wgsl`** are now overridden (WebGPU genuinely runs the source, unlike Vulkan's SPIR-V). Integration: a new `DrawFamily::CustomEffect` + `CustomEffectDrawCommand` (vertex/index bytes, the uniform block **captured by value per draw**, the captured unit-0 texture, the vertex layout derived from the bound `VertexDeclaration` with `location = element index`, and the blend/depth/cull/viewport/scissor snapshots) queued from a branch at the top of both `Draw*PrimitivesEx` when `params.customEffectRenderer` is set, replayed through the one shared `ReplayDrawsInOrder` path (no forked replay); the renderer sets `World`/`View`/`Projection` (column-major, untransposed) on the effect at queue time exactly as EasyGL's `BindCustomEffectMatrices`. The pipeline builder takes a `colorAttachmentCount` (1 here) so MRT (`WEBGPU-85/86/87`) reuses it for N slots. **Test:** new `WebGPU_ShaderEffect3D` (`webgpu_shadereffect_3d_test.cpp`), a Lambert `texture*diffuse*N·L` custom WGSL effect drawing a `VertexPositionNormalTexture` quad — Check A `World=Identity`+light-faces-normal+`uDiffuseColor=red` → red (proves the WGSL runs, lights, samples), Check B `World=RotateY(180°)` → black (proves `World` reaches the WGSL vertex stage, flipping the normal), Check C `uDiffuseColor=blue` → blue (proves the uniform drives the pixel). Discrimination confirmed: forcing the fragment to a constant colour fails all three (0/3). **Full WebGPU suite 87/87 on `:0`** (86 prior + this), so the shared-replay change regressed nothing. This row is the single-colour-target custom-WGSL foundation and the enabler for MRT (`WEBGPU-85/86/87`, done 2026-08-27) and custom SpriteBatch effects (`WEBGPU-142`, done 2026-08-27), both of which reuse this `WebGPUEffectRenderer`. |

## Phase 64.1 — WebGPU backend: stock-effect fog parity (FNA EffectHelpers.SetFogVector)

> Fog was previously implemented only for `EnvironmentMapEffect`. Every other FNA stock 3D effect
> (`BasicEffect`, `AlphaTestEffect`, `DualTextureEffect`, `SkinnedEffect`) also exposes
> `FogEnabled`/`FogStart`/`FogEnd`/`FogColor`. The `GpuDrawParams` fog fields (`fogColor`,
> `fogVector`) are already filled by every effect's `FillGpuDrawParams()`; the work here is to carry
> them into each family's WGSL and apply FNA's `ApplyFog`. The design widens the shared primary
> `Uniforms` UBO from 128 to 160 bytes (appends `fogColor` + the FNA view-space `fogVector`); the WGSL
> computes `fogFactor = 1 - clamp(dot(vec4(objectPos,1), fogVector), 0, 1)` in the vertex stage and
> `rgb = mix(fogColor, rgb, fogFactor)` in the fragment stage — the exact `colored3d`/`env_map3d`
> parity form the Vulkan renderer already uses.

| #   | Task                                                                                                          | Status | Notes                                                                 |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| WEBGPU-145 | `BasicEffect` fog on the `colored3d`/`textured3d`/`colored_textured3d`/`lit_textured3d` (per-pixel + per-vertex) WGSL families. | ✅ | Done 2026-08-27, verified on the real RADV GPU (`:131`). Widened the shared primary `Uniforms` UBO to 160 bytes (`FillExtUniforms`/`FillColoredUniforms`/`FillAlphaTestUniforms` + all 9 primary-UBO command arrays now 40 floats); `coloredBindGroupLayout_`/`litBindGroupLayout_` binding-0 `minBindingSize`→0 so the families still binding 128 bytes (instanced/pbr/skinned-pbr, which do not read the fog tail) stay valid. Fog blend added to the 5 BasicEffect WGSL fragment shaders (both `lit_textured3d` variants apply it in the unlit branch too, since BasicEffect fog is independent of lighting). New test `WebGPU_BasicEffect_Fog` (`webgpu_basiceffect_fog_test.cpp`), 18/18: `FogStart==FogEnd` collapses every family to `FogColor` (the discriminator that fails if the WGSL blend is dropped — verified by temporarily neutering `colored3d`'s blend: Colored checks then fail), half-fog matches `lerp(FogColor,base,keep)` exactly for colored/textured (via calibration through the fog-off path) and lies between base and `FogColor` for the lit families, `FogStart`/`FogEnd` boundaries, fog-disabled preserves base, and a different `FogColor` genuinely changes the result. Full WebGPU suite 94/94, `EnvironmentMapEffect` fog unregressed. |
| WEBGPU-146 | `AlphaTestEffect` fog on its WGSL families (strides 20/24/32). | ✅ | Done 2026-08-27, verified on the real RADV GPU (`:131`). Both `alpha_test3d` WGSL modules (uncolored stride 20/32, vertex-colour stride 24) now declare the fog tail (a `fogPad` vec4f skips floats [28..31], since the alpha-test `Uniforms` block only uses `diffuseColor`/`alphaTest`/`extra` = floats [16..27]) and apply `mix(fogColor, rgb, fogFactor)` to the pixel that SURVIVES the alpha-test discard (FNA applies fog after the discard). New test `WebGPU_AlphaTestEffect_Fog` (`webgpu_alphatesteffect_fog_test.cpp`), 6/6: for each module, `FogStart==FogEnd`→`FogColor`, half fog matches `lerp(FogColor,base,keep)`, fog-disabled preserves base. `WebGPU_AlphaTest3D` unregressed. |
| WEBGPU-147 | `DualTextureEffect` fog on its WGSL families (strides 20/24). | ✅ | Done 2026-08-27, verified on the real RADV GPU (`:131`). Both `dual_texture3d` WGSL modules use the standard BasicEffect `Uniforms` layout (fog at floats [32..39], no padding), so they gained the fog fields + `mix(fogColor, rgb, fogFactor)` on the composed `sample0*2 x sample1 x tint` result. New test `WebGPU_DualTextureEffect_Fog` (`webgpu_dualtextureeffect_fog_test.cpp`), 6/6: for each module the base is verified as `grey*2 x tex1 = tex1`, then `FogStart==FogEnd`→`FogColor` and half fog matches `lerp(FogColor,base,keep)`. `WebGPU_DualTexture3D` unregressed. |
| WEBGPU-148 | `SkinnedEffect` fog on its 4 WGSL variants (strides 52/56, all `WeightsPerVertex`, both `PreferPerPixelLighting`). | ✅ | Done 2026-08-27, verified on the real RADV GPU (`:131`). All 4 skinned WGSL modules ({per-pixel, per-vertex} x {stride-52, stride-56 vertex-colour}) gained the fog fields in the primary `Uniforms` block and apply `mix(fogColor, rgb, fogFactor)`; the fog factor uses the SKINNED view-space position (`dot(vec4(skinnedPos.xyz,1), fogVector)`), matching FNA SkinnedEffect (which skins the position before `ComputeCommonVSOutput`) and Vulkan's `skinned3d.vert.glsl`. `skinnedBindGroupLayout_` binding-0 `minBindingSize`→0. New test `WebGPU_SkinnedEffect_Fog` (`webgpu_skinnedeffect_fog_test.cpp`), 12/12: for each of the 4 modules, ambient-only base = DiffuseColor, `FogStart==FogEnd`→`FogColor` (drop-the-fog discriminator), half fog matches `lerp(FogColor,base,keep)`. `WebGPU_Skinned3D`/`WebGPU_SkinnedPbr3D` unregressed; full WebGPU suite 97/97. |
| WEBGPU-149 | Correct the FNA fog blend to premultiply `FogColor` by the OUTPUT ALPHA, and strengthen the fog tests. | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-27 (review-fix), verified on the real RADV GPU (`:131`).** FNA `ApplyFog` (Common.fxh) is `color.rgb = lerp(color.rgb, FogColor * color.a, fogFactor)` -- it lerps toward `FogColor * outputAlpha`, not plain `FogColor`. WEBGPU-145..148 (and the ported Vulkan/env-map shaders) omitted the `* color.a`, which is only visible for alpha<1. Fixed all 16 fog blends across every stock-effect WGSL path (BasicEffect colored/textured/colored_textured/lit×2, AlphaTest×2, DualTexture×2, EnvironmentMap, Skinned×4) to `mix(fogColor.xyz * outAlpha, rgb, keep)`; output alpha unchanged. Also set the three fog-family bind-group layouts' binding-0 `minBindingSize` to the exact 160-byte size (all consumers bind 160 bytes; every `*DrawCommand::uniforms` is `std::array<float,40>`) with a `static_assert`, replacing the weaker `minBindingSize=0`; fixed the stale "128-byte" comments. **Tests strengthened** (`FogColor*alpha` discriminator that fails if the alpha-multiply is removed -- verified by temporarily reverting colored3d): `WebGPU_BasicEffect_Fog` **29/29** now also covers stride 24 (`VertexPositionColorTexture`) and an indexed draw; `WebGPU_AlphaTestEffect_Fog` **12/12** adds stride 32 (`VertexPositionNormalTexture`); `WebGPU_DualTextureEffect_Fog` **8/8**; `WebGPU_SkinnedEffect_Fog` **20/20** tests `WeightsPerVertex` 1/2/4 with a Z-shifting bone (`bone1=Translate(0,0,+4)`) so `dot(skinnedPos,FogVector)` is provably distinguished from `dot(originalPos,FogVector)` (WPV=2 moves the skinned z out of the fog range → no fog); the `WebGPU_EnvironmentMapEffect_Fog` `FoggedRgb` was corrected to scale `FogColor` by the output alpha. Full WebGPU suite 100/100. **This is what makes the "full stock-effect fog parity" claim true for alpha<1.** |
| WEBGPU-150 | Common WebGPU test preflight/launcher: a permanently-unavailable display/GPU returns CTest skip 77, a transient X11 error is retried, real failures and expected aborts propagate. | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-27 (review-fix).** `modules/renderers/webgpu/webgpu_test_launcher.sh` (POSIX sh; the WebGPU tests are Linux-only) wraps every display/GPU-backed WebGPU CTest: (1) a **preflight** checks the DISPLAY's X socket (a short retry absorbs a transient race) and exits **77** if unreachable -- this uniformly skips headless runs INCLUDING supervisor/expected-abort tests (which must be skipped, not run, without a display, so "display unavailable" is distinguished from the expected abort by never reaching it); (2) it runs the test and **retries only on a narrow display/adapter-unavailable signature** (`x11 not available`, `no adapter`, `timed out waiting for adapter`, …), skipping (77) after the retries; (3) any other non-zero exit -- a real failure or an expected abort -- is **propagated unchanged**. Wired via an opt-in in the shared `cmake/TestHelpers.cmake` (`CNA_RENDERER_GPU_TEST_LAUNCHER`, set only by the WebGPU examples) that routes single-executable tests whose ENVIRONMENT sets a `DISPLAY` through the launcher; pure-CMake tests (checksum, present-mode) and the native smoke test (its own `SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION`) are unwrapped. `SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77` was already applied directory-wide. **Discriminating self-test** `WebGPU_LauncherSelfTest` (`webgpu_launcher_selftest.sh`, 6/6, uses `CNA_WEBGPU_LAUNCHER_FORCE_DISPLAY` to simulate present/absent with no real X server): absent display→77, pass→0, real failure→propagate 3, expected abort (SIGABRT)→propagate 134 (not skip), persistent x11-unavailable→77, transient→retry succeeds→0. **Verified end to end:** on `:131` the full suite is 101/101 through the launcher; forcing an absent display makes the GPU tests (incl. `WebGPU_PresentLifecycle`, the expected-abort supervisor) report **Skipped** with 0 failures. |
| WEBGPU-151 | Versioned/hashed download-cache stamp + migration, so a pre-checksum cache is re-validated (not blindly trusted). | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-27 (review-fix).** The old gate was `if(NOT EXISTS stamp)`, so a `.cna-extracted` stamp written before the checksum work made the whole verification skip -- an unverified cache. The stamp now carries **version + asset + expected SHA-256** (`cna_webgpu_write_stamp`), and `cna_webgpu_prepare_cache` (in `cmake/WebGPUChecksum.cmake`, now driving `ThirdPartyWebGPU.cmake`) decides the cache state without downloading: a trusted stamp with its archive present is reused; a **legacy/mismatched stamp with an authentic cached archive** (hash matches) is re-extracted and the stamp upgraded; a **corrupt/tampered archive OR a changed version/asset whose archive no longer matches** purges exactly this package's archive+stamp+extracted `lib/`+`include/` (`cna_webgpu_purge_cache` -- targeted, never a wide/recursive delete of `_deps`) and forces a fresh, hash-verified download; a missing archive downloads. `CNA_WEBGPU_ROOT`/`AUTO_DOWNLOAD=OFF` unchanged. **Integration test** `WebGPU_CacheMigration` (`cmake/tests/webgpu_cache_test.cmake`, 12/12, pure CMake, builds a real fixture zip so the extract path runs) exercises: correct new stamp→no download, legacy stamp→re-extract+upgrade, wrong hash→purge (archive+extracted removed)+download, changed version/asset→re-validate, interrupted extraction (archive, no stamp)→extract+stamp, no archive→download. **Verified end to end on the real cache:** a forced legacy 2-line stamp on the existing `cmake-build-webgpu` was re-validated against the pinned hash and upgraded to 3 lines on reconfigure, with no re-download. |

## Phase 65 — WebGPU backend: state management

| #   | Task                                                                                                          | Status | Notes                                                                 |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| WEBGPU-77 | `SetDepthTestEnabled()` / `SetDepthWriteEnabled()`: bake into pipeline key | ✅ | Already true for every 3D pipeline family since `WEBGPU-32`/`33`/`34`/`35`/`37` (each keyed by `(topology, depthTest, depthWrite, depthFunc)` at minimum); 2026-07-17 confirms and extends this via the shared `Make3DPipelineKey()` helper (`WEBGPU-30`), which now also folds blend/cull/wireframe/depthBias into the exact same key alongside depth. |
| WEBGPU-78 | `SetBlendState()`: map `BlendState` preset → `WGPUBlendState` | ✅ | Verified 2026-07-17: `ApplyBlendState()` (storage-only, mirrors `VulkanGraphicsBackend::ApplyBlendState()`'s Opaque-collapses-to-disabled derivation) + `FillWGPUBlendState()`/`ToWGPUBlendFactor()`/`ToWGPUBlendOperation()` (shared helpers) bake the real per-`BlendState` factors/op into every one of the 10 3D pipeline families' `WGPUColorTargetState::blend` — previously always `nullptr` regardless of `BlendState` (blending was completely disabled for every 3D draw, a real, substantial gap, confirmed missing before this task). Runtime-verified by `WebGPU_GraphicsState` Checks C/D: `BlendState.NonPremultiplied` genuinely blends a 50%-alpha draw to a value strictly between the two colours, while `BlendState.Opaque` on the identical draw produces a pure unblended overwrite — a genuine differential proof of real factor wiring, not just an enabled/disabled bit. |
| WEBGPU-79 | `SetRasterizerState()`: `CullMode` → `WGPUCullMode`; `FillMode::WireFrame` unsupported (log warning) | ✅ | Verified 2026-07-17 — see `WEBGPU-41`'s row (same `ApplyRasterizerState()`/`ToWGPUCullMode()` work). `FillMode::WireFrame` is stored but not logged with a runtime warning: since `WEBGPU-115` (2026-08-04) a log line is not the boundary — the capability query reports `false` and the draw throws. `WebGPU_GraphicsState` Check G now asserts that refusal, that the backbuffer the refused draw was aimed at is untouched, and that Solid renders exactly afterwards. |
| WEBGPU-80 | `SetScissorRectangle()`: `wgpuRenderPassEncoderSetScissorRect` | ✅ | Verified 2026-07-17: `SetScissorRect()` (storage-only) applied once per render pass in `EnsureFrameRendered()` via `wgpuRenderPassEncoderSetScissorRect`, mirroring Vulkan's own `vkCmdSetScissor` call — genuinely dynamic wgpu-native state, not baked into any pipeline. A disabled scissor test still issues an explicit full-target rect (WebGPU has no separate enable toggle — the rect IS the only clip). Runtime-verified by `WebGPU_GraphicsState` Check E: a full-screen quad is clipped exactly at the `ScissorRectangle` boundary (pixel just inside shows the quad, just outside shows the untouched clear colour). |
| WEBGPU-81 | `SetViewport()`: `wgpuRenderPassEncoderSetViewport` | ✅ | Verified 2026-07-17: `SetViewport()` (storage-only) applied once per render pass via `wgpuRenderPassEncoderSetViewport`, defensively clamped to the current physical surface size at the point of GPU application. Real regression found and fixed while landing this: `GraphicsDevice.Viewport`'s own default value (set once via `UpdateViewportFromWindow()`→`IGraphicsBackend::GetViewportSize()`, not refreshed every frame) can be wider than the actual physical swapchain surface in this backend specifically (`GetViewportSize()` returns the CNA-specific *logical/letterboxed* viewport size, not necessarily the physical one) — harmless while `SetViewport()` was a no-op, but once wired for real this stale/oversized value visibly stretched every 3D **and 2D SpriteBatch** draw in the pass (both share the one deferred render pass). Caught via this task's own regression sweep: `git stash` of this whole change on this same branch/environment made `WebGPU_Clear_Readback`/`WebGPU_BasicEffect_PreferPerPixelLighting`/`WebGPU_Skinned3D`/`WebGPU_SkinnedPbr3D` pass 100%, proving the failures were newly introduced, not pre-existing/flaky; the clamp (matching wgpu-native's own hard x+width/y+height-within-target requirement) restores all 4 to passing alongside the new checks — root-caused, not just masked, since clamping is independently correct per the WebGPU spec regardless of why the stored value was oversized. The underlying `GetViewportSize()` logical-vs-physical semantics question is a separate, pre-existing, out-of-scope concern for a future task (not touched here — no `Microsoft::Xna::Framework` changes). Runtime-verified by `WebGPU_GraphicsState` Check F: a half-width `Viewport` confines a full-clip-space quad to its own sub-rectangle. |
| WEBGPU-82 | `SetSamplerState()`: per-slot `WGPUSampler` cache (filter + address mode key) | ✅ | 2026-07-17: extended beyond the SpriteBatch-only 18-entry cache. `ApplySamplerState()` now stores real per-slot `SamplerState` (filter/addressU/addressV/maxAnisotropy, 16 slots) and `GetOrCreateSlotSampler()` (a genuine `unordered_map`-keyed `WGPUSampler` cache, full XNA `TextureFilter` enum incl. anisotropic/mip variants, distinct from `samplerCache_`) is now actually READ by every texture-consuming 3D `Queue*Draw()` (Textured/LitTextured/AlphaTest/DualTexture/Pbr/Skinned/SkinnedPbr) for texture slot 0 — previously every one of these hardcoded Linear/Clamp/Clamp regardless of `GraphicsDevice.SamplerStates[0]` (confirmed: `command.textureFilter`/`addressU`/`addressV` were declared on every `*DrawCommand` struct but never assigned in any `Queue*Draw()` before this task). Re-audited 2026-08-26 -> ✅: the "no dedicated pixel test yet" gap is closed. `WebGPU_StockEffectSamplerContract` (REMED-GFX-169) plus `WebGPU_PointSamplingContract`/`WebGPU_TextureFilterOrdinalContract`/`WebGPU_DualTextureSlotSamplerContract` now prove that a non-default per-slot filter/address mode observably changes a stock 3D effect's sampled output -- all Passed on the real RADV GPU (`:0`). Slot 1 is exercised by the dual-texture slot-sampler contract. |
| WEBGPU-83 | `SetDepthStencilState()`: stencil ops → `WGPUStencilFaceState` | ✅ | **UPDATE 2026-08-27 (Phase 3): two-sided winding is now pixel-validated — WEBGPU-83 is COMPLETE.** New WebGPU-local test `WebGPU_StencilTwoSided` (`webgpu_stencil_twosided_test.cpp`) replicates the cross-renderer parity contract of `easygl_depthstencilstate_stencil_twosided_test.cpp` (Task 318): a back-facing triangle under `TwoSidedStencilMode=true` picks up the `CounterClockwise*` ops (NotEqual→Increment→0x06→green) while the two-sided=false control applies the front ops to every face (Equal→Decrement→0x04→background) — opposite outcomes toggled by that one flag, so a bypassed test or a swapped front/back slot cannot pass both. **2/2 PASS on the RADV GPU**, confirming the front=primary/CW → `stencilFront`, back=CCW → `stencilBack` mapping is correct (the "silent winding quirk" the Phase-1 note feared did NOT bite). Full WebGPU suite 92/92. --- **UPDATE 2026-08-27 (Phase 2): the stencil bake now covers ALL 3D families, not just colored3d.** The same shared machinery (`StencilKeyParams`/`FillWGPUStencilState`/`HashStencilState`/`CaptureStencilStateEXT`) is wired into every `GetOrCreatePipeline*3D` (textured3d, colored-textured3d, lit-textured3d + its per-pixel VertexLit sibling, alpha-test3d, dual-texture3d, env-map3d, instanced3d, pbr3d, skinned3d, skinned-pbr3d): each takes a `const StencilKeyParams& stencil` param, folds `HashStencilState` into its own cache key, bakes `FillWGPUStencilState` into its `depthStencil`, captures the state per-draw in its `*DrawCommand`, and applies `wgpuRenderPassEncoderSetStencilReference` per draw. A new WebGPU-local test `WebGPU_StencilFamily` (`webgpu_stencil_family_test.cpp`) stamps then gates on the **Textured3D** route and asserts the gate is *rejected* outside the stamped region (the discriminator that fails if the family ignored stencil) — 2/2 on the RADV GPU. **Still open (Phase 3):** only the **two-sided** front/back winding remains unvalidated by a dedicated two-sided differential test (both proven routes use non-two-sided stencil); the two-sided mapping is implemented but not pixel-verified. --- **UPDATE 2026-08-27 (Phase 1): stencil ops are now baked into the pipeline on the colored3d route, and the shared cross-renderer acceptance test flipped to assert it.** `GetOrCreatePipelineColored3D` now calls `FillWGPUStencilState` (XNA `CompareFunction`/`StencilOperation` → `WGPUStencilFaceState` via the new `ToWGPUStencilOperation`; front/back = the primary/CW ops, or CW→front + CCW→back when `twoSidedStencilMode`), with the read/write masks folded into the pipeline cache key (WebGPU keeps masks as *pipeline* state, unlike Vulkan's dynamic masks) via a colored3d-local `HashStencilState` XOR (a disabled stencil folds a constant, so it never fragments the cache; `Make3DPipelineKey` stays shared/unchanged). The stencil state is captured per-draw in the `ColoredDrawCommand` (`CaptureStencilStateEXT`), and the reference is applied dynamically per draw (`wgpuRenderPassEncoderSetStencilReference` from `command.stencilRef`) so a stamp and a gate in one pass can differ. **Acceptance:** the shared `rendertarget_depthstencil_usage_test` WEBGPU contract flipped `stencilInRT`/`stencilPreserves` false→true, and its **C2** ("the stencil gate passes exactly where the stamp was and is rejected everywhere else") now passes on the real RADV GPU (`:0`) — 29/29, full suite 90/90. Before this, C2 vacuously asserted "gate passes everywhere". **Still open (narrowed):** the other 3D families (textured3d/lit/skinned/PBR/env-map/dual-texture/alpha-test/instanced) still store the stencil state but do not bake it — the shared `StencilKeyParams`/`FillWGPUStencilState`/`HashStencilState`/`CaptureStencilStateEXT` infra is ready to extend to them the same way; and the **two-sided** front/back winding is implemented but not yet validated by a two-sided differential test (the acceptance test uses non-two-sided stencil, so the primary path is proven, the winding of two-sided is not). Original 2026-07-18 deferral note follows. --- Re-audited 2026-08-26 -- still 🟨, gap unchanged. Deliberately scoped down 2026-07-17 (see task report): `ApplyDepthStencilState()` now stores the FULL real stencil parameter set (`stencilEnable_`/`stencilFunc_`/`stencilPass_`/`stencilFail_`/`stencilDepthFail_`/`stencilReadMask_`/`stencilWriteMask_`/`twoSidedStencilMode_`/`ccwStencil*_`), extending what was previously depth-only storage — but baking these into any pipeline's `WGPUStencilFaceState` is deliberately DEFERRED, not attempted in this task. Rationale: `VulkanGraphicsBackend::FillDepthStencilState()`'s own comment documents a real, empirically-discovered front/back-winding quirk specific to that backend that required swapping which `VkStencilOpState` slot XNA's "front"/CCW settings land in; baking stencil ops into this backend's 10 pipeline families without an equivalent WebGPU-side differential stencil test risks the identical silent-wrongness class — and this task's own cull-mode mapping (`WEBGPU-41`/`79`) already needed exactly one such empirical correction after a first, purely-derived guess turned out backwards, reinforcing the risk is real, not theoretical, for this exact class of winding/orientation subtlety. `SetReferenceStencil()`'s own dynamic-state half (`wgpuRenderPassEncoderSetStencilReference`, applied once per pass) IS implemented and wired (see `WEBGPU-80`/`81`'s row), independent of this remaining gap. A dedicated stencil-ops task (with its own differential pixel test, mirroring Vulkan's `stencil_twosided` precedent) remains open. |
| WEBGPU-84 | `OcclusionQuery`: `WGPUQuerySet` (type=Occlusion) + `wgpuRenderPassEncoderBeginOcclusionQuery` | ✅ | Done 2026-08-26. `CreateOcclusionQuery()` now returns a real `WebGPUOcclusionQueryRenderer` (a `friend` of the renderer, mirroring `VulkanOcclusionQueryRenderer`'s idiom). Each query reserves a slot in a shared 32-slot occlusion `WGPUQuerySet`; `OcclusionQuery.Begin()/End()` set/clear an `activeOcclusionQuery_`, `RecordDrawOrder` tags each queued draw with it, and `ReplayDrawsInOrder` wraps that query's first contiguous run of draws in one `wgpuRenderPassEncoderBeginOcclusionQuery`/`EndOcclusionQuery` pair (only the first run per flush -- a slot may be written once per resolve, the same policy Vulkan uses). Every render pass now carries `occlusionQuerySet_`; after the passes `ReplayOrderedSegments` resolves the set into a `QueryResolve` buffer and copies it to a `MapRead` buffer on the same encoder, and `IsComplete()`/`PixelCount()` map it lazily (also drained at the next flush). **The whole path is gated on `activeOcclusionQuery_`, so a frame with no query records exactly what it did before** -- the full suite stayed at its prior pass count. Capability flipped: `SupportsCapability(OcclusionQuery)` now returns true (the `WEBGPU-135` false arm removed) with `WebGpuMrtOcclusionContractTests` updated in the same commit to assert true. New pixel test `WebGPU_OcclusionQuery` (3/3 on the real RADV GPU, `:0`): the SAME full-screen quad reads back `PixelCount == 0` behind a depth occluder and `4096` (a full 64x64 target) in front, with two independent per-slot query results. Known scope: a query whose draws span more than one render-pass segment records only its first segment (documented; occlusion queries normally wrap draws to one target). |

## Phase 66 — WebGPU backend: Multiple Render Targets

| #   | Task                                                                                                          | Status | Notes                                                                 |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| WEBGPU-85 | MRT render pass: `WGPURenderPassDescriptor` with array of `WGPURenderPassColorAttachment` (up to 4) | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-27, verified on the real AMD RADV GPU (`:0`), full WebGPU suite 88/88.** `PassDestination` now carries up to 4 colour attachments (slot 0 in its single fields, slots 1..N-1 in `mrt*` arrays; depth stays single/shared) and `ReplayOrderedSegments` builds an N-entry `WGPURenderPassColorAttachment` array from it — one shared replay path, count-parameterised, no MRT/non-MRT fork. `RenderPendingDrawsToRenderTarget` fills the extra attachments from the bound set. **See the Phase-0 note below for why WEBGPU-76 had to land first.** — Phase-0 decision gate, 2026-08-27 — ordering fixed so the next reader does not re-derive it: WEBGPU-76 (custom WGSL `ShaderEffect`) MUST land first; MRT then follows.** Rationale, evidence-backed, not a guess: (1) **Every** stock WGSL fragment shader in `WebGPURenderer.cpp` writes exactly one output — `@fragment fn fs_main(...) -> @location(0) vec4f` (all ~30 of them: lines 2790, 3020, 3196, 3270, 3549, 3675, 3953, 4010, 4229, 4281, 4538, 5004, 5057, 5291, 9052, 9712, 9844, 9986, 10105, …). Nothing this renderer can draw writes `@location(1..3)`. (2) The renderer has **no** custom-WGSL execution path: `ExecutesShaderEffectSourceEXT()` is not overridden (0 occurrences in `WebGPURenderer.cpp`) so it inherits `IGraphicsRenderer`'s `return false` (`IGraphicsRenderer.hpp:2179`), and WebGPU is one of the few renderers that does **not** implement `CreateEffectRenderer` (22 others do). So there is no way to author a fragment shader that writes a second slot. (3) WebGPU pipeline validation requires each non-null colour target to have a matching fragment `@location(i)` output — a stock `@location(0)`-only pipeline cannot even be **created** against a 2-target fragment state, so a stock draw into an MRT set fails at pipeline creation, not merely semantically. (4) This is exactly why the Sokol precedent (the one renderer that shipped MRT here) verified MRT only with a custom `ShaderEffect` writing distinct per-slot outputs and **refuses** a stock-pipeline draw while MRT is bound (`SokolRenderer.cpp` ~5478-5484). (5) Therefore Phase-0 option (b) — "prove real per-slot writes another way" — is unreachable: the only sanctioned mechanism to write slot ≥1 is a multi-`@location` custom WGSL shader (WEBGPU-76). A clears-only MRT (pass descriptor + readback of clear values, no fragment-output routing) is explicitly disallowed by this task. **Conclusion: option (a). Do WEBGPU-76 first (multi-output WGSL is its enabling case for MRT), then build 85/86/87 on top and verify with a custom per-slot ShaderEffect à la Sokol.** Phases 1-5 of the MRT work-order (pass array, `GetOrCreateMRTRenderPipeline`, `SetRenderTargets` 2..4, the capability-trap flip in `WebGpuMrtOcclusionContractTests`/`WebGpuWireFrameContractTests`, and the `WebGPU_MRT` pixel test) are all unchanged and correct — they are simply gated behind WEBGPU-76 existing first. |
| WEBGPU-86 | `GetOrCreateMRTRenderPipeline(colorAttachmentCount)`: pipeline with matching `targetCount` in fragment state | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-27.** Every pipeline that replays into a pass now matches the pass's attachment count, keyed by it (folded into `Make3DPipelineKey` and `SpritePipelineKey` only when > 1, so single-target keys — and the instanced/viewport/scissor cardinality tests — are byte-identical). A **custom `ShaderEffect`** (`IssueCustomEffectDraw`) builds N fragment targets and its WGSL writes `@location(0..N-1)` (this is the real MRT path). A **stock/built-in draw** (3D and SpriteBatch) into an MRT set writes attachment 0 only: `InitStockColorTargetsEXT` widens each stock pipeline to N targets with slots 1..N-1 at `writeMask` 0 (the "the 2D/stock pipeline writes attachment 0" behaviour every other renderer has) — chosen over the Sokol refuse-route because the shared cross-renderer MRT tests (`graphicsdevice_ordered_clear_test` M1, `rendertarget_invalid_mip_level_test` H1, `backbuffer_pass_order_test`) do stock draws into MRT and read attachment 0 back. `replayColorAttachmentCount_` is set at replay from the `PassDestination` and read by all stock builders. |
| WEBGPU-87 | `SetRenderTargets(vector<RenderTarget2D*>)`: configure MRT pass descriptor | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-27.** `SetRenderTargets` accepts 2..4 `RenderTarget2D` targets: it validates every target shares width/height/sample count (refusing a mismatch with a `System::NotSupportedException` that names the disagreeing target), refuses a cube face or a null target in an MRT set, and refuses count > 4 (XNA's ceiling). Slot 0 binds as before; slots 1..N-1 go into `mrtExtraTargets_`. The single-target and cube-face paths are byte-identical, and any single-target / cube-face / backbuffer bind — including `SetRenderTarget(nullptr)` — flushes the outgoing set and fully clears `mrtExtraTargets_`. |
| WEBGPU-142 | Custom-WGSL `ShaderEffect` on the **SpriteBatch** route: `SpriteBatch.Begin(..., effect)` runs the effect's shader per sprite. | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-27, verified on the real AMD RADV GPU (`:0`), full WebGPU suite 89/89.** `WebGPUSpriteBatchRenderer::SetCustomEffect` (previously a hard "not implemented" throw) now resolves the `Effect`'s `WebGPUEffectRenderer` via `GetEffectRendererPtr()` (a compiled Effect-Framework effect, which has no WGSL, is refused with a `System::NotSupportedException`) and stores it in `activeSpriteCustomEffect_`. `QueueSprite` captures that effect and its uniform block **by value** into the `SpriteCommand`; `IssueSprite` routes a sprite with a custom effect to the new `IssueSpriteWithCustomEffect`, which builds a pipeline from the effect's `vs_main`/`fs_main` modules with the fixed `SpriteVertex` layout (`position`/`uv`/`color` at `@location(0/1/2)`; no matrices — sprite vertices are already NDC), the sprite's blend/write-mask on slot 0 (extra MRT slots write nothing), a disabled Depth24PlusStencil8 state matching the stock sprite pipeline, and binds `{uniform block @0, the sprite's own sampler @1, the sprite's texture @2}` using the effect's reserved binding convention. Pipelines are cached on the effect keyed by the sprite pass state (a `"SPRITE01"` salt keeps them disjoint from the effect's 3D-route keys). **Test:** `WebGPU_SpriteBatch_ShaderEffect` (`webgpu_spritebatch_shadereffect_test.cpp`) — a tint effect renders a white texture red (A) then blue (B) under `uTint`, and a no-effect batch renders white (C, proving the effect is per-`Begin`). Discrimination confirmed: dropping `uTint` from the fragment fails A/B (1/3). |
| WEBGPU-143 | Per-slot MRT `ColorWriteChannels`/`1`/`2`/`3` for a custom-effect MRT draw. | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-27, verified on the real AMD RADV GPU (`:0`), full WebGPU suite 89/89.** `ApplyBlendState` previously kept only `colorWriteChannels[0]`; it now stores all four (`colorWriteChannels_[4]`, the framework already delivers them from `BlendState.ColorWriteChannels`/`1`/`2`/`3`). `QueueCustomEffectDraw` captures the four masks into the command, and `IssueCustomEffectDraw` applies slot *i*'s mask to `@location(i)` (XNA `ColorWriteChannels` is bit-identical to `WGPUColorWriteMask`) and folds them into the pipeline key. **Test:** `WebGPU_MRT` gained a 4-target Check C — the same custom effect writes all four slots, but with `ColorWriteChannels`=Red / `1`=Green / `2`=Blue / `3`=All each slot keeps only its enabled channel(s) over a black clear (slot 0→`(200,0,0)`, 1→`(0,40,0)`, 2→`(0,0,120)`, 3→full). Discrimination confirmed: forcing every slot to `writeMask=All` fails slots 0/1/2 (7/10). Stock/single-target draws are unchanged (they use `colorWriteMask_`, still slot 0). Note: XNA has one blend EQUATION for all targets, so only the write mask is per-slot; there is no per-slot independent blend to model. |
| WEBGPU-144 | GPU-native block-compressed textures (DXT1/3/5, BC7): upload BC blocks to a `WGPUTextureFormat_BC*` texture instead of CPU-decompressing to RGBA8 (partially supersedes `WEBGPU-111`). | ✅ (Phase 1 + 2) | **UPDATE 2026-08-27 (Phase 2, XNB-24): the content loaders now reach the native path too, full WebGPU suite 93/93.** `Texture2D::FromStream` (DDS) and the `.xnb` `Texture2DReader` keep DXT content compressed and upload the raw blocks (via the same compressed `SetData` path Phase 1 built) instead of CPU-decoding to Color. Gated on a new renderer-opt-in capability `IGraphicsRenderer::LoadsCompressedContentNativelyEXT()` (default false; **WebGPU-only** — the owner chose the WebGPU-only scope over reusing `IsCompressedTransferFormatEXT`, so Skia and every other renderer keep their deliberate decode-to-Color loaders, no Skia contract/test churn) AND the per-format `IsCompressedTransferFormatEXT`, so a loaded DXT texture keeps its `Dxt*` format exactly when the device can transfer it and decodes to Color otherwise (asserted both ways). The `FromStream(...,w,h,zoom)` resize overload always decodes (it resamples RGBA). **Test:** `WebGPU_CompressedContent` — FromStream DXT1 and `.xnb` DXT5 each keep native `Dxt*` storage + a 4-level chain, and the FromStream texture renders red (3/3). **Bug found + fixed along the way:** baking full mip chains exposed a compressed-mip upload defect — a sub-4x4 tail level (2x2/1x1) must be `wgpuQueueWriteTexture`'d with its **block-aligned** copy extent (`ceil(dim/4)*4`), which wgpu-native v29 validates against, not its logical texel size (Phase 1's single 4x4 level never reached it). New defaults test `RendererCapabilityDefaultsTest.LoadsCompressedContentNativelyDefaultsToFalse` pins the opt-in default. Pre-existing (NOT Phase 2): `Texture2DContentTypeReaderTest.NormalizedByte2RejectsAFourBytePerTexelLevel` fails on a WebGPU device (WebGPU has no `NormalizedByte2`; the test lacks the renderer-skip its `Reads` sibling has) — proven identical at HEAD before this change. --- Phase 1: **Done 2026-08-27, verified on the real AMD RADV GPU (`:0`), full WebGPU suite 90/90.** The WEBGPU-111 premise was partly stale: `ImageData` already carries `surfaceFormat`/`mipLevels` and `Texture2D` already has the compressed block-transfer contract (`IsCompressedTransferFormatEXT`, `SetCompressedDataBytes`/`GetCompressedDataBytes` → `UpdatePixelsLevel`/`GetData`), which only Skia (a CPU rasterizer) implemented. **This is the first renderer to upload BC blocks to the GPU natively.** WebGPU-local: the device requests `WGPUFeatureName_TextureCompressionBC` after `wgpuAdapterHasFeature` (stored in `bcSupported_`); `IsCompressedTransferFormatEXT`/`ClassifySurfaceFormatEXT` report the DXT/BC7 formats supported; `WebGPUTextureRenderer` reads `ImageData.surfaceFormat`, creates a `WGPUTextureFormat_BC{1,2,3,7}*` texture (Dxt1→BC1, Dxt3→BC2, Dxt5→BC3, Dxt5Srgb→BC3Srgb, Bc7→BC7, +Srgb; no RenderAttachment usage), uploads blocks with a block-stride copy layout, and keeps the per-mip blocks as the authoritative store its block `GetData` reads back. **Test:** `WebGPU_CompressedTexture` — a solid-red DXT1 (8-byte block) and a DXT5 (16-byte block) each sample RED (the GPU decoded the block natively, not the black clear) and `GetData` round-trips the exact block bytes (4/4). **Phase 2 DONE (see the Phase-2 update at the top of this row):** the XNB `Texture2DReader` and the DDS/`FromStream` path now reach the native path for WebGPU via `LoadsCompressedContentNativelyEXT()`. BC4/5/6 and non-BC compressed families (ETC/PVRTC/ATC) are not in CNA's `SurfaceFormat` enum. |
