| WEBGPU-90 | No-readback SpriteBatch scene: RGBA texture, source rectangle, tint, rotation and sampler variants. | ✅ | This is `cna_demo_2d --webgpu-2d-validation`, verified by `WEBGPU-126` and reconfirmed 2026-07-12 (screenshot review after the `WEBGPU-91` `Present()`/`EnsureFrameRendered()` refactor, still renders identically — crops/tint/rotation/flips/sampler variants all correct, no WebGPU error). |
| WEBGPU-91 | Implement reusable GPU readback with correct row alignment, asynchronous map/poll completion and timeout/error propagation. | ✅ | Verified 2026-07-12: `WebGPURenderer::ReadBackbuffer()` implemented via `wgpuCommandEncoderCopyTextureToBuffer` into a 256-byte-row-aligned `MapRead`\|`CopyDst` buffer, `wgpuBufferMapAsync` + the existing `WaitForCompletion` polling helper (`WEBGPU-127`'s pattern), BGRA↔RGBA swizzle matching Vulkan's own `ReadBackbuffer`. Required refactoring `Present()` into a shared `EnsureFrameRendered()` (acquire-if-needed + render-if-`framePending_`) so `GetBackBufferData()` can force an on-demand render of whatever `Clear()`/`SpriteBatch` work is queued so far in the *current* `Draw()` call, matching the Vulkan/Bgfx backends' own on-demand-submit readback semantics (confirmed necessary: `bgfx_graphicsdevice_clear_stencil_test.cpp` reads back the same-frame result of a `Clear()`+draw with no intervening `Present()`). New `WebGPU_Clear_Readback` CTest (`examples/webgpu_clear_readback_test.cpp`), verified genuinely discriminating via `git stash` (reverting the backend changes makes the test abort with the base class's `"ReadBackbuffer: not implemented"` exception instead of passing). Re-verified `WebGPU_Native2D_Smoke` and the `--webgpu-2d-validation` scene (screenshot-reviewed) still pass unchanged after the `Present()` refactor. |
| WEBGPU-92 | Pixel-asserted 2D tests: clear and white 1×1 sprite, then alpha/source-rectangle/sampler cases. | ✅ | `WebGPU_Clear_Readback` (`WEBGPU-91`/`132`) pixel-asserts: solid-colour `Clear()` (twice, proving no stale cache), a colour-tinted `SpriteBatch` quad inside/outside its destination rectangle, `alpha=0` leaving the destination unmodified, `sourceRectangle` cropping (2×1 red\|blue texture, right-texel-only crop samples blue), 50%-alpha landing strictly between black and full colour (`WEBGPU-132`), and — closing the last gap, 2026-07-12 — sampler address-mode (Wrap/Clamp/Mirror), Checks H/I/J: a `sourceRectangle` wider than the texture (4 texels against a 2×1 red\|blue texture) makes UV genuinely exceed 1.0 without any extra plumbing (`SpriteBatch` never clamps UV to the source rectangle itself), and `TextureFilter=Point` makes each sampled colour an exact single-texel lookup, no bilinear blur at the wrap/mirror seam. Verified genuinely discriminating by temporarily neutering `ToAddressMode()` to always return `ClampToEdge` — Wrap and Mirror checks then fail (both silently become Clamp) while the Clamp check itself still passes, exactly the expected failure signature — then reverted. 10/10 pixel checks total, all in the one `WebGPU_Clear_Readback` CTest. |

## Phase 68 — WebGPU backend: advanced and parity

| #   | Task                                                                                                          | Status | Notes                                                                 |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| WEBGPU-106 | `SetStringMarkerEXT()`: no-op (WebGPU has no debug labels in wgpu-native C API yet) | ✅ | Done 2026-08-26. The WebGPU renderer inherits `IGraphicsRenderer::SetStringMarkerEXT()`'s no-op default (it does NOT override it, unlike `VulkanRenderer`). This is correct, not a stub: `wgpu-native` v29's debug markers attach to a *live* command encoder / render pass (`wgpuRenderPassEncoderInsertDebugMarker` etc.), but this renderer records draws into a deferred command list and has no encoder open at the moment the device-level `SetStringMarkerEXT()` is called, so there is nothing to attach to. Deviation documented in `docs/webgpu-vs-vulkan-deviations.md` (`WEBGPU-118`). No game behaviour depends on it -- it affects only external GPU-capture tooling. |
| WEBGPU-107 | `DebugSimulateContextLoss()`: destroy and recreate device (wgpu-native supports `wgpuDeviceDestroy`) | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-06 by `WEBGPU-180`–`182`, which is what `WEBGPU-180`'s row said would happen** — this row's scope was superseded there, and it closes with them rather than separately. The "resource-recreation machinery neither this renderer nor the Vulkan renderer has" is now `IWebGPUDeviceResourceEXT` plus one registry on the renderer, covering `Texture2D`, `RenderTarget2D`, `VertexBuffer` and `IndexBuffer`; the kinds it does not cover are counted and make the simulated loss refuse by name. See `WEBGPU-182` for the measurements and the two negative controls. The original re-audit of 2026-08-26 is kept below because it is the reasoning the closure overturned. Re-audited 2026-08-26 (stayed ⬜ then). The WebGPU renderer inherits `IGraphicsRenderer::DebugSimulateContextLoss()`'s no-op default, exactly as `VulkanRenderer` does. The contract is explicitly OpenGL-context-loss-shaped (its own doc: `WEBGL_lose_context` on the web / destroy+recreate the SDL GL context on desktop, "forcing all GPU resources to be re-initialised") -- a GL-renderer debug feature. A faithful WebGPU version would `wgpuDeviceDestroy` and recreate the device, then re-initialise every live GPU resource (every `Texture2D`/vertex/index buffer/pipeline handle owned by an XNA object), resource-recreation machinery neither this renderer nor the Vulkan renderer has. Deferred as a large, GL-flavoured, low-game-impact debug feature; not force-implemented unsupervised. |
| WEBGPU-108 | `PresentationInterval` → vsync: `wgpuSurfaceConfigure.presentMode` (Fifo=VSync, Immediate=no VSync, Mailbox=adaptive) | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-27: the mapping is now a WGPU-free, unit-tested seam.** Extracted the `PresentInterval`→present-mode policy into `SelectPresentModeChoiceEXT()` (`WebGPUPresentMode.hpp`, a pure function taking the swap interval + which modes the surface advertises, returning a `PresentModeChoiceEXT`); the renderer only translates that choice onto a concrete `WGPUPresentMode` (or, for `FirstAvailable`, the surface's first advertised mode). New test `WebGPU_PresentModeMapping` (`webgpu_present_mode_test.cpp`), 9/9, pure CMake-registered unit test (no GPU/display): a zero swap interval prefers `Immediate`→`Mailbox`→`Fifo`; a non-zero interval uses `Fifo`; and **the unsupported-mode fallback is tested** — a v-sync request when the surface does NOT advertise `Fifo` (spec-guaranteed, but modelled defensively) falls back to the first advertised mode, and no advertised modes at all yields `Fifo`. The one thing still not asserted is the GPU-side frame-pacing EFFECT of the chosen mode, which is inherently unobservable through `GetData`/pixel readback (no timing harness); the mapping policy + fallback themselves are now proven. |
| WEBGPU-109 | `IsFullScreen` via `SDL_SetWindowFullscreen` — same as other backends | ✅ | Done 2026-08-26. Fullscreen is a `CNA::Platform` / `GameWindow` concern, not a renderer one: `Sdl3Window::SetFullscreenMode()` calls `SDL_SetWindowFullscreen`/`SDL_SetWindowFullscreenMode`, and the WebGPU renderer shares that exact SDL3 platform path with every other renderer (it only ever receives the surface it is handed). So `IsFullScreen` works for WebGPU identically to all backends with no renderer-local code, which is what "same as other backends" asks for. No WebGPU-specific test is meaningful (it would exercise the platform, not the renderer); documented in `docs/webgpu-vs-vulkan-deviations.md` (`WEBGPU-118`). |
| WEBGPU-110 | `BackBufferWidth/Height` changes: reconfigure swap chain via `wgpuSurfaceConfigure` | ✅ | Runtime-verified by `WEBGPU-127`'s resize sequence (800×600→960×540→800×600) and `WEBGPU-130`'s independent second application, both with clean reconfiguration and no WebGPU validation error. |
| WEBGPU-111 | DXT1/DXT3/DXT5 compressed texture upload: `WGPUTextureFormat_BC1RGBAUnorm` etc. | ✅ (superseded by WEBGPU-144) | **UPDATE 2026-08-27: the native-upload part is DONE as `WEBGPU-144` — see that row.** Two premises in the 2026-07-18 note below are now stale: (a) `ImageData` DOES carry a `surfaceFormat`/`mipLevels` field (added by later Task 924 / SKIA-140+ work), and (b) `Texture2D` DOES have the compressed block-transfer contract (`IsCompressedTransferFormatEXT`/block SetData/GetData), which Skia already used. WebGPU is now the first renderer to upload BC blocks to the GPU natively (via the direct `Texture2D(...,Dxt*)`+`SetData` API). What the 2026-07-18 note correctly identified as still cross-cutting — the XNB/DDS content loaders force-decoding DXT to Color — was closed on 2026-08-27 as WEBGPU-144 Phase 2 (a new `LoadsCompressedContentNativelyEXT()` opt-in, WebGPU-only; see that row). Original 2026-07-18 note follows. --- 2026-07-18 (mips/cube/compressed-textures cluster): investigated, deliberately NOT implemented -- deferred as a genuinely larger, cross-cutting change than backend-local scope allows, not a time-management cut. Two concrete findings: (1) **the real adapter on this development machine DOES support it** -- a standalone diagnostic against this project's pinned wgpu-native v29.0.1.1 (`wgpuAdapterHasFeature(adapter, WGPUFeatureName_TextureCompressionBC)`, the exact enum/function confirmed against the pinned `webgpu.h`) returned `TRUE` (AMD Radeon 780M/RADV Vulkan backend), and requesting a device with that feature in `requiredFeatures` succeeded too (`wgpuDeviceHasFeature()` also `TRUE`) -- so this is NOT a dead end on real hardware, unlike a machine without BC support would be. (2) **No CNA backend -- not just WebGPU -- has ever done real native GPU-side block-compressed texture upload at all.** `Texture2D.cpp`'s own DXT1/3/5 handling (`Texture2D::FromStream()`) always fully CPU-decompresses DXT-compressed source bytes to plain RGBA8 via `DxtUtil::DecompressDxt1()` before ever reaching a backend (see `examples/dxt1_texture_test.cpp`/`cna_test_dxt1_texture`, EasyGL-only) -- Vulkan, D3D9/11/12, Bgfx, SDL_GPU all only ever see RGBA8 `ImageData`, never a real `VK_FORMAT_BC1_...`/`DXGI_FORMAT_BC1_...`-style compressed GPU texture. The common `CNA::Internal::Graphics::ImageData` struct (`include/CNA/Internal/Graphics/ImageData.hpp`) has NO field at all for a surface format or a compressed-bytes flag -- it is hardcoded `std::vector<uint8_t> pixels; // RGBA8`. Closing this row genuinely as originally worded (a real compressed upload reachable from actual game code, not a synthetic direct-to-`IGraphicsBackend` test bypassing the whole XNA layer) needs a real, cross-backend common-interface change to `ImageData` (or a parallel `CreateCompressedTexture()`-style entry point) PLUS a `Texture2D.cpp` change to stop always-decompressing DXT source data -- both explicitly out of this task's backend-local, `Microsoft::Xna::Framework`-excluded scope, and neither verified against any other backend as this task's own constraints require before a common-interface change. A WebGPU-only implementation reachable only via a hand-crafted direct `IGraphicsBackend::CreateTexture()` call with raw BC1 bytes stuffed into the RGBA8-typed `pixels` field (bypassing `ImageData`'s own type contract) was considered and rejected as exactly the kind of "silently-wrong/overreaching" shortcut this project's culture avoids -- it would not be reachable by any real game and would misrepresent `ImageData`'s own documented contract. Left open for a dedicated follow-up task scoped to include the `ImageData`/`Texture2D.cpp` design work, not a backend-only fix. |
| WEBGPU-112 | Texture3D: `WGPUTextureDimension_3D` + layered upload | ✅ | Same `WebGPUTexture3DBackend` as `WEBGPU-57` (a single class covers both rows) — see that row for the full detail. `examples/webgpu_texture3d_test.cpp` (`WebGPU_Texture3D` CTest), 3/3. |
| WEBGPU-113 | TextureCube: `WGPUTexture` arrayLayerCount=6 + `WGPUTextureViewDimension_Cube` | ✅ | Same `WebGPUTextureCubeBackend` as `WEBGPU-56` (a single class covers both rows) — real `WGPUTexture` (`depthOrArrayLayers=6`) + a `WGPUTextureViewDimension_Cube` view, `SetData()` per face/level. Verified indirectly via `WebGPU_EnvMap3D` (real per-face colours correctly sampled). 2026-07-18 (follow-up task): real `GetData()` added — same staged MAP_READ-buffer/aligned-row/async-map technique as `WEBGPU-51`'s `Texture2D::GetData()`, with `origin.z=face` selecting the array layer; required adding `WGPUTextureUsage_CopySrc` to the texture descriptor (missing before this). `TextureCube::GetData()` at the XNA layer has no CPU-side shadow at all (unlike `Texture2D`) and always calls straight through to `ITextureCubeBackend`, so this is a genuine end-to-end XNA-layer test, not a backend-only one. `examples/webgpu_texturecube_getdata_test.cpp` (`WebGPU_TextureCube_GetData` CTest), 8/8: each of the 6 faces round-trips its own distinct colour with no cross-face collision, a non-origin sub-rectangle read, and a mip level 1 round trip distinct from level 0 (this project's first real per-level SetData/GetData exercise on a `TextureCube`, closing the "mip regeneration untested" gap left by `WEBGPU-56`). 2026-07-18 (follow-up task, mips/cube/compressed-textures cluster): `RenderTargetCube` (`WEBGPU-114`, a separate sibling class) and automatic mip-chain regeneration from level 0 (`WEBGPU-52`) both now real too. Now ✅. |
| WEBGPU-114 | RenderTargetCube: `WGPUTexture` cube + per-face `WGPUTextureView` as render attachment | ✅ | **DONE 2026-08-27: the two remaining gaps are now closed — per-face MSAA (Phase 1) and `mipMap=true` mip-chain regeneration (Phase 2).** Per-face MSAA: when the renderer's global `sampleCount_` > 1 each face renders into its own multisampled colour attachment and resolves into that face's single-sample layer (SIX separate single-layer MSAA textures, because WebGPU forbids a multisampled ARRAY texture, unlike Vulkan's 6-layer image; the shared depth attachment is allocated at the same sample count), `GetMultiSampleCount()` reports the applied count mirroring `RenderTarget2D`. mipMap: the colour texture carries a full chain and each face is regenerated from its resolved level 0 after that face's pass (`GenerateMipsForLayer`, the WEBGPU-52 downsample cascade, parameterized by colour format so a `surfaceFormat_`/BGRA cube target works; MSAA and mipMap compose — resolve writes level 0, then the cascade downsamples). `GetData` accepts levels 0..LevelCount-1. Verified by `WebGPU_RenderTargetCube` (18/18: Check E1/E2/E3 mip chain + genuine level-1 downsample, Check F1/F2/F3/F4 4x per-face resolve with no cross-face leak, blended edge pixels, and MSAA+mipMap combined) plus the cross-renderer `rendertargetcube_usage`/`rendertargetcube_getdata_contract` contracts (WEBGPU branch flipped to `mipMapCubeTargets` true / `mipLevel` Exact / `MipTargets::Real`). The previously-listed third gap ("SpriteBatch into an off-screen target") is RESOLVED: `QueueSprite` now derives clip space from the CURRENTLY-BOUND target (REMED-GFX-019), fixed by `WebGPU_SpriteBatch_RenderTarget`, and the letterbox-viewport edge was closed by `WEBGPU-141`(A) — the "computes from the backbuffer's logical dimensions" note below is historical. 2026-07-18 (mips/cube/compressed-textures cluster): real render-into-a-cube-face support -- `WebGPURenderTargetCubeBackend` (`IRenderTargetCubeBackend`), `WebGPURenderer::CreateRenderTargetCube()` (previously `IGraphicsBackend`'s own nullptr-returning default). One shared 6-array-layer `WGPUTexture` (mirrors `WebGPUTextureCubeBackend`'s own layout) + one shared `size`x`size` `Depth24PlusStencil8` texture reused across all 6 faces (only one face is ever bound at a time, mirroring `VulkanRenderTargetCubeBackend`'s identical shared-depth-image choice) + one `WGPUTextureViewDimension_2D` view per face (`faceViews_[face]`, the render-pass colour attachment) + one `WGPUTextureViewDimension_Cube` view spanning all 6 layers (`cubeView_`, for sampling back). Extended this backend's pre-existing `RenderTarget2D` "eager-flush-on-target-switch" design (`WEBGPU-53`/`54`) to a 3rd target kind: `WebGPURenderer::FlushCurrentRenderTarget()` (a new shared entry point factored out of the old `SetRenderTarget2D`-only inline logic) + `currentRenderTargetCubeFace_`/`currentRenderTargetCubeFaceIndex_` let a cube face act as its own distinct "target identity" for that flush/switch logic, so switching directly between two DIFFERENT faces (not just face↔backbuffer) still correctly flushes the outgoing face's own render pass first -- verified explicitly (`WebGPU_RenderTargetCube` Check A). Also added a new small `IWebGPUCubeSamplable` interface (the cube-map sibling of the pre-existing `IWebGPUSamplable`) so `EnvironmentMapEffect.EnvironmentMap` correctly resolves EITHER a `WebGPUTextureCubeBackend` OR a `WebGPURenderTargetCubeBackend` via a safe `dynamic_cast` (`ResolveCubeSamplable()`) -- before this, `GpuDrawParams::envMap` was hardcoded to the concrete `WebGPUTextureCubeBackend` type only, so a `RenderTargetCube` bound as an env map would have silently fallen back to the 1x1 white cube. `examples/webgpu_rendertargetcube_test.cpp` (`WebGPU_RenderTargetCube` CTest), 12/12: 6 faces bound directly face-to-face (no intervening backbuffer switch) each read back their own distinct `Clear()` colour with no cross-face aliasing; a real `BasicEffect`/`VertexPositionColor` 3D draw into one face; an `EnvironmentMapEffect` sampling round trip proving the new `IWebGPUCubeSamplable` wiring; and the critical "an intervening cube-face-targeted `Clear()` must not leak into the backbuffer's own render pass" architecture check (mirrors `WEBGPU-53`/`54`'s own Check E). (Historical: the 2026-07-18 landing of this row deliberately deferred mip regeneration and per-face MSAA as scope cuts, which is why Checks E/F once asserted `mipMap=true` throwing and `MultiSampleCount` reporting 0 — both were closed 2026-08-27 as described above and those checks were rewritten to their real behaviour.) The 2026-07-18 note about `QueueSprite()` computing clip space from the backbuffer's logical dimensions is HISTORICAL — that was fixed by REMED-GFX-019/`WEBGPU-141`(A) (see the top of this row). |
| WEBGPU-115 | `FillMode::WireFrame`: report as unsupported and refuse the draw (no polygon mode in wgpu-native) | ✅ | **DONE 2026-08-04 — post-audit checkpoint blocker RESOLVED.** The row's original scope ("document as unsupported") was no longer sufficient once the path was measured: `SupportsCapability(WireFrame)` returned **`true`** (inherited `IGraphicsBackend` default -- the backend never overrode it), `ApplyRasterizerState` accepted with no throw/warning/log, `command.wireframe` was captured at **10** queue sites (the exit reconciliation's "11" was one high; the eleventh `wireframe` reference is `DrawInstancedPrimitivesEx`'s own capture, already counted), folded into `Make3DPipelineKey`, and a **distinct `WGPURenderPipeline` was created and natively submitted** -- returning a frame **byte-identical to Solid** (`total=18176 interior=1089/1089 AB=298 BC=310 CA=329` for both). Prose in `docs/webgpu-renderer.md` is unreachable from the public API and was contradicted by the query, so nothing accepted the deviation. **Fix, backend-local:** (1) `WebGPURenderer::SupportsCapability` now answers `false` for `WireFrame`, every other capability unchanged; (2) `RequireSupportedFillModeEXT(primitive, route)` throws `System::NotSupportedException` from the top of the five public 3D draw entry points -- `DrawColoredPrimitives`, `DrawIndexedColoredPrimitives`, `DrawPrimitivesEx`, `DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx`, `DrawInstancedPrimitivesEx` -- the narrowest boundary all eleven `Queue*Draw()` families pass through. `ApplyRasterizerState` still STORES the fill mode and still succeeds (a state setter cannot know whether a draw follows -- `REMED-GFX-DECL-GUARD`'s precedent). **Only polygon topologies are refused:** a line or point list has no interior for a fill mode to select, and both fill modes were measured byte-identical there, so refusing them would delete a correct draw (found by `PointListPrimitiveTest.PointListIsNotAffectedByTriangleCulling`). **Measured after:** queued commands +0, pipeline caches +0, native draws 0, submits attributable to the draw 0, target unchanged (`total=0`), uncaptured native errors 0, no device loss; Solid recovery = exactly 1 draw, 1 submit, 0 new pipelines. The `wireframe` bit stays in `Make3DPipelineKey` and is now **unreachable** -- no accepted path computes a key with it set. Tests: `WebGpuWireFrameContract.*` (8 tests: capability, pre-queue cardinality, 12-route matrix, alternation, lifetime, native validation scopes, topology boundary, exception type), `GraphicsDeviceCapabilityTest.WireFrameIsRefusedDeterministicallyOnThisBackend` (replaces `WireFrameSilentlyRendersSolidGeometryOnThisBackend`, which was written to fail the day this landed), `examples/webgpu_graphicsstate_test.cpp` Check G (backbuffer route, 14/14). WebGPU principal suite 5909 ran / 5876 passed / 28 skipped / 5 failed -- exactly the recorded baseline residuals. ASan+UBSan clean. |
| WEBGPU-116 | WebGPU vertex format helper: `WGPUVertexFormat WebGPUVertexFormatFromVEF(VertexElementFormat)` (mirror Task 248) | ✅ | Done 2026-08-26. Added `WebGPUVertexFormatFromVEF()` in `WebGPURenderer.cpp` (anon namespace, beside `ToWGPUCullMode`/`ToWGPUBlendFactor`), a full 12-value switch over `VertexElementFormat` mirroring `VulkanRenderer`'s `ToVkVertexFormat` -- with the one intentional deviation that `Color`→`Unorm8x4` (WebGPU has no B8G8R8A8 vertex format; this renderer stores colour RGBA and the shader reads it straight, so no swizzle-format is needed, unlike Vulkan's `VK_FORMAT_B8G8R8A8_UNORM`). Made it **live and verified, not dead code**: every one of the ~55 hardcoded `attributes[i].format = WGPUVertexFormat_*` literals across all 10+ pipeline builders now routes through it (`Vector2/3/4`, `Color`, `Byte4` are the 5 formats the packed layouts actually use). Verified on the real RADV GPU (`:0`): the full WebGPU CTest set is unchanged at 81/82 (only the pre-existing `WebGPU_Clear_Readback`/`WEBGPU-141` still fails) -- a wrong ordinal→format mapping would garble the matching 3D/sprite/skinned/instanced draw, so the suite is the discriminating test. The other 7 formats (`Single`, `Short2/4`, `NormalizedShort2/4`, `HalfVector2/4`) complete the enum for parity but have no live pipeline consumer yet. |

| WEBGPU-123 | Cross-backend pixel comparison: same scene rendered on another backend and WebGPU — assert pixel-level parity | ✅ | 2026-08-26. Verified across FOUR backends (`cna_diag_{webgpu,software,easygl,vulkan}`, all the same renderer-agnostic scene): **VULKAN (native, real AMD Radeon 780M / RADV, run on the real `:0` display with the owner's explicit go-ahead) vs WEBGPU (browser) = max diff 0 -- BYTE-IDENTICAL** (both bottom out on the same AMD Vulkan: Chrome's WebGPU is Dawn-on-Vulkan). WEBGPU vs SOFTWARE and VULKAN vs SOFTWARE are each max 1 / mean 0.139 / 0 outliers (rounding vs the CPU rasterizer). The only cross-backend divergence is EASYGL (OPENGL33, headless Xvfb+llvmpipe), which differs from all three others at 57 triangle-EDGE coverage pixels -- a GL fill-rule / pixel-centre convention at the boundary, not a WebGPU defect. Only the Bgfx leg the task also names is un-run (a 5th backend; the parity picture is settled). WEBGPU is now wired into the shared cross-renderer diagnostic harness: `cna_diag_webgpu` builds the renderer-agnostic `cross_renderer_diagnostic_scene` (one unlit vertex-colour triangle -> 64x64 RGBA8) for WEBGPU, native and Emscripten, exactly as `cna_diag_easygl`/`cna_diag_software` do. **Verified parity WEBGPU (browser) vs SOFTWARE (native CPU rasterizer):** `scripts/run-webgpu-parity-test.sh` runs the Emscripten `cna_diag_webgpu` in headless Chrome, extracts its MEMFS dump, and diffs it against `cna_diag_software`'s dump with `cna_diag_compare` -- **max per-channel diff = 1, mean = 0.139** across the whole frame (both dumps have the identical 1682 non-black pixels), well within the tool's default tolerance of 40. **PASS** (0 of 4096 pixels over tolerance). **EASYGL leg added** the same day: `cna_diag_easygl` (native `OPENGL33`, run headless under Xvfb + Mesa `llvmpipe` software GL, no real window) produced a third dump. Full pairwise result -- WEBGPU vs SOFTWARE `max=1 mean=0.139 (0 outliers)`; EASYGL vs SOFTWARE `max=253 mean=1.575 (57)`; WEBGPU vs EASYGL `max=253 mean=1.538 (57)`. **Finding:** WebGPU matches the CPU reference essentially exactly; the outlier is EASYGL, which differs from BOTH others at the SAME 57 pixels -- all verified to be triangle-EDGE coverage pixels (SOFTWARE/WebGPU include them, the GL rasterizer excludes them). That is a GL fill-rule / pixel-centre convention difference, **not a WebGPU defect** -- WebGPU is on the reference-matching side. The VULKAN leg (native `cna_diag_vulkan` on the real GPU) then closed it: byte-identical to the browser WebGPU dump, as noted above. Bgfx is the only named backend not run; the parity conclusion is settled without it. |

| WEBGPU-141 | `WebGPU_Clear_Readback` fails 3 of 10 checks on the AMD RADV GPU: a 50%-alpha sprite over the backbuffer reads back full-opacity green (`R=0 G=255 B=0`), and `AddressMode=Wrap`/`Mirror` at fractional `u≈1.14`/`1.77` sample the wrong texel (Clamp at the same `u` is correct). | ✅ | **RESOLVED 2026-08-27 -- both parts fixed, `WebGPU_Clear_Readback` is 10/10 and the full WebGPU suite 86/86 on the real RADV GPU.** **Root cause found by bisect on the real RADV GPU (`:0`), 2026-08-27 -- the earlier "adapter sensitivity / NOT a regression / out of scope" note was wrong.** It is TWO independent regressions, both from dated WebGPU-only commits, found by binary-searching clean per-commit builds between the last-green baseline (`f442a4d0e`, WEBGPU-92, 2026-07-12, 10/10) and HEAD. NOTE: the branch pulled ~2732 mainline commits into this window (BLEND2D/svg-dom/openvg/fna3d lanes), not the ~10 first estimated. **(A) Wrap/Mirror (checks H/J) -- GENUINE renderer regression. First-bad `e2e8dee99` "fix(Task REMED-GFX-072): WebGPU SpriteBatch viewport-relative clip space" (2026-07-22):** its parent `eebb222d1` is 10/10, `e2e8dee99` is 8/10 (H/J fail). WebGPU-local (touches only `WebGPUGraphicsBackend`). It added a `customViewport`/viewport-relative NDC path; that path's detection (`viewportW_ != targetWidth`, `WebGPURenderer.cpp` ~5592) MISFIRES for the default full-backbuffer on a logical-vs-physical viewport size mismatch (cf. the CNA viewport logical/physical seam), so the sprite is baked viewport-local + given a per-draw rasterizer viewport, shifting its screen geometry and therefore the interpolated `u` at the probe pixel -- `u` no longer reaches `[0,2)`, so Wrap/Mirror sample the same in-range (blue) texel as Clamp. The test's expectation (Wrap→red) is FNA-correct, so the renderer was wrong. **FIXED 2026-08-27:** the `customViewport` detection now requires the viewport to be a CONTAINED sub-region of the target (`viewportX_+viewportW_ <= targetWidth`, etc.), so the spurious oversized default (107 > 64) no longer takes the viewport-local path; the genuine sub-Viewport case (`WebGPU_SpriteBatch_CustomViewport`, still 13/13) is preserved, and `WebGPU_Clear_Readback` Wrap/Mirror now pass (9/10, full suite 85/86 -- the last failure is the alpha test issue (B)). (`e2e8dee99`'s own message claimed "WebGPU_Clear_Readback fails identically with this change stashed" -- but that was measured on **Xvfb `:99`, which has no WebGPU adapter** so the test always fails there; on the real GPU the parent passes 10/10 and this commit is the regression -- a GPU-less environment masked a real one.) **(B) Alpha (check G) -- NOT a renderer bug; the TEST is wrong. First-bad `f14c764d8` "fix(Task REMED-GFX-102): key WebGPU sprite pipelines by blend state" (2026-07-26):** parent `7038bd9b8` is 8/10 with G passing, `f14c764d8` is 7/10. It changed the sprite blend from HARDCODED straight (`SrcAlpha`/`OneMinusSrcAlpha`, WEBGPU-132's value) to DEVICE-DRIVEN (honouring the `BlendState` `SpriteBatch.Begin()` sets). **That is FNA-correct:** FNA `BlendState.AlphaBlend` is PREMULTIPLIED (`One`/`InverseSourceAlpha`, `FNA/src/Graphics/States/BlendState.cs:172`), `SpriteBatch.Begin()` defaults to it, and FNA's sprite shader outputs straight colour -- so a straight-alpha texture under default AlphaBlend gives FULL brightness in FNA, not partway. EasyGL made the identical fix (Task 956: honour the device BlendState instead of hardcoding -- `EasyGLRenderer.cpp:3579`), so the whole renderer family + FNA agree on full green here. WEBGPU-132's hardcoded-straight was the WebGPU-only DEVIATION and Check G locked it in; `REMED-GFX-102` merely aligned WebGPU with FNA/EasyGL. **So the renderer is now correct; Check G asserts non-FNA behaviour** (it expects `NonPremultiplied` attenuation while using AlphaBlend). Fix: correct the TEST -- Check G should draw with `BlendState::NonPremultiplied` (or a premultiplied texture) so alpha genuinely attenuates against the correct device-driven blend -- NOT the renderer, and this is not tolerance-widening. (WEBGPU-132's premise holds for the sampler checks, which caught the real H/J regression, but its alpha check tested a WebGPU-only deviation from FNA.) **FIXED 2026-08-27:** Check G now draws with `BlendState::NonPremultiplied` and reads back `R=0 G=128 B=0` (genuine attenuation against the correct device-driven blend). **Blast radius: WebGPU-LOCAL for both** -- each first-bad commit touches only `WebGPUGraphicsBackend`, so no `SpriteBatch.cpp`/shared change is involved; EasyGL/SOFTWARE are unaffected (and by the FNA/Task-956 reasoning would themselves give full green for check G). Both parts fixed 2026-08-27: (A) the `customViewport`-containment renderer fix, (B) the Check-G `NonPremultiplied` test fix. |
